A Kunen-Like Model with a Critical Failure of the Continuum Hypothesis
Omer Ben Neria, Eyal Kaplan

Abstract
We construct a model of the form L ⁡ [ A , U ] L[A,U] that exhibits the simplest structural behavior of σ \sigma -complete ultrafilters in a model of set theory with a single measurable cardinal κ \kappa , yet satisfies 2 κ = κ + ⁣ + 2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{++} . This result establishes a limitation on the extent to which structural properties of ultrafilters can determine the cardinal arithmetic at large cardinals, and answers a question posed by Goldberg concerning the failure of the Continuum Hypothesis at a measurable cardinal in a model of the Ultrapower Axiom. The construction introduces several methods in extensions of embeddings theory and fine-structure-based forcing, designed to control the behavior of non-normal ultrafilters in generic extensions.



1
Introduction

A hallmark of large cardinal properties in set theory is their capacity to determine fundamental aspects of the set-theoretic universe.
For example:

1.

In cardinal arithmetic, Solovay (
[
27
]
) proved that the Singular Cardinal Hypothesis (SCH) holds above a strongly compact cardinal
\kappa
.

2.

In the study of compactness in set theory, various large cardinal principles have been shown to secure (or are equivalent to) various compactness principles. In particular, incompactness combinatorial principles such as squares, nonreflecting stationary sets, or Aronszajn trees have been shown to fail around or above large cardinals (e.g.,
[
14
]
,
[
16
]
,
[
8
]
,
[
7
]
provide a comprehensive account to the subject).

The effect of large cardinal hypotheses on other key properties is amplified under further structural assumption regarding the behavior of large cardinal ultrafilters. These are typically given in either terms of properties of ultrafilters,
or in terms of the connections between them.
An example of the first is the normality assumption that separates ultrafilters witnessing supercompactness from those witnessing strong compactness.
An example of the second is the linearity of the Mitchell order

\triangleleft

on
\sigma
-complete ultrafilters.

A remarkable demonstration of the extent structural assumptions about ultrafilters shed light on other key properties is Goldberg’s work about the Ultrapower Axiom
[
13
]
. The Ultrapower Axiom (UA) states that for every two
\sigma
-complete ultrafilters
U,W
with associated ultrapower maps
j_{U}:V\to M_{U}
,
j_{W}:V\to M_{W}
, there are
\sigma
-complete ultrafilters
W^{\prime}\in M_{U}
and
U^{\prime}\in M_{W}
such that the internal ultrapowers
\Ult(M_{U},W^{\prime})
of
M_{U}
and
\Ult(M_{W},U^{\prime})
of
M_{W}
are isomorphic to the same transitive class
P
, and the associated ultrapower maps
k_{U^{\prime}}:M_{W}\to P
and
k_{W^{\prime}}:M_{U}\to P
generate a commutative system with
j_{W},j_{U}
respectively, i.e.,
k_{U^{\prime}}\circ j_{W}=k_{W^{\prime}}\circ j_{U}.

It is shown in
[
13
]
that under the Ultrapower Axiom every strongly compact cardinal is either supercompact or a measurable limit of supercompacts, and that the Generalized Continuum hypothesis (GCH) holds at and above a supercompact cardinal
\kappa
.
1
1

1

I.e.,
2^{\lambda}=\lambda^{+}
for every cardinal
\lambda\geq\kappa
.

The prospects of these result are promising in light of a recent Theorem of Woodin, showing that for every
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}
such that
L(A,\mathbb{R})\models AD^{+}
,
HOD^{L(A,\mathbb{R})}\models UA
. The last implies that the axiom
V=\text{Ultimate}-L
implies
UA
.

These results involving the Ultrapower Axiom can be viewed as extensions of fundamental results in inner model theory. Indeed, all known fine-structural based extender models
L[\vec{E}]
satisfy the UA as well as many other strong properties such as the GCH. For example, by results of Kunen and Silver on the structure of a model of the form
L[U]
, where
U
is normal
2
2

2

An ultrafilter
U
on
\kappa
is normal if for every
A\in U
and
f\colon\kappa\to\kappa
such that for every
\alpha\in A
,
f(\alpha)<\alpha
, there exists
B\in U
,
B\subseteq A
and
\gamma<\kappa
such that for every
\alpha\in B
,
f(\alpha)=\gamma
.

\kappa
-complete ultrafilter on a measurable cardinal
\kappa
, nowadays known as a “
Kunen model
”,
L[U]
satisfies

•

The General Continuum Hypothesis (
[
26
]
).

•

Every
\sigma
-complete ultrafilter
W\in L[U]
is isomorphic to a finite power
U^{n}
of
U
(
[
18
]
). In particular, UA holds.

The results from UA + a supercompact cardinal
\kappa
, and the broader indications given by inner model theory, suggest that the ability to derive instances of GCH from structural properties of ultrafilters can go beyond UA and a supercompact cardinal, and can possibly extend to “local” versions in which structural assumptions such as UA around a measurable or a strong cardinal
\kappa
could yield local instances of GCH around
\kappa
.

The main result of this paper provides a limitation to this perspective by constructing a “Kunen-like” model
L[A,U]
that satisfies the simplest possible (nontrivial) behavior of
\sigma
-complete ultrafilter, yet fails to satisfy GCH at its unique measurable cardinal
\kappa
.

Theorem 1.1
.

There exists a “Kunen-Like” model of set theory, of the form
L[A,U]
satisfying

1.

There is a unique measurable cardinal
\kappa
and a unique
\kappa
-complete normal ultrafilter
U

2.

Every
\sigma
-complete ultrafilter is isomorphic to a finite power
U^{n}
of
U

3.

2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{++}

The Large cardinal consistency assumption used in our construction is of a cardinal
\kappa
that is
(\kappa+2)
-strong.

Theorem
1.1
also provides the first instance of a forcing over a model of UA, which adds an unbounded subset to a measurable cardinal
\kappa
, and preserves its measurablity and UA.

In order to prove the main result, we make several contributions to both the study of the possible behavior of
\sigma
-complete ultrafilters and especially to non-normal ultrafilters, and to the study of forcing iteration theory.
Concerning the former, the possible behavior of normal ultrafilters goes back to problems from the 1970s regarding the possible number of normal ultrafilters on a measurable cardinal
\kappa
, and the possible structure of the Mitchell order on them.
Questions about the number of normal measures and their ordering are closely related to some of the earliest developments in forcing theory and inner model theory, relating to large cardinals. For example, in forcing theory, the work of Kunen and Paris
[
17
]
on elementary embeddings in forcing extensions, constructs a model with the maximal possible number of normal ultrafilters on a single measurable cardinal. In inner model theory, the work of Mitchell
[
20
,
21
]
on coherent sequences of normal ultrafilters
\vec{U}
and their associated canonical inner models
L[\vec{U}]
, constructs models with any number
\lambda\in[1,\kappa^{++}]
of normal ultrafilters on a measurable cardinal
\kappa
.
Over the years new ideas and methods in forcing theory and inner model theory have been developed to tackle these questions.
Results by Baldwin
[
2
]
, Apter–Cummings–Hamkins
[
1
]
, Leaning
[
19
]
, and Friedman-Magidor
[
10
]
regarding the possible number of normal ultrafilters, and by Cummings
[
5
]
,
[
6
]
, Witzany
[
29
]
, and the first author
[
4
]
,
[
3
]
, on the possible behavior of the Mitchell order.

The work of Friedman and Magidor
[
10
]
settled the question regarding possible number normal ultrafilters by introducing a forcing method that can construct models with any number
\lambda\in[1,\kappa^{++}]
of normal ultrafilters, from the minimal large cardinal assumption. In addition
[
10
]
constructs a model with a single measurable cardinal
\kappa
with a single normal ultrafilter
U
, while
2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{++}
. The Friedman-Magidor model is the starting point to Theorem
1.1
, as it satisfies the portion of the “Kunen-like” model regarding normal ultrafilters. Nevertheless, it does not satisfy that every
\sigma
-complete ultrafilter on
\kappa
is isomorphic to a finite power
U^{n}
of the unique normal ultrafilter
U
.
The missing part of the relevant theory is to control the behavior of non-normal ultrafilters on a measurable cardinals. Section
2
provides a blueprint for addressing the missing part. It introduces abstract properties, referred to as ”blueprints”, of forcing notions and iterated forcings (i.e., see Definitions
2.12
,
2.22
) and shows that generic extensions of suitable canonical inner models
V=L[\bar{E}]
by a poset
\mathbb{P}
satisfying the relevant blueprint are “Kunen-Like” (see Theorem
2.2
).

The rest of the paper is devoted to constructing a poset
\mathbb{P}
which satisfies the ”Kunen-Like” blueprint assumptions.
The first main ingredient of the poset construction is the iteration theory of generalized Miller forcings, introduced by Friedman and Zdomskyy
[
12
]
. A variation of their forcing that will be used is described in Section
4.1
.
The second main ingredient is the use of the fine-structure theory of the ground model
V=L[\bar{E}]
for the construction of
\mathbb{P}
. The use of fine-structure is key in successfully fulfilling one of the main blueprint assumptions of
\mathbb{P}
, which requires it to incorporate a nonstationary support iteration of coding posets. In addition, each coding poset
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
destroys the stationarity of various non-reflecting stationary sets
S_{\alpha}
that consist of low-cofinality ordinals (i.e., cofinality
\omega_{1}
). It is well-known that each single instance
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
of such a poset is sufficiently distributive, and, therefore, does not collapse cardinals. The problem resides in the iteration of such posets, which in general can collapse cardinals.
Our solution builds on the fine-structure theory of
L[\bar{E}]
, and specifically on the construction methods of squares and morasses. This approach was motivated by a recent work of Foreman, Magidor, and Zeman, that is outlined in
[
9
]
and expected to appear in details in a sequel work. The iteration constructed in
[
9
]
is based on an Easton support iteration of coding posets. Our construction extends this method to large (nonstationary) support iterations and in establishing a strong form of distributivity given in terms of a certain fusion property (definition
2.4
(2)). The fine-structure background is given in Section
3
, and its application to the main poset construction is given in Section
4
.

Our notations for ultrafilters, ultrapowers, fine-structure, and forcing, are mostly standard. The most notable exception is our use of the Jerusalem forcing convention, according to which a condition
p
extends (i.e., is more informative than) a condition
q
is denoted by
p\geq q
, and the weakest condition in a poset
\mathbb{P}
is denoted by
0_{\mathbb{P}}
.

2
Blueprints for controlling
\kappa
-complete Ultrafilters in Generic Extensions

The goal of this section is to provide two blueprints for posets
\mathbb{P}
that violate GCH at a measurable cardinal
\kappa
while maintaining certain control over the measures on
\kappa
(i.e.,
\kappa
-complete ultrafilters on
\kappa
in the generic extension).

The first blueprint will be an abstraction of the Friedman Magidor construction that yields a generic extension in which
\kappa
is the only measurable cardinal,
2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{++}
, and there is a single normal measure
U
on
\kappa
. We shall refer to it as the Friedman-Magidor (FM) blueprint.

The second blueprint will extend the FM-blueprint by adding assumptions to
\mathbb{P}
which guarantee that every measure on
\kappa
in the generic extension is a finite power
U^{n}
of
U
, therefore yielding a “Kunen-like” model. We shall refer to the second blueprint as the “Kunen-like” blueprint.

Notation 2.1
.

1.

We force with the Jerusalem convention for forcing by which a condition
q^{\prime}
is stronger (more informative) than
q
is denoted by
q^{\prime}\geq q
.

2.

For a poset
\mathbb{Q}
,
the weakest condition of
\mathbb{Q}
is denoted by
0_{\mathbb{Q}}
, and the
canonical
\mathbb{Q}
-name for the generic filter is denoted by
\dot{G}_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

3.

For a transitive inner model
N
of
V
, and an extender/measure
E\in N
such that the ultrapower of
N
by
E
is well-founded, we denote the ultrapower embedding by
j_{E}:V\to N_{E}\cong\Ult(N,E)
, where
N_{E}
is the transitive collapse of the ultrapower model.

4.

A full-supported iterated forcing of length
\kappa
is a forcing
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\kappa\rangle
, where for each
\alpha<\kappa
,
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is a
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-name for a forcing notion in
V^{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}}
. For every
\alpha\leq\kappa
, a condition
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
is a function with domain
\alpha
, such that for every
\beta<\alpha
,
p\upharpoonright\beta\in\mathbb{P}_{\beta}
and
p\upharpoonright\beta\Vdash p(\beta)\in\dot{Q}_{\beta}
. Its support is the set
\supp(p)=\alpha\setminus\{\beta<\alpha\colon p\upharpoonright\beta\Vdash p(\beta)=\dot{0}_{\mathbb{Q}_{\beta}}\}
.
Given
\alpha<\kappa
and a generic
G_{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}}
for
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
over the ground model
V
, we use the notation
\mathbb{P}/\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}\in V[G_{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}}]
to denote the associated quotient forcing.

5.

An iterated forcing
\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\kappa\rangle
with an Easton support is defined similarly, by adding the additional requirement that for every
\alpha\leq\kappa
and
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
, the set
\supp(p)
is an Easton set. In other words, for every
\lambda\leq\kappa
inaccessible,
\supp(p)
is bounded in
\lambda
. Similarly, we define an iterated forcing with a nonstationary support by requiring that for every
\alpha\leq\kappa
and
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
, the set
\supp(p)
is nonstationary in every inaccessible cardinal. In other words, for every inaccessible
\lambda\leq\kappa
,
\supp(p)\cap\lambda
is a nonstationary set.

Remark 2.2
.

If
W,U
are two
\kappa
-complete ultrafilters with the same ultrapower models
M_{W}=M_{U}
and embeddings
j_{W}=j_{U}
then
U\cong_{RK}W
.

Definition 2.3
.

1.

Let
Z\subseteq\mathbb{Q}
. A condition
q_{Z}\in\mathbb{Q}
is an
upper bound
for
Z
if
q_{Z}\geq q
for all
q\in Z
.
We say that
q_{Z}
is an
exact upper-bound
if it is an upper-bound and for every condition
q^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Q}
, if
q^{\prime}
extends all conditions in
Z
then it also extends
q_{Z}
.

2.

A poset
\mathbb{Q}
is
\kappa
-distributive if for every sequence
\langle D_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\beta\rangle
of length
\beta<\kappa
, consisting of dense open subsets of
\mathbb{Q}
, then
\cap\vec{D}=\bigcap_{\alpha<\beta}D_{\alpha}
is dense in
\mathbb{Q}
.

3.

Let
\mathbb{Q}
be a poset and
X\prec(H_{|\mathbb{Q}|^{+}},\mathbb{Q})
.
We say that a subset
G_{X}\subseteq X\cap\mathbb{Q}
is an
X
-generic set
for
\mathbb{Q}
if every two conditions in
G_{X}
have a common extension in
G_{X}
, and
G_{X}\cap D\neq\emptyset
for every
D\in X
which is dense in
\mathbb{Q}
.

We say that a condition
q\in\mathbb{Q}
is a
generic condition for
X
if

q\Vdash(\dot{G}_{\mathbb{Q}}\cap X)\text{ is }X\text{-generic .}

Definition 2.4
.

1.

A sequence
\vec{X}=\langle X_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
is said to be a
Continuous Elementary Chain
in a transitive structure
H
if
X_{\alpha}\prec H
for all
\alpha<\kappa
,
\alpha\subseteq X_{\alpha}\subseteq X_{\beta}
for all
\alpha<\beta
, and
X_{\delta}=\bigcup_{\alpha<\delta}X_{\alpha}
for every limit ordinal
\delta\leq\kappa
.

We say that a continuous chain
\vec{X}
is
Internally Approachable
if it satisfies further that
\vec{X}\upharpoonright\delta+1=\langle X_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\leq\delta\rangle\in X_{\delta+1}
for every successor ordinal
\delta+1<\kappa
.

2.

Let
\kappa
be a regular cardinal and
S\subseteq\kappa
stationary.
We say that a poset
\mathbb{Q}
of size
|\mathbb{Q}|\geq\kappa
has the
\kappa
-Continuous-Fusion
property with respect to
S
(
\kappa
C-Fusion(S)) if for every condition
q\in\mathbb{Q}
, a sequence
\vec{D}=\langle D_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
of dense open subsets of
\mathbb{Q}
, and a structure

(H_{|\mathbb{Q}|^{+}},<_{wo},\mathbb{Q},\vec{D},A)
that extends
H_{|\mathbb{Q}|^{+}}
,
there is an extension
q^{*}\geq q
, a club
C\subseteq\kappa
, and a continuous elementary chain
\vec{X}=\langle X_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
in
(H_{|\mathbb{Q}|^{+}},\mathbb{Q},\vec{D})
, so that

(a)

|X_{\alpha}|<\kappa
for all
\alpha<\kappa
.

(b)

For every
\alpha\in S\cap C
and an
X_{\alpha}
-generic set
G_{X_{\alpha}}\subseteq X_{\alpha}\cap\mathbb{Q}
(in
V
). If
G_{X_{\alpha}}
is compatible with
q^{*}
, then
G_{X_{\alpha}}\cup\{q^{*}\}
has an exact upper-bound, denoted
q^{*}_{G_{X_{\alpha}}}
, which also belongs to
D_{\alpha}
.

(c)

q^{*}
is a generic condition for
X=\bigcup_{\alpha<\kappa}X_{\alpha}
.

We shall call a condition
q^{*}
with these properties a
\kappa
-Continuous-Fusion witness for
\vec{X},\vec{D}
.

If
\kappa
is Mahlo then we say
\mathbb{Q}
has the
\kappa
C-Fusion property if it has the
\kappa
C-Fusion(S) property with respect the set of regular cardinals
S=\kappa\cap Reg
.

3.

We say that a poset
\mathbb{Q}
is
self coding
via sequences
\vec{S}^{\mathbb{Q}}
and
\vec{q}^{\mathbb{Q}}
when

(a)

\vec{q}^{\mathbb{Q}}=\langle q^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\tau}\mid\tau<|\mathbb{Q}|\rangle
is an enumeration of
\mathbb{Q}
,

(b)

there is a regular cardinal
\lambda_{\mathbb{Q}}\leq|\mathbb{Q}|
such that
\vec{S}^{\mathbb{Q}}=\langle S^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\tau}\mid\tau<|\mathbb{Q}|\rangle
consists of pairwise almost disjoint stationary subsets of
\lambda_{\mathbb{Q}}
,

(c)

for every
\tau<|\mathbb{Q}|
,

0_{\mathbb{Q}}\Vdash(\check{S}^{\mathbb{Q}}_{2\tau}\text{ is not stationary }\iff q^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\tau}\in\dot{G}_{\mathbb{Q}})\text{ and }

0_{\mathbb{Q}}\Vdash(\check{S}^{\mathbb{Q}}_{2\tau+1}\text{ is not stationary }\iff q^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\tau}\not\in\dot{G}_{\mathbb{Q}}),

4.

Let
\kappa
be a regular cardinal,
S\subseteq\kappa
stationary, and
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
an iterated forcing of length
\kappa
.
We say that
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
has the
\kappa
-Iteration-Fusion property with respect to
S
(
\kappa
Iteration-Fusion(S)) if for every condition
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
and a sequence
\langle D_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
of dense open subsets of
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
there is
p^{*}\geq p
and a club
C\subseteq\kappa
such that for every
\alpha\in C\cap S
,
the set

\{q\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}\mid q{}^{\frown}(p^{*}\setminus(\alpha+1))\in D_{\alpha}\}\text{ is dense in }\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}.

If
\kappa
is Mahlo then we say
\mathbb{Q}
has the
\kappa
Iteration-Fusion property if it has the
\kappa
Iteration Fusion(S) property with respect the set of regular cardinals
S=\kappa\cap Reg
.

Example 2.5
.

We show that for the generalized Sacks forcing from
[
15
]
and
[
11
]
, one can transform the standard tree-type fusion construction to prove the
\kappa
-Continuous-Fusion property from Definition
2.4
above.

Assume that
\kappa
is Mahlo, and let
Sacks^{*}(\kappa)
be the Sacks forcing with splitting at singulars, namely
p\in Sacks^{*}(\kappa)
if and only if:

•

p
is a sub-tree of the binary tree
2^{<\kappa}
.

•

p
is closed under union of increasing sequences of length
<\kappa
of its elements.

•

There exists a club
C\subseteq\kappa
such that for every singular
\alpha\in C
, every
t\in p\cap 2^{\alpha}
is a splitting point of
p
(namely, both
t^{\frown}\langle 0\rangle
and
t^{\frown}\langle 1\rangle
belong to
p
).

•

For every regular
\xi<\kappa
and
t\in p\cap 2^{\xi}
,
t
is not a splitting point of
p
.

We order
Sacks^{*}(\kappa)
by reverse inclusion.
It’s not hard to see that for every pair of conditions
p,q\in Sacks^{*}(\kappa)
,
p\vee q:=p\cap q
is the least upper bound of
{p,q}
. Furthermore,
Sacks^{*}(\kappa)
is
\kappa
-directed closed.

Claim 2.6
.

Sacks^{*}(\kappa)
satisfies the
\kappa
-continuous-Fusion property with respect to
S=\kappa\cap Reg
.

Proof.

Assume that
q\in Sacks^{*}(\kappa)
,
\vec{D}=\langle D_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\kappa\rangle
is a sequence of dense open subsets of
Sacks^{*}(\kappa)
. Let
\langle X_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\kappa\rangle
be any continuous and internally approachable chain of elementary substructures of
\left(H_{\kappa^{++}},Sacks^{*}(\kappa),\vec{D}\right)
, such that for every
\alpha<\kappa
,
X_{\alpha+1}
is closed under
\alpha
-sequences. such that
q,Sacks^{*}(\kappa),\langle D_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\kappa\rangle
are all in
X_{0}
.
We construct an increasing Fusion sequence of conditions, namely an increasing sequence
\langle q_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\kappa\rangle
extending
q
, alongside a continuous, increasing sequence
\langle\xi_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\kappa\rangle
of ordinals below
\kappa
, such that:

•

For every
\alpha<\kappa
, if
\xi_{\alpha}
is singular, then it is a splitting level of
q_{\alpha}
. Here, a splitting level of a condition
p
is a singular
\xi<\kappa
such that every node in
\mbox{Lev}_{\xi}(p)
is a splitting point of
p
, where
\mbox{Lev}_{\xi}(p):=p\cap 2^{\xi}
.

•

For every
\alpha<\beta<\kappa
,
\mbox{Lev}_{\xi_{\alpha}+1}(q_{\beta})=\mbox{Lev}_{\xi_{\alpha}+1}(q_{\alpha})
.

•

For every
\alpha<\kappa
,
q_{\alpha+1}\in X_{\alpha+1}
.

•

For every
\alpha<\kappa
and
t\in\mbox{Lev}_{\xi_{\alpha}+1}(q_{\alpha})\cap X_{\alpha+1}
, the tree

(q_{\alpha+1})_{t}=\{s\in q_{\alpha}\colon s,t\mbox{ are compatible sequences}\}

belongs to

D^{*}_{\alpha}:=D_{\alpha}\cap\left(\bigcap\{D\colon D\in X_{\alpha}\mbox{ is dense and open subset of }Sacks^{*}(\kappa)\}\right).

We point out that if
\alpha=\xi_{\alpha}
is regular then
q_{\alpha+1}
does not split at level
\alpha
, and has a unique extension
t
, and therefore
q_{\alpha+1}=(q_{\alpha+1})_{t}\in D_{\alpha}
.

The construction of a Fusion sequence of conditions is done by induction. At limit steps, take
q_{\alpha}=\bigcap\{q_{\beta}\colon\beta<\alpha\}
,
\xi_{\alpha}=\sup\{\xi_{\beta}\colon\beta<\alpha\}
. For the successor step, assume that
q_{\alpha},\xi_{\alpha}
have been constructed, and let
\xi_{\alpha+1}
be the first splitting level of
q_{\alpha}
above
\xi_{\alpha}
. For every
t\in q_{\alpha}\cap 2^{\xi_{\alpha+1}+1}
, shrink
\left(q_{\alpha}\right)_{t}
so that it enters the dense open set
D^{*}_{\alpha}
. Note that by
\kappa
-closure, and the fact that
|X_{\alpha}|<\kappa
,
D^{*}_{\alpha}
is indeed dense open. It’s not hard to verify that the resulting tree
q_{\alpha+1}
has an associated club whose singular points are splitting levels, and thus
q_{\alpha+1}\in Sacks^{*}(\kappa)
. Finally, note that
q_{\alpha}\in X_{\alpha+1}
by induction and internal approachability. Since
D^{*}_{\alpha}\in X_{\alpha+1}
(again, by internal approachability), we can choose
q_{\alpha+1}
such that
q_{\alpha+1}\in X_{\alpha+1}
.

This concludes the inductive construction. Let
q^{*}
be the tree generated from

\bigcup_{\alpha<\kappa}\mbox{Lev}_{\xi_{\alpha}+1}(q_{\alpha}).

The fact that
q^{*}\in Sacks^{*}(\kappa)
follows since every singular point in the club
\{\xi_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\kappa\}
is a splitting point of
q^{*}
.
We argue that
q^{*}
,
\langle X_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\kappa\rangle
and the club
C=\{\alpha<\kappa\mid\xi_{\alpha}=\alpha\}
are witnesses for the
\kappa
-continuous-Fusion property with respect to
S=\kappa\cap Reg
:

1.

Assume that
\alpha\in C
is regular and
G_{\alpha}
is an
X_{\alpha}
generic set which is compatible with
q^{*}
. We argue that
G_{X_{\alpha}}\cup\{q^{*}\}
has an exact upper bound, which is the subtree of
q^{*}
obtained by extending the stem of
q^{*}
to level
\xi_{\alpha}
, as determined by
\cap G_{X_{\alpha}}
. More formally, we will show below that
\cap G_{X_{\alpha}}
has a stem
t
of length
\xi_{\alpha}=\alpha
, which belongs to
q^{*}
. The desired exact upper bound will be the tree
q^{*}_{X_{\alpha}}:=(q^{*})_{t}
, which is the sub-tree of
q^{*}
which includes only nodes of
q^{*}
which are compatible with
t
.

First note that for every
\beta<\alpha
,
\xi_{\beta}\in X_{\alpha}
(by the above construction,
\xi_{\beta+1}\in X_{\beta+1}
is the least splitting point of
q_{\beta}
above
\xi_{\beta}
. Thus, by internal approachability, every initial segment of the sequence
\langle\xi_{\beta}\colon\beta<\alpha\rangle
belongs to
X_{\alpha}
). Thus,
q^{*}_{X_{\alpha}}
meets the dense open set of conditions which decide the generic stem up to height
\xi_{\beta}
. This is true for every
\beta<\alpha
separately, and thus
q^{*}_{X_{\alpha}}
might split only at levels
\geq\xi_{\alpha}
. But
\xi_{\alpha}=\alpha
, is regular, and thus
q^{*}_{X_{\alpha}}
does not split in level
\xi_{\alpha}
as well, and by the point made at the end of the construction of the sequence
\langle q_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
, we get that
q^{*}_{X_{\alpha}}\in D_{\alpha}
.

We argue that
q^{*}_{X_{\alpha}}=(q^{*})_{t}
is an exact upper bound of
G_{X_{\alpha}}\cup\{q^{*}\}
. Indeed, for every
p\in G_{X_{\alpha}}
,
(q^{*})_{t}
meets the dense open set of conditions which either extend
p
or incompatible with it (this dense open set belongs to
X_{\beta}
for some
\beta<\alpha
, and thus by the above construction,
(q^{*})_{t\upharpoonright(\xi_{\beta}+1)}
belongs to it). But
q^{*}
is compatible with every condition of
G_{X_{\alpha}}
, and thus
(q^{*})_{t}\geq p
. For exactness, note that if
q^{\prime}
is an upper bound of
G_{X_{\alpha}}\cup\{q^{*}\}
, then, arguing as above,
t
is the stem of
q^{\prime}
since
q^{\prime}
extends
G_{X_{\alpha}}
. But
q^{\prime}
extends
q^{*}
, and thus
q^{\prime}
extends
(q^{*})_{t}
.

2.

We argue that
q^{*}
is a generic condition for
X=\bigcup_{\alpha<\kappa}X_{\alpha}
. Assume that
G\subseteq Sacks^{*}(\kappa)
is generic over
V
and
q^{*}\in G
. We argue that
G\cap X
is
X
-generic. Indeed, given
D\in X
dense open in
Sacks^{*}(\kappa)
, there exists a regular
\alpha<\kappa
with
\alpha=\xi_{\alpha}
and
D\in X_{\alpha}
. It follows that for every
t\in\mbox{Lev}_{\alpha+1}(q^{*})\cap X_{\alpha+1}
,
(q^{*})_{t}\in D
. Let
t
be the initial segment of the generic branch up to height
\alpha
. Then
(q^{*})_{t}\in G
. We argue that also
(q^{*})_{t}\in D
. Since
X_{\alpha+1}
is closed under
\alpha
-sequences,
t\in X_{\alpha+1}
. It follows that
(q^{*})_{t}\in G\cap D
as desired.

∎

Lemma 2.7
.

Let
\mathbb{Q}
be a poset,
\kappa
an uncountable regular cardinal, and
\langle X_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
be a continuous elementary chain such that
X_{\alpha}\prec(H_{{|\mathbb{Q}|}^{+}},\mathbb{Q})
and
|X_{\alpha}|<\kappa
for all
\alpha<\kappa
. Suppose that
\kappa
remains regular in generic extensions by
\mathbb{Q}
and
q^{*}\in\mathbb{Q}
is a generic condition for
X=\bigcup_{\alpha<\kappa}X_{\alpha}
. Then there is a
\mathbb{Q}
-name of a club
\dot{C}\subseteq\kappa
such that
q^{*}
is a generic condition for
X_{\alpha}
for every
\alpha\in\dot{C}
. Namely,

q^{*}\Vdash\forall\alpha\in\dot{C}.\thinspace(\dot{G}_{\mathbb{Q}}\cap X_{\alpha})\text{ is }X_{\alpha}\text{-generic.}

Proof.

It suffices to verify that in every generic extension
V[G]
by
G\subseteq\mathbb{Q}
with
q^{*}\in G
, there is a club
C\subseteq\kappa
such that for every
\alpha\in C
,
(G\cap X_{\alpha})
is an
X_{\alpha}
generic set. Since
q^{*}
is a generic condition for
X
, for every dense open set
D\subseteq\mathbb{Q}
,
D\in X
, the intersection
G\cap D\cap X
is nonempty. Let
\beta_{D}<\kappa
be the minimal
\beta<\kappa
so that
G\cap D\cap X_{\beta}\neq\emptyset
. Define a function
f:\kappa\to\kappa
by

f(\alpha)=\sup\{\beta_{D}\mid D\in X_{\alpha}\text{ dense open in }\mathbb{Q}\}.

Let
C\subseteq\kappa
be the club of closure points of
f
. Since the sequence
\langle X_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
is a continuous chain, it follows that for every limit ordinal
\alpha\in C
and a dense open set
D\in X_{\alpha}
,
G\cap D\cap X_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset
. Hence
G
is a generic set for
X_{\alpha}
.
∎

Lemma 2.8
.

Let
\mathbb{Q}
be a
\kappa
-distributive poset satisfying the
\kappa
C-fusion property. Assume that
p\in\mathbb{Q}
,
n<\omega
and
\dot{f}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-name for a function from
\kappa^{n}
to
V
. Then there are a condition
p^{*}\geq q
, a
\mathbb{Q}
-name
\dot{C}^{*}
for a club in
\kappa
, and a function
F\in V
,
F\colon\kappa\to V
, such that, for every Mahlo
\alpha<\kappa
,
|F(\alpha)|\leq 2^{\alpha}
, and–

p^{*}\Vdash\forall\alpha\in(\dot{C}^{*}\cap Reg)\thinspace\left(\cup_{\vec{\beta}\in\alpha^{n-1}}\dot{f}(\vec{\beta}{}^{\frown}\{\alpha\})\right)\subseteq\check{F}({\alpha}).

Proof.

We can bound the values taken by the function
\dot{f}
and pick sufficiently large
\theta
such that
0_{\mathbb{P}}\Vdash\forall\alpha<\kappa\thinspace\dot{f}(\check{\alpha})\in H_{\theta}^{V}
. Let
<_{\theta}
be a well-ordering of
H_{\theta}
. Let
\vec{D}=\langle D_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
be a sequence of dense sets in
\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
given by

D_{\alpha}=\{q\in\mathbb{Q}\mid\exists\langle x^{\alpha}_{\vec{\beta}}\rangle_{\vec{\beta}\in\alpha^{n-1}}\subseteq H_{\theta}^{V}.\thinspace\forall\vec{\beta}\in\alpha^{n-1}\thinspace q\Vdash\dot{f}(\vec{\beta}{}^{\frown}\{\alpha\})=\check{x}^{\alpha}_{\vec{\beta}}\}.

The density of
D_{\alpha}
follows since
\mathbb{Q}
is
\kappa
-distributive.
Since
\mathbb{Q}
has the
\kappa
C-Fusion property, there are
q^{*}\geq q
, a club
C_{1}\subseteq\kappa
, and a continuous elementary chain
\vec{X}=\langle X_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
of substructures
X_{\alpha}\prec(H_{\kappa^{++}}^{V[G]},\mathbb{Q},\vec{D})
, such that for each inaccessible cardinal
\alpha\in C_{1}
, given a condition
q_{X_{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{Q}
which is an exact upper bound to a
X_{\alpha}
-generic set, if
q_{X_{\alpha}}\parallel q^{*}
then
q^{*}\vee q_{X_{\alpha}}\in D^{*}_{\alpha}
.
Moreover, by Lemma
2.7
there is a
\mathbb{Q}
-name
\dot{C}_{2}
of a club in
\kappa
such that for every
\alpha\in\dot{C}_{2}
,
q^{*}
forces that
\dot{G}_{\mathbb{Q}}\cap X_{\alpha}
is a generic set for
X_{\alpha}
. Since
\mathbb{Q}
is
\kappa
-distributive,
\dot{G}_{\mathbb{Q}}\cap X_{\alpha}\in V
for every such
\alpha
. Therefore by the C-Fusion property,
q^{*}
forces that for every inaccessible
\alpha\in C_{1}\cap\dot{C}_{2}
, we must have an exact upper bound
\dot{q}_{\alpha}
for
\dot{G}_{\mathbb{Q}}\cap X_{\alpha}
. Such
\dot{q}_{\alpha}
is compatible with
q^{*}
.

For each
\alpha\in C_{1}
, let

\Gamma(X_{\alpha})=\{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}\mid q||q^{*}\text{ and }q\text{ is an exact upper-bound of some }X_{\alpha}\text{-generic set }G_{X_{\alpha}}^{q}\}.

For every
q\in\Gamma(X_{\alpha})
let
\langle x^{\alpha}_{\vec{\beta}}(q)\rangle_{\vec{\beta}\in\alpha^{n-1}}\subseteq H_{\theta}^{V}
be such that for every
\vec{\beta}\in\alpha^{n-1}
,

q\vee q^{*}\Vdash\dot{f^{*}}(\vec{\beta}{}^{\frown}\{\alpha\})=\check{x}^{\alpha}_{\vec{\beta}}(q).

Since
\vec{X}
is continuous we may assume
|X_{\alpha}|=\alpha
for all
\alpha\in C_{1}
, and thus
|\Gamma(X_{\alpha})|\leq(2^{\alpha})^{V[G_{\kappa}]}=\alpha^{++}
. Let

F(\alpha)=\{x^{\alpha}_{\vec{\beta}}(q)\mid\vec{\beta}\in\alpha^{n-1},q\in\Gamma(X_{\alpha})\}.

It follows that
|F(\alpha)|\leq\alpha^{++}
and

q^{*}\Vdash\forall\alpha\in\check{C}_{1}\cap\dot{C}_{2}\cap Reg\ \forall\vec{\beta}\in\alpha^{n-1}\ \dot{f}(\vec{\beta}{}^{\frown}\{\alpha\})\in\check{F}(\alpha).

∎

Lemma 2.9
.

Suppose that
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
is an iterated forcing which has the

\kappa
Iteration-Fusion property, and for each
\alpha<\kappa
,

\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
is trivial if
\alpha
is not inaccessible,
and the quotient tail forcing
\mathbb{P}/\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
is (forced to be)
\alpha
-distributive.
Then for every
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
and a sequence
\langle D_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
of dense open subsets of
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
, there is an extension
p^{\prime}\geq p
and a closed unbounded set
C\subseteq\kappa
such that for each regular
\alpha\in C
, the set

\{w\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}\mid w{}^{\frown}(p^{\prime}\setminus\alpha+1)\in\bigcap_{\beta<\alpha^{++}}D_{\beta}\}

is dense in
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}/(p^{\prime}\upharpoonright\alpha+1)
.

Proof.

For each
\alpha<\kappa
, let
\dot{d}_{\alpha}
be the
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}
-name of the set of conditions

\dot{d}_{\alpha}=\{q\in\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}/\dot{G}(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha})\mid\exists w\in\dot{G}(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha})\thinspace w{}^{\frown}q\in D_{\alpha}\}.

For each
\alpha<\kappa
,
0_{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}}
forces
\dot{d}_{\alpha}
is dense open in
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}/\dot{G}(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1})
. Moreover, since
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}/\dot{G}(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1})=\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}/\dot{G}(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha^{*}})
where
\alpha^{*}
is the first inaccessible cardinal above
\alpha
, and
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}/\dot{G}(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha^{*}})
is
\alpha^{*}
-distributive,
0_{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}}
forces
\dot{d}^{*}_{\alpha}=\bigcap_{\beta<\alpha^{++}}\dot{d}_{\beta}
is also dense open.
It follows that for each
\alpha<\kappa
the set

D^{\prime}_{\alpha}=\{q\in\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}\mid q\upharpoonright\alpha+1\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}}q\setminus\alpha+1\in\dot{d}^{*}_{\alpha}\}

is dense open in
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
.
Applying the
\kappa
Iteration-Fusion assumption for
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
and the sequence
\langle D^{\prime}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
we find
p^{\prime}\geq p
and a closed unbounded set
C\subseteq\kappa
such that for every inaccessible cardinal
\alpha\in C
, the set

\{w\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}\mid w{}^{\frown}(p^{\prime}\setminus\alpha+1)\in D^{\prime}_{\alpha}\}=\{w\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}\mid w{}^{\frown}(p^{\prime}\setminus\alpha+1)\in\bigcap_{\beta<\alpha^{++}}D_{\alpha}\}

is dense in
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}/(p^{\prime}\upharpoonright\alpha+1)

∎

Lemma 2.10
.

Suppose that
\kappa
is Mahlo and
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
is an iterated forcing which has the
\kappa
Iteration-Fusion property, and, for every
\alpha<\kappa
,
|\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}|<\kappa
. Then
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
preserves stationary subsets of
\kappa
. In particular,
\kappa
remains Mahlo in
V^{\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}}
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that every club subset of
\kappa
in
V^{\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}}
contains a club subset of
\kappa
from
V
.

Assume that
\dot{C}
is a
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
-name for a club subset of
\kappa
. For every
\alpha<\kappa
, let
D_{\alpha}
be the dense open subset of conditions deciding the
\alpha
-th element
\dot{c}_{\alpha}
in the increasing enumeration of
\dot{C}
. By the
\kappa
Iteration-Fusion property, every condition
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
can be extended to a condition
p^{*}\geq p
, such that for some club
E\subseteq\kappa
and for every regular
\alpha\in E
, the set

\{q\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}\colon\exists c<\kappa,q^{\frown}\left(p^{*}\setminus(\alpha+1)\right)\Vdash c=\dot{c}_{\alpha}\}

is dense open in
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}
.

Given such
p^{*}
and club
E\subseteq\kappa
, let
C^{*}
be the set of closure points of the function which maps each
\alpha\in E\cap Reg
to–

\sup\{c<\kappa\colon\exists q\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1},q^{\frown}\left(p^{*}\setminus(\alpha+1)\right)\Vdash c=\dot{c}_{\alpha}\}<\kappa

where the supremum above is indeed below
\kappa
since
|\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}|<\kappa
. Then
C^{*}
is a club in
\kappa
, and
p^{*}\Vdash C^{*}\subseteq\dot{C}
.
∎

Lemma 2.11
.

Suppose that
\kappa
is Mahlo and
2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{+}
. Let
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
be an iterated forcing which has the
\kappa
Iteration-Fusion property, such that for every
\alpha<\kappa
,
|\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}|<\kappa
,
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
is trivial if
\alpha
is not inaccessible, and the quotient tail forcing
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}/\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
is (forced to be)
\alpha
-distributive.
Then
2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{+}
in
V^{\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}}
.

Proof.

Assume that
G\subseteq\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
is generic over
V
. Let
\dot{A}
is a
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
name for a subset of
\kappa
. For every
\alpha<\kappa
, let

D_{\alpha}=\{q\in\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}\colon q\upharpoonright(\alpha+1)\Vdash\exists A_{\alpha}\subseteq\alpha,q\setminus(\alpha+1)\Vdash\dot{A}\cap\alpha=A_{\alpha}\}.

Note that
D_{\alpha}
is indeed dense for every
\alpha<\kappa
, since
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}/\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}
is
\alpha^{+}
-distributive.
By the
\kappa
Iteration-Fusion property, there exist
p\in G
and a club
C\subseteq\kappa
such that, for every
\alpha\in C\cap Reg
, the set

\{q\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}\colon q\Vdash\exists A_{\alpha}\subseteq\alpha,\ p\setminus(\alpha+1)\Vdash\dot{A}\cap\alpha=A_{\alpha}\}

is dense in
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}
. It follows that, for every
\alpha\in C\cap Reg
, there exists a
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}
-name
\dot{A}_{\alpha}
, such that the weakest condition of
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}
forces that

p\setminus(\alpha+1)\Vdash\dot{A}\cap\alpha=\dot{A}_{\alpha}.

The set
(\dot{A})_{G}
is uniquely determined from the sequence
\langle\dot{A}_{\alpha}\colon\alpha\in C\cap Reg\rangle
and
G
. By standard arguments, we can identify each name
\dot{A}_{\alpha}
with a function from
\alpha
to the set
X_{\alpha}
of maximal antichains in
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}
. Since
\kappa
is strongly inaccessible and
|\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}|<\kappa
, there are
2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{+}
elements in
\prod_{\alpha\in C\cap Reg}(X_{\alpha})^{\alpha}
. It follows that
2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{+}
holds in
V[G]
.
∎

2.1
The Friedman-Magidor Blueprint

Definition 2.12

(FM-blueprint)
.

Let
\kappa
be a measurable cardinal and
\mathbb{P}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle
a
nonstationary-support
iteration of length
\kappa+1
. The Friedman-Magidor (FM) blueprint includes the following additional assumptions for
\mathbb{P}
:

FM1.

For each
\alpha\leq\kappa
, the
{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha}-
name
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is the trivial poset unless
\alpha
is Mahlo.

FM2.

For every Mahlo cardinal
\alpha\leq\kappa
,

0_{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}}
forces
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
satisfies the following requirements

(a)

\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
has size
|\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}|=\alpha^{++}
and adds
\alpha^{++}
new subsets to
\alpha
,

(b)

\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is
\alpha
-distributive, satisfies the
\alpha^{++}
.c.c, and preserves the Mahloness of
\alpha
.

(c)

\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
has the
\alpha
C-Fusion property,

(d)

\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is self coding via a pair
(\dot{\vec{q}}^{\alpha},\dot{\vec{S}}^{\alpha})
.

FM3.

For every Mahlo cardinal
\alpha\leq\kappa
,

(a)

\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
has the
\alpha
Iteration-Fusion property,

(b)

0_{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}}\Vdash(\mathbb{P}/\mathbb{P}_{\alpha})\text{ is }\alpha\text{-distributive}
.

FM4.

The iteration is uniformly definable in sense that there are parameter-free formulas
\varphi_{\mathbb{P}}(v,x)
,
\varphi_{\mathbb{Q}}(v,x,y)
, and
\varphi_{F}(v,x)
, such that the following holds in every Mahlo cardinal
\alpha\leq\kappa
and an inner model
M
containing
H_{\alpha^{++}}
,

(a)

\varphi_{\mathbb{P}}(\alpha,x)
defines
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}
,

(b)

\varphi_{F}(\alpha,x)
defines a
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}
-name for a sequence of functions
\vec{f}^{\alpha}=\langle f^{\alpha}_{\tau}\mid\tau<\alpha^{++}\rangle\subseteq{}^{\alpha}\alpha
that witnesses
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
adds
\alpha^{++}
new subsets to
\alpha
,

(c)

\varphi_{\mathbb{Q}}(\alpha,x,y)
defines the
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-name for a pair
(\vec{q}^{\alpha},\vec{S}^{\alpha})
, which are forced by
0_{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}}
to be witnesses that
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
is self coding.

Throughout this section, we assume GCH holds in the ground model
V
.
We start with a lemma which states a useful combinatorial property of the a poset
\mathbb{P}
that satisfies the FM-blueprint.

Lemma 2.13
.

(GCH) Suppose that
\mathbb{P}
satisfies the FM-blueprint assumptions. Let
\dot{f}
be a
\mathbb{P}
-name for a function from
\kappa^{n}
for some
n<\omega
to ground model sets in
V
, and
p\in\mathbb{P}
a condition. Then there are
p^{*}\geq p
, a
\mathbb{P}
-name
\dot{C}
of a club in
\kappa
, and a function
F:\kappa\to V
such that
|F(\alpha)|\leq\alpha^{++}
for every Mahlo cardinal, and

p^{*}\Vdash\forall\alpha\in(\dot{C}\cap Reg)\thinspace\left(\cup_{\vec{\beta}\in\alpha^{n-1}}\dot{f}(\vec{\beta}{}^{\frown}\{\alpha\})\right)\subseteq\check{F}({\alpha}).

Proof.

We can bound the values taken by the function
\dot{f}
and pick sufficiently large
\theta
such that
0_{\mathbb{P}}\Vdash\forall\alpha<\kappa\thinspace\dot{f}(\check{\alpha})\in H_{\theta}^{V}
.
We fix a well-ordering
<_{\theta}
of
H_{\theta}
.
Given
p^{*}\in\mathbb{P}=\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}*\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
. We write it as
p=p\upharpoonright\kappa*\dot{p}_{\kappa}
where
p\upharpoonright\kappa\in\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
and
p\upharpoonright\kappa\Vdash\dot{p}_{\kappa}\in\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
. We break the ground approximation argument of
\dot{f}
into two parts, where we first approximate the
\mathbb{P}
-name
\dot{f}
by a
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
name of a function
\dot{F^{*}}
, and then approximate
\dot{F^{*}}
by a function
F\in V
.

For the first approximation step, fix
G_{\kappa}\subseteq\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
generic over
V
, and apply Lemma
2.8
in
V[G_{\kappa}]
to find
F^{*}\in V[G_{\kappa}]
,
p^{*}_{\kappa}\geq p_{\kappa}
and a
\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
-name
\dot{C}^{*}
for a club subset of
\kappa
such that, for every Mahlo
\alpha<\kappa
,
V[G_{\kappa}]\models|F(\alpha)|\leq 2^{\alpha}
, and–

p_{\kappa}^{*}\Vdash\forall\alpha\in(\dot{C}^{*}\cap Reg)\thinspace\left(\cup_{\vec{\beta}\in\alpha^{n-1}}\dot{f}(\vec{\beta}{}^{\frown}\{\alpha\})\right)\subseteq\check{F}^{*}({\alpha}).

For each inaccessible cardinal
\alpha\in C^{*}
, let
\bar{x}^{\alpha}=\langle x^{\alpha}_{i}\mid i<\alpha^{++}\rangle
be an enumeration of
F^{*}(\alpha)
according to the fix well-ordering
<_{\theta}
, of length
\alpha^{++}
(possibly with repetitions).

Moving back to
V
for the second approximation step, there is
\bar{p}\geq_{\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}}p\upharpoonright\kappa
which forces the relevant properties above for
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
-names,
\dot{F}^{*},\langle\dot{\bar{x}}^{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
, and
\dot{p}^{*}_{\kappa}
, of
F^{*}
,
\langle\bar{x}^{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
, and
p^{*}_{\kappa}
respectively. Also, let
\dot{C}_{1}
be the

\mathbb{P}=(\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}*\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa})
-name for
\dot{C}^{*}
.
Working in
V
, we may take a sequence of dense open subsets of
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
,

\langle D^{\alpha}_{i}\mid\alpha<\kappa,i<\alpha^{++}\rangle
such that for an inaccessible cardinal
\alpha<\kappa
and
i<\alpha^{++}
,
D^{\alpha}_{i}
consists of conditions

r\in\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
for which there is a set
x^{\alpha}_{i}(r)\in H_{\theta}
so that

r\Vdash\dot{x}^{\alpha}_{i}=\check{x}_{i}^{\alpha}(r)
.
By Lemma
2.9
there is an extension
p^{\prime}\geq\bar{p}
and a closed unbounded set
C_{2}\subseteq\kappa
such that for every inaccessible cardinal
\alpha\in C_{2}
, the set

W_{\alpha}=\{w\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}\mid w{}^{\frown}(p^{\prime}\setminus\alpha+1)\in\bigcap_{\beta\leq\alpha,i<\beta^{++}}D^{\beta}_{i}\}

is dense in
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}/(p^{\prime}\upharpoonright\alpha+1)
.
For each
w\in W_{\alpha}
denote
w{}^{\frown}(p^{\prime}\setminus\alpha+1)
by
r_{w}
, and
define

F(\alpha)=\{x^{\alpha}_{i}({r_{w}})\mid w\in W_{\alpha}\text{ and }i<\alpha^{++}\}.

Define
p^{*}=p^{\prime}{}^{\frown}\dot{p}^{*}_{\kappa}\in\mathbb{P}=\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}*\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
and
\dot{C}^{*}=\dot{C}_{1}\cap\check{C}_{2}
. It follows that
|F(\alpha)|\leq\alpha^{++}
for every inaccessible cardinal
\alpha<\kappa
, and
p^{*}
forces that for each
\alpha\in\dot{C}^{*}
and
\vec{\beta}\in\alpha^{n-1}
,
\dot{f}(\vec{\beta}{}^{\frown}\{\alpha\})\in\check{F}(\alpha)
.
∎

Theorem 2.1
.

Suppose that
V=L[\bar{E}]
is a fine-structural extender model (in the sense of
[
28
]
) which is minimal for the existence of a measurable cardinal
\kappa
carrying a
(\kappa,\kappa^{++})
-extender
E
.
Let
\mathbb{P}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle
be an iterated forcing poset which satisfies the FM-blueprint. Then in a generic extension
V[G]
by
G\subseteq\mathbb{P}
,
\kappa
is the only measurable cardinal, there is a unique normal measure
U
on
\kappa
, and
j_{U}\upharpoonright V=j_{E}
.

Fix a
V
-generic filter
G\subseteq\mathbb{P}
.
We need to show that in
V[G]
, there is a unique normal measure
U
on
\kappa
, and that
j_{U}\upharpoonright V=j_{E}
is the ultrapower embedding by the unique
(\kappa,\kappa^{++})
-extender
E\in V=L[\bar{E}]
.
We break the proof of this statement into the following four parts, given in the next Lemmas.

Lemma 2.14
.

In
V[G]
there exists a unique
M_{E}
-generic filter
G^{*}\subseteq j_{E}(\mathbb{P})
which contains
j_{E}``G
.

By Silver’s argument for extensions of elementary embedding in forcing generic extension, such
M_{E}
-generic
G^{*}
gives rise to a unique extension
j^{*}:V[G]\to M_{E}[G^{*}]
of the embedding
j_{E}
, defined by
j^{*}(\sigma_{G})=j_{E}(\sigma)_{G^{*}}
for a name
\sigma\in V^{\mathbb{P}}
.

Lemma 2.15
.

Every
a\in M_{E}[G^{*}]
has the form
j^{*}(g)(\kappa)
for some
g\in{}^{\kappa}V[G]
,
g\in V[G]
.

This implies the model
M_{E}[G^{*}]
and the embedding
j^{*}
identify with the ultrapower of
V[G]
by the
j^{*}
-derived normal measure

U=\{X\subseteq\kappa\mid\kappa\in j^{*}(X)\}.

Lemma 2.16
.

Let
U^{*}
be a
\kappa
-complete ultrafilter in
V[G]
, and
j_{U^{*}}:V[G]\to M_{U^{*}}
be its induced ultrapower embedding. Then
there is
a finite normal iteration
\langle M^{*}_{i},j^{*}_{i,j}:i\leq j\leq\ell\rangle
of
V=M^{*}_{0}
by extenders
E^{*}_{i}\in M^{*}_{i}
,
i<\ell
(i.e.,
j^{*}_{i,i+1}=j^{M^{*}_{i}}_{E^{*}_{i}}:M^{*}_{i}\to M^{*}_{i+1}\cong\Ult(M^{*}_{i},E^{*}_{i})
is the ultrapower map), whose critical points
\kappa^{*}_{i}=cp(E^{*}_{i})
,
i<\ell
, are increasing,
such that
j_{U^{*}}\upharpoonright V=j^{*}_{0,\ell}
,
j_{U^{*}}``V=M^{*}_{\ell}
,
M_{U^{*}}=M^{*}_{\ell}[H^{*}]
is a generic extension of
M^{*}_{\ell}
by
H^{*}=j_{U^{*}}(G)
.

Lemma 2.17
.

\kappa
is the only measurable cardinal in
V[G]
, and
if
U^{*}
is a normal measure on
\kappa
in
V[G]
whose associated ultrapower embedding is
j_{U^{*}}:V[G]\to M_{U^{*}}
, then
j_{U^{*}}\upharpoonright V=j_{E}
and
M_{U^{*}}=M_{E}[H^{*}]
for some
M_{E}
-generic filter
H^{*}\subseteq j_{E}(\mathbb{P})
.

By combining the statement of Lemma
2.17
with the uniqueness part of Lemma
2.14
, it follows that
H^{*}=G^{*}
and that
U^{*}=U
, which concludes the proof of Theorem
2.1
.
We proceed to prove Lemmas
2.14
-
2.17
.

Proof.

(Lemma
2.14
)

Write
j=j_{E}
,
M=M_{E}
, and
G=G_{\kappa}*G(\kappa)
, where
G_{\kappa}\subseteq\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
is
V
-generic, and
G(\kappa)\subseteq\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}=(\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\kappa})_{G_{\kappa}}
.
We first show that in
V[G]
,
j:V\to M
extends to
j^{1}:V[G_{\kappa}]\to M[G^{*}_{j(\kappa)}]
where
G^{*}_{j(\kappa)}\subseteq j(\mathbb{P}_{\kappa})
is
M
-generic and contains
j``G_{\kappa}
.
The uniform definability property of the FM-blueprint and the fact
H_{\kappa^{++}}\subseteq M
imply that
j(\mathbb{P})_{\kappa+1}=\mathbb{P}_{\kappa+1}
. In particular,
G
is
M
-generic for
j(\mathbb{P}_{\kappa})_{\kappa+1}
.
Moving forward to extend
j^{1}
to have domain
V[G]=V[G_{\kappa}*G(\kappa)]
, define

G^{*}(j(\kappa))=\langle j^{1}``G(\kappa)\rangle=\{q^{*}\in j^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa})\mid\forall q\in G(\kappa)\thinspace q^{*}\parallel j^{1}(q)\}.

To show
G^{*}_{j(\kappa)}
is
M
-generic it suffices to verify that in
M[G]
, for every dense open subset
D\subseteq j(\mathbb{P}_{\kappa})/G
there is
p\in G_{\kappa}
such that
(j(p)\setminus\kappa+1)_{G}\in D
.
Let
\dot{D}
be a
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa+1}
-name for
D
.
Since
E
is
(\kappa,\kappa^{++})
,
D=j(f)(\nu)
for some
f:\kappa\to\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{P}_{\kappa})
and
\nu\in[\kappa,\kappa^{++})
, so that for every Mahlo cardinal
\alpha<\kappa
and
\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha^{++})
,
f(\beta)
is a
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}
-name of a dense open subset of
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}/\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}
.
For each Mahlo cardinal
\alpha<\kappa
, by the FM-blueprint assumption,
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}/\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}
is
<\beta_{\alpha}
-distributive, where
\beta_{\alpha}
is the first Mahlo cardinal above
\alpha
. Therefore,
0_{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}}\Vdash\bigcap_{\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha^{++})}f(\beta)
is dense open.
Hence the set

D_{\alpha}=\{\ p\in\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}\mid p\upharpoonright(\alpha+1)\Vdash p\setminus(\alpha+1)\in\bigcap_{\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha^{++})}f(\beta)\ \}

is dense open in
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
, and
by the
\kappa
Iteration-Fusion property of
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
there is
p^{*}\in G
and a club
C\subseteq\kappa
so that for every Mahlo cardinal
\alpha\in C
,
p^{*}\upharpoonright(\alpha+1)\Vdash p^{*}\setminus(\alpha+1)\in\bigcap_{\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha^{++})}f(\beta)
.
Applying
j
, we see that
j(p^{*})\upharpoonright\kappa+1\Vdash j(p^{*})\setminus(\kappa+1)\in\dot{D}
. Since
j(p^{*})\upharpoonright\kappa+1\in G
, it follows that
j(p^{*})\setminus(\kappa+1)\in D
.

Having established
G^{*}_{j(\kappa)}\subseteq j(\mathbb{P}_{\kappa})
is generic over
M
and contains
j``G_{\kappa}
, we conclude
j
extends to

j^{1}:V[G_{\kappa}]\to M[G^{*}_{j(\kappa)}].

Moving forward to extend
j^{1}
to have domain
V[G]=V[G_{\kappa}*G(\kappa)]
we would like to define
G^{*}(j(\kappa))\subseteq j^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa})
that is generic over
M[G^{*}_{j(\kappa)}]
. To this end,
we consider some properties of
\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
and
j^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa})
that follow from the assumption that
\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
has the
\kappa
-Continuous-Fusion (
\kappa
-CF) property. By
\kappa
-CF, for every
q\in G(\kappa)
and a sequence of dense open sets
\vec{D}=\langle D_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
there is a continuous chain of structures
\vec{X}=\langle X_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
and
q^{*}\in G(\kappa)
that is a
\kappa
-Continuous-Fusion witness for
q,\vec{D},\vec{X}
.
As
|X_{\alpha}|<\kappa
for all
\alpha<\kappa
we have that
j(X_{\alpha})=j[X_{\alpha}]
, and
since the sequence
\vec{X}
is continuous, we see that the
\kappa
-th structure
j(\vec{X})_{\kappa}
of
j^{1}(\vec{X})
is equal to

j^{1}(\vec{X})_{\kappa}=\bigcup_{\alpha<\kappa}j^{1}(X_{\alpha})=j^{1}[\bigcup\vec{X}].

Having
q^{*}\in G(\kappa)
being a generic condition for
X=\bigcup\vec{X}
we conclude that

G_{j^{1}(\vec{X})_{\kappa}}=j^{1}[G(\kappa)\cap\bigcup\vec{X}]

is generic for
j^{1}(\vec{X})_{\kappa}
, and consists of conditions that are compatible with
j^{1}(q^{*})
.
Moreover, as
\kappa\in j(C\cap S)
for
S=Reg\cap\kappa
, it follows from the elementarity of
j^{1}
and the fact
q^{*}
is a
\kappa
-Continuous-Fusion witness for
\vec{D},\vec{X}
that the set

\{j^{1}(q^{*})\}\cup G_{j^{1}(\vec{X})_{\kappa}}

has an exact upper bound, denoted
j^{1}(q^{*})_{G_{j(\vec{X})_{\kappa}}}
.
With this in mind, we
define
G^{*}_{j(\kappa)}\subseteq j^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa})
by

G^{*}(j^{1}(\kappa))=\langle\{j^{1}(q^{*})_{G_{j^{1}(\vec{X})_{\kappa}}}\mid q^{*}\in G_{\kappa}\text{ is a }\kappa-CF\text{ witness for some }\vec{D},\vec{X}\}\rangle

Namely,
G^{*}(j^{1}(\kappa))
is the set generated (i.e., closing under weaker conditions) by the set of conditions of the form
j^{1}(q^{*})_{G_{j^{1}(\vec{X})_{\kappa}}}
, where
q^{*}\in G(\kappa)
, where
q^{*}\in G(\kappa)
is
\kappa
-CF witness for some
\vec{X},\vec{D}
.

Let us first verify that
G^{*}(j^{1}(\kappa))
is a filter, i.e., that every two conditions of the form
j^{1}(q_{1}^{*})_{G_{j^{1}(\vec{X})_{\kappa}}}
and
j^{1}(q_{2}^{*})_{G_{j^{1}(\vec{X})_{\kappa}}}
are compatible, whenever
q^{*}_{1},q^{*}_{2}\in G(\kappa)
are
\kappa
-CF witnesses for pairs
\vec{X}^{1}=\langle X^{1}_{\alpha}\rangle_{\alpha<\kappa},\vec{D}^{1}
and
\vec{X}^{2}=\langle X^{2}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle,\vec{D}^{2}
, respectively.

By density, there is
q^{*}\in G(\kappa)
that extends both
q^{*}_{1},q^{*}_{2}
and is a
\kappa
-CF witness for a sequence
\vec{X}
(and a trivial
\vec{D}
) consisting of structure

X_{\alpha}\prec(H_{|\mathbb{Q}|^{+}},\in,<_{wo},\mathbb{Q},\vec{X}^{1},\vec{X}^{2}).

By continuity of the sequences
\vec{X},\vec{X}^{1},\vec{X}^{2}
, there is a club set of
\alpha<\kappa
such that
X^{1}_{\alpha},X^{2}_{\alpha}\subseteq X_{\alpha}
.
Applying
j^{1}
, we conclude
j^{1}(q^{*})
extends both
j^{1}(q^{*}_{1}),j^{1}(q^{*}_{2})
, and that

G_{j^{1}(\vec{X})_{\kappa}}
contains both
G_{j^{1}(\vec{X^{1}})_{\kappa}}
and
G_{j^{1}(\vec{X^{2}})_{\kappa}}
.
It follows that
j^{1}(q^{*})_{j^{1}(\vec{X})_{\kappa}}
is an upper bound of
\{j^{1}(q^{*}_{i})\}\cup G_{j^{1}(\vec{X^{i}})_{\kappa}}
for both
i=1,2
, and hence, is a common extension of their exact upper bounds
j^{1}(q^{*})_{j^{1}(\vec{X^{1}})_{\kappa}}
,
j^{1}(q^{*})_{j^{1}(\vec{X^{2}})_{\kappa}}
.

Next, we need to show
G^{*}(j^{1}(\kappa))\subseteq j^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa})
is generic over
M[G^{*}_{j(\kappa)}]
. Suppose
D^{*}\subseteq j^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa})
,
D^{*}\in M[G^{*}_{j(\kappa)}]
is dense open, and let
\dot{D^{*}}
be a
j(\mathbb{P}_{\kappa})
-name for
D^{*}
. Choosing a function
f^{*}
and
\nu<\kappa^{++}
such that
\dot{D}^{*}=j(f^{*})(\nu)
, we may assume that for every
\alpha<\kappa
,
f^{*}(\alpha)
is a
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
-name of a dense open subset of
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\kappa}
. Define for each
\alpha<\kappa
,
D^{*}_{\alpha}=\bigcap_{\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha^{++})}(f^{*}(\beta))_{G_{\kappa}}
. Since
\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
is
\kappa
-distributive,
D^{*}_{\alpha}
is dense open.
Working in
V[G_{\kappa}]
where
\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
has the
\kappa
C-Fusion property, there is a dense set of conditions
q^{*}\in\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
for which there is a club
C\subseteq\kappa
, and an internally approachable continuous chain
\vec{X}=\langle X_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
of elementary substructures of
(H_{\kappa^{++}}[G_{\kappa}],\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa})
that witness the
\kappa
C-Fusion property with respect to the sequence of dense sets
\langle D^{*}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
.
Let
X=\bigcup_{\alpha<\kappa}X_{\alpha}

and
j^{1}(\vec{X})=\vec{X}^{*}=\langle X^{*}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<j(\kappa)\rangle
. By the continuity of
\vec{X}^{*}
, we have
X^{*}_{\kappa}=j^{1}``X
. By the
\kappa
C-Fusion property,
q^{*}
is a generic condition for
X
. Moving to
V[G]=V[G_{\kappa}*G(\kappa)]
we may assume
q^{*}\in G(\kappa)
and conclude that
G(\kappa)\cap X
is an
X
-generic set for
\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
.
Since
j\upharpoonright X:X\leftrightarrow j``X=X^{*}_{\kappa}
belongs to
M
(as
|X|=\kappa
and
{}^{\kappa}M\subseteq M
) and
G(\kappa)\in M[G^{*}_{j(\kappa)}]
, we conclude that
G_{X^{*}_{\kappa}}=j^{1}``(G(\kappa)\cap X)
is an
X^{*}_{\kappa}
generic set for
j^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa})
. Moreover, this is forced by
j^{1}(q^{*})
with respect to
\vec{X}^{*}
. It follows that
G_{X^{*}_{\kappa}}\cup\{j^{1}(q^{*})\}
an exact upper-bound
q^{*}_{G_{X^{*}_{\kappa}}}
, which belongs to
D^{*}_{\kappa}\subseteq D^{*}
. We conclude that
G^{*}(j(\kappa))\cap D^{*}\neq\emptyset
, and that

G^{*}=G^{*}_{j(\kappa)}*G^{*}(j(\kappa))
is a generic subset of
j(\mathbb{P})
over
M
, and contains
j``G
.

Next, we show
G^{*}
is unique. Suppose
H^{*}\subseteq j(\mathbb{P})
is
M
-generic and contains
j``G
. First,
G_{\kappa}\subseteq j``G
as
j(p)\upharpoonright\kappa=p\upharpoonright\kappa
for all
p\in\mathbb{P}=\mathbb{P}_{\kappa+1}
. Therefore
H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}=G_{\kappa}
. Second, the uniformly definable self coding properties of
\mathbb{P}
guarantee
H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
must be equal to
G(\kappa)
. Indeed, recall the sequences
\vec{q}^{\kappa}=\langle q^{\kappa}_{\tau}\mid\tau<\kappa^{++}\rangle
and
\vec{S}^{\kappa}=\langle S^{\kappa}_{\tau}\mid\tau<\kappa^{++}\rangle
which are used to code
\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
(over both
V
and
M
), if
q^{\kappa}_{\tau}\in(H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa})\triangle G(\kappa)
for some
\tau<\kappa^{++}
then
V[G]
and
M[H^{*}]
will disagree on which of the two sets
S^{\kappa}_{2\tau},S^{\kappa}_{2\tau+1}
are not stationary. This is absurd as
M[H^{*}]\subseteq V[G]
.
Having established that
H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}_{\kappa+1}=G
, it follows from the construction of
G^{*}
that
G^{*}/G
is generated by
j``G/G
. Since
j``G\subseteq H^{*}
we must have
H^{*}=G^{*}
.
∎

Proof.

(Lemma
2.15
)

For every
a\in M[G^{*}]
there is a function
f:\kappa\to V^{\mathbb{P}}
in
V
and
\nu<\kappa^{++}
such that
a=(j(f)(\nu))_{G^{*}}
. Therefore, to show that
a=j^{*}(g)(\kappa)
for some
g:\kappa\to V[G]
in
V[G]
, it suffices to verify that for every
\nu<\kappa^{++}
there is
h:\kappa\to\kappa
in
V[G]
so that
\nu=j^{*}(h)(\kappa)
. To this end, let
\varphi_{F}(v,x)
be the parameter-free formula from the uniformly definable requirement of the FM-blueprint. Since
H_{\kappa^{++}}
is contained in
M=M_{E}
then
\varphi_{F}(\kappa,x)
defines the same
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa+1}
sequence
\vec{f}^{\kappa}=\langle f^{\kappa}_{\nu}\mid\nu<\kappa^{++}\rangle\subseteq{}^{\kappa}\kappa
of pairwise distinct functions in both
M[G^{*}]
and
V[G]
.
Similarly, for each Mahlo cardinal
\alpha<\kappa
, let
\vec{f}^{\alpha}=\langle f^{\alpha}_{\tau}\mid\tau<\alpha^{++}\rangle\subseteq{}^{\alpha}\alpha
be the sequence of pairwise distinct functions definable in
V[G]
by
\varphi_{F}(\alpha,x)
.
Working in
V[G]
, define for each
\nu<\kappa^{++}
the function
h_{\nu}\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
. For each Mahlo cardinal
\alpha<\kappa
, if
f^{\kappa}_{\nu}\upharpoonright\alpha=f^{\alpha}_{\tau}
for some
\tau<\alpha^{++}
then
h_{\nu}(\alpha)=\tau
. Otherwise,
h_{\nu}(\alpha)=0
.
For
\nu<\kappa^{++}
, as
j^{*}(f^{\kappa}_{\nu})\upharpoonright\kappa=f^{\kappa}_{\nu}
,
j^{*}(h_{\nu})(\kappa)=\nu
.
∎

Proof.

(Lemma
2.16
)

Let
U^{*}
be a measure in
V[G]
on a measurable cardinal
\kappa^{*}
(not necessarily normal). Denote its ultrapower embedding by
j_{U^{*}}:V[G]\to M_{U^{*}}
.
Having
V=L[\bar{E}]
be a fine-structural extender model we have
M_{U^{*}}=L[j_{U^{*}}(\bar{E})][j_{U^{*}}(G)]
. I.e., it is a generic extension of
its core model
L[j_{U^{*}}(\bar{E})]
by a generic filter
j_{U^{*}}(G)\subseteq j_{U^{*}}(\mathbb{P})
. Denote
L[j_{U^{*}}(\bar{E})]
by
M^{*}
and
j_{U^{*}}(G)
by
H^{*}
.
Moreover, by a Theorem of Schindler (
[
24
]
) the restriction
j^{*}:=j_{U^{*}}\upharpoonright V:V\to M^{*}
is an iterated ultrapower embedding of
V
which is uniquely determined by applying comparison of
V
and
M^{*}
.
Having,
M_{U^{*}}^{\omega}\subseteq M_{U^{*}}
and
i(\mathbb{P})
being
\omega_{1}
-distributive means
(M^{*})^{\omega}\subseteq M^{*}
. Hence, the iterated ultrapower of
V
that generates
M^{*}
must have finite length
\ell<\omega
, namely

j^{*}=j^{*}_{\ell-1,\ell}\circ j^{*}_{\ell-2,\ell-1}\circ\dots\circ j^{*}_{0,1}

where
\ell<\omega
and for each
i<\ell
,

j^{*}_{i,i+1}=j^{M^{*}_{i}}_{E^{*}_{i}}:M^{*}_{i}\to M^{*}_{i+1}\cong\Ult(M^{*}_{i},E^{*}_{i})

is an ultrapower embedding by an extender
E^{*}_{i}\in M^{*}_{i}
(
M^{*}_{0}=V
) is an extender in
M^{*}_{i}
with critical point
cp(E^{*}_{i})=\kappa^{*}_{i}
and height
ht(E^{*}_{i})\leq((\kappa^{*}_{i})^{++})^{M^{*}_{i}}
,

\kappa=\kappa^{*}_{0}<\kappa^{*}_{1}<\dots<\kappa^{*}_{\ell-1}
.
∎

Proof.

(Lemma
2.17
)

The statement of the lemma follows from the next two claims.

Claim 2.18
.

E^{*}_{0}=E
. In particular
\kappa^{*}=\kappa_{0}=\kappa
is the only measurable cardinal in
V[G]
.

Proof.

(Claim
2.18
)

The minimality assumption of
V=L[\bar{E}]
being a minimal model for the property of
\kappa
carrying a
(\kappa,\kappa^{++})
-extender implies
\kappa
is the maximal measurable cardinal in
V
. Hence
\kappa_{0}\leq\kappa
. Clearly
\kappa_{0}
is a nontrivial iteration stage of
\mathbb{P}
and so
2^{\kappa_{0}}=\kappa_{0}^{++}
in
V[G]
.

Suppose that
E^{*}_{0}\neq E
. Then
ht(E^{*}_{0})<\kappa_{0}^{++}
which implies
(\kappa_{0}^{++})^{M_{1}}<j_{E^{*}_{0}}(\kappa_{0})<\kappa_{0}^{++}
.
Since
(\kappa_{0}^{++})^{M_{1}}=(\kappa_{0}^{++})^{N}
, and
M_{U^{*}}=N[H^{*}]\models 2^{\kappa_{0}}=(\kappa_{0})^{++}
, there is a bijection
f\in N[H^{*}]
,

f:(\kappa_{0}^{++})^{N}\leftrightarrow\mathcal{P}(\kappa_{0})^{M_{U^{*}}}
. But
\mathcal{P}(\kappa_{0})^{M_{U^{*}}}=\mathcal{P}(\kappa_{0})^{V[G]}
, and so the last contradicts the fact
|\mathcal{P}(\kappa_{0})|^{V[G]}=\kappa_{0}^{++}
.
This conclude the proof of Claim
2.18
.
∎

Claim 2.19
.

If
U^{*}
is normal then
\ell=1
.

Proof.

(Claim
2.19
)

Suppose otherwise. Let

j_{1,\ell}=j_{\ell-1,\ell}\circ j_{\ell-2,\ell-2}\circ\dots\circ j_{2,1}:M_{1}\to M_{\ell}.

Then
j_{1,\ell}
is not trivial and
cp(j_{1,\ell})=\kappa_{1}>\kappa_{0}^{++}=\kappa^{++}
.
Since
U^{*}
is normal there is
f:\kappa\to\kappa
in
V[G]=V[G_{\kappa}*G(\kappa)]
such that
\kappa_{1}=j_{U^{*}}(f)(\kappa)
.
By Lemma
2.13
there is a function
F\in V
with
\dom(F)=\kappa
and
|F(\alpha)|\leq\alpha^{++}
for all inaccessibles
\alpha<\kappa
, a
\mathbb{P}
-name of a club
\dot{C}^{*}
, and a condition
p^{*}\in G
such that

p^{*}\Vdash\forall\alpha\in\dot{C}^{*}.\ \dot{f}(\check{\alpha})\in\check{F}(\check{\alpha})
.
Applying
j_{U}^{*}
we see that

j_{U^{*}}(p^{*})\Vdash\left(\check{\kappa}\in j_{U^{*}}(\dot{C}^{*})\right)\implies\left(\kappa_{1}\in j_{U^{*}}(F)(\kappa)\right).

Having
j_{U^{*}}(p^{*})\in H^{*}
, and
\kappa\in j_{U^{*}}(C^{*})
as
C^{*}=\dot{C}^{*}_{G}
is a club and
\cp(j_{U^{*}})=\kappa
, we conclude that

\kappa_{1}\in j_{U^{*}}(F)(\kappa)=j_{1,\ell}(j_{0,1}(F))\left(j_{1,\ell}(\kappa)\right)=j_{1,\ell}(j_{0,1}(F)(\kappa))=j_{1,\ell}``[j_{0,1}(F)(\kappa)].

This is absurd as
\kappa_{1}
is the critical point of
j_{1,\ell}
and cannot belong to a pointwise image of
j_{1,\ell}
.
This conclude the proofs of Claim
2.19
and Lemma
2.17
, in turn.
∎

∎

2.2
The Extended “Kunen-Like” Blueprint

Definition 2.20
.

Let
\kappa
be an uncountable regular cardinal. Let
<^{\kappa}_{Sing}
be the order relation on
{}^{\kappa}\kappa
defined by

f<^{\kappa}_{Sing}g
if and only if there is a club
C\subseteq\kappa
such that
f(\alpha)<g(\alpha)
for every singular ordinal
\alpha\in C
.

Let us list some basic properties of
<^{\kappa}_{Sing}
.

Lemma 2.21
.

1.

For every uncountable regular cardinal
\kappa
,
<^{\kappa}_{Sing}
is a well-founded pre-order on
{}^{\kappa}\kappa
.

2.

Let
M_{1},M_{2}
be transitive classes of
ZFC
such that

•

\kappa
is a regular uncountable cardinal in both,

•

M_{1},M_{2}
agree on cofinalities of cardinal
\alpha<\kappa
,

•

\mathcal{P}(\kappa)^{M_{1}}\subseteq\mathcal{P}(\kappa)^{M_{2}}
.

Suppose that in
M_{1}
, there is an increasing sequence in
<^{\kappa}_{Sing}
of length
\rho_{1}\in On
, then
\rho_{1}\leq\left(rank(<^{\kappa}_{Sing})\right)^{M_{2}}.

Proof.

1.

This is an immediate consequence of the fact that the club filter on a regular uncountable cardinal
\kappa
is
\sigma
-complete.

2.

\mathcal{P}(\kappa)^{M_{1}}\subseteq\mathcal{P}(\kappa)^{M_{2}}
implies that every club
C\subseteq\kappa
in
M_{1}
belongs to
M_{2}
. Since
M_{1},M_{2}
agree on cofinalities of cardinal
\alpha<\kappa
,
(C\cap Sing)^{M_{1}}=(C\cap Sing)^{M_{2}}
. It is therefore clear that for every
f,g\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa\cap M_{1}
,
M_{1}\models f<^{\kappa}_{Sing}g
implies
M_{2}\models f<^{\kappa}_{Sing}g
as well.
It follows by a straightforward induction on ordinals
\rho
, that for every
f\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa\cap M_{1}
with
\rho=rank_{<^{\kappa}_{Sing}}(f)^{M_{1}}
that
rank_{<^{\kappa}_{Sing}}(f)^{M_{2}}\geq\rho
.
As the assumption clearly implies
rank^{M_{1}}(<^{\kappa}_{Sing})\geq\rho_{1}
, we conclude that
rank^{M_{2}}(<^{\kappa}_{Sing})\geq\rho_{1}
.

∎

Definition 2.22

(“Kunen-like” blueprint)
.

Let
\kappa
be a measurable cardinal and
\mathbb{P}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle
an iteration of length
\kappa+1
. The “Kunen-like” blueprint includes the assumptions of the Friedman-Magidor blueprint
2.12
, with the following two additional assumptions:

KL1.

For every Mahlo cardinal
\alpha\leq\kappa
,
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
adds an increasing sequence in
<^{\alpha}_{Sing}
of length
\alpha^{++}
,

KL2.

\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is self coding, by sequences
\vec{q}^{\alpha}
and
\vec{S}^{\alpha}
, where
\vec{S}^{\alpha}=\langle S^{\alpha}_{\tau}\mid\tau<\alpha^{++}\rangle
consists of almost disjoint stationary subsets of
\alpha^{+}\cap Cof(\rho_{\alpha})
, for some regular cardinal
\rho_{\alpha}<\alpha
.

Theorem 2.2
.

Suppose that
V=L[\bar{E}]
is a fine-structural extender model which is minimal for the existence of a measurable cardinal
\kappa
carrying a
(\kappa,\kappa^{++})
-extender
E
.
Let
\mathbb{P}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle
be a iterated forcing poset which satisfies the “Kunen-like” blueprint. Then in a generic extension
V[G]
by
G\subseteq\mathbb{P}
,
2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{++}
,
\kappa
is the only measurable cardinal, there is a unique normal measure
U
on
\kappa
, and every
\sigma
-complete ultrafilter
U^{*}
is Rudin-Kiesler isomorphic to a finite power
U^{\ell}
of
U
.

Before turning to the proof of Theorem
2.2
let us describe the elementary embedding and generic filter associated to
U^{\ell}
for
1\leq\ell<\omega
.

Remark 2.23
.

We give a precise construction of finite powers
U^{\ell}
,
\ell<\omega
of the normal measure
U\in V[G]
on
\kappa
from the previous section. For this, we define by induction on
n
, ultrapower inner models
N_{n}
, elementary embeddings
i_{0,n}:N_{0}\to N_{n}
.

Set
N_{0}=V[G]
and

i_{0,1}=j^{N_{0}}_{U}:N_{0}\to N_{1}\cong\Ult(N_{0},U).

Denote the critical point
\kappa
by
\kappa_{0}
.
For
n\geq 1
, given
i_{0,n}:N_{0}\to N_{n}
define

i_{n,n+1}=j^{M_{n}}_{i_{0,n}(U)}:N_{n}\to N_{n+1}\cong\Ult(N_{n},i_{0,n}(U))

to be the ultrapower embedding, and set
\kappa_{n}:=i_{0,n}(\kappa)
and

i_{0,n+1}=j^{N_{n}}_{i_{0,n}(U)}\circ i_{0,n}
.

For
\ell\geq 1
we can describe
U^{\ell}
by

U^{\ell}=\{X\subseteq\kappa^{\ell}\mid\left(\thinspace\kappa_{0},\dots,\kappa_{\ell-1}\thinspace\right)\in i_{0,\ell}(X)\}.

Clearly, the ultrapower of
V[G]
by
U^{\ell}
is
M_{\ell}
, and the ultrapower embedding is
i_{0,\ell}
.

For
n<\omega
, the construction of the map
i_{n,n+1}:N_{n}\to N_{n+1}
as an (internal) ultrapower map of
N_{n}
by
i_{0,n}(U)
.
This means that the description of this ultrapower as an extension a ground embedding in a generic extension by
i_{0,n}(\mathbb{P})
.
More specifically, we have

•

\kappa_{n}
is the only measurable cardinal in
N_{n}
, and its unique normal measure is
i_{0,n}(U)
.

•

N_{n}=M_{n}[G^{(n)}]
is the generic extension of
M_{n}:=L[i_{0,n}(\bar{E})]
, is the ultrapower by
G^{(n)}=i_{0,n}(G)\subseteq i_{0,n}(\mathbb{P})
.

•

The restriction of the ultrapower map
j^{N_{n}}_{i_{0,n}(U)}
to the ground
M_{n}
is the ultrapower embedding of
M_{n}
by its unique
(\kappa_{n},\kappa_{n}^{++})
-extender
E_{n}:=i_{0,n}(E)
.

•

For each finite
\ell\geq 1
, the restriction of
i_{0,\ell}=j^{V[G]}_{U^{\ell}}
to
V
is the
\ell
-th iterated ultrapower of
V
by
E
(and its images).
Namely, given by the iteration sequence
\langle M_{n},j_{n,m}\mid n\leq m\leq\ell\rangle
, where

M_{0}=V,\text{ and }j_{n,n+1}=j^{M_{n}}_{j_{0,n}(E)}:M_{n}\to M_{n+1}\cong\Ult(M_{n},j_{0,n}(E))\text{ for every }n<\ell.

In particular, for each
n<\omega
,
E_{n}=i_{0,n}(E)=j_{0,n}(E)
.

•

G^{(0)}=G
and for each
n<\omega
,

G^{(n+1)}\subseteq i_{0,n+1}(\mathbb{P})
is generated by the set
G^{(n)}\cup j^{M_{n}}_{E_{n}}``[G^{(n)}]
.
Moreover,
j_{0,n+1}(\mathbb{P}_{\kappa})=\mathbb{P}^{M_{n+1}}_{\kappa_{n+1}}
and

G^{(n+1)}\upharpoonright j_{0,n+1}(\mathbb{P}_{\kappa})

is generated from

G^{(n)}\cup j^{M_{n}}_{E_{n}}``[G^{(n)}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}^{M_{n}}_{\kappa_{n}}]
(see the first part of the proof of Lemma
2.14
), and

G^{(n+1)}\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}^{M_{n+1}}_{\kappa_{n+1}}
is generated from
j^{1}_{E_{n}}``[G^{(n)}\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}^{M_{n}}_{\kappa_{n}}]
, where
j^{1}_{E_{n}}
is the extension of
j^{M_{n}}_{E_{n}}
to
M_{n}[G^{(n+1)}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}^{M_{n+1}}_{\kappa_{n+1}}]
(Second part of the proof of Lemma
2.14
).

In particular we see that for each
n<\ell

G^{(n)}=G^{(\ell)}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}^{M_{\ell}}_{\kappa_{n}+1}\text{ and }

i_{0,n}``[G\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}]\text{ generates the generic }G^{(n)}\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}^{M_{n}}_{\kappa_{n}}\text{ over }M_{n}[G^{(n)}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}^{M_{n}}_{\kappa_{n}}].

2.3
Proof of Theorem
2.2

In this subsection we prove Theorem
2.2
.
Let
U^{*}
be a
\sigma
-complete ultrafilter in
V[G]
. Since by Theorem
2.1
,
\kappa
is the only measurable cardinal in
V[G]
, then
U^{*}
is
\kappa
-complete. We first appeal to the analysis and notations from the proof of Lemma
2.15
, whose first part (up to and including Claim
2.18
) does not assume normality for
U^{*}
.
Let
j_{U^{*}}:V[G]\to M_{U^{*}}
be the ultrapower embedding. Summarizing the relevant findings in Lemma
2.15
we know
U^{*}
and
j_{U^{*}}
have the following properties:

•

j_{U^{*}}\upharpoonright V=j^{*}_{0,\ell}:V\to N=M^{*}_{\ell}
is a an iterated ultrapower of
V
of some finite length
\omega>\ell\geq 1
by extenders
E^{*}_{i}\in M_{i}
whose critical points are
\kappa^{*}_{i}=cp(E^{*}_{i})
, for
i<\ell
.

•

M^{*}_{0}=V
and
E^{*}_{0}=E
by Claim
2.18
. In particular
\kappa_{0}=\kappa
.

•

for each
i<\ell
,

j^{*}_{i,i+1}:M^{*}_{i}\to M^{*}_{i+1}\cong\Ult(M_{i},E^{*}_{i})
by an extender
E^{*}_{i}\in M^{*}_{i}
whose critical point is
\kappa^{*}_{i}=cp(E^{*}_{i})>((\kappa^{*}_{i-1})^{++})^{M^{*}_{i}}
.

•

M_{U^{*}}=M^{*}_{\ell}[H^{*}]
where
H^{*}\subseteq j^{*}_{0,\ell}(\mathbb{P})
is generic over
M^{*}_{\ell}
.

Definition 2.24
.

We refer to
\ell
as the
ground iteration length of

U^{*}
and further denote it by
\ell(U^{*})
.

\ell(U^{*})
clearly depends on
U^{*}
. For example if
U^{*}=U^{k}
is a finite power of the normal measure
U
then
\ell(U^{*})=k
.

We establish the statement of Theorem
2.2
by showing that
\ell(U^{*})
identifies
U^{*}
.

Proposition 2.25
.

For every
\kappa
-complete ultrafilter
U^{*}
in
V[G]
,
U^{*}\cong_{RK}U^{\ell}
where
\ell=\ell(U^{*})
is the ground iteration length of
U^{*}
.

We prove Proposition

2.25
by
induction on
\ell=\ell(U^{*})
.

The proof will be based on an analysis of
U^{*}
and its ultrapower, and a comparison with the description of
U^{\ell}
and its ultrapower parameters, as given in Remark
2.23
.
The analysis of
U^{*}
and its ultrapower will make extensive use of the following measures
U^{*}_{i}

1\leq i\leq\ell
, derived from
j_{U^{*}}
.

Definition 2.26
.

For
1\leq i\leq\ell
, let
U^{*}_{i}
be a
\kappa
-complete ultrafilter on
\kappa^{i}
defined by

U^{*}_{i}=\{A\subseteq\kappa^{i}\mid(\kappa^{*}_{0},\dots,\kappa^{*}_{i-1})\in j_{U^{*}}(A)\}

We denote the ultrapower of
V[G]
with
U^{*}_{i}
by
j_{U^{*}_{i}}:V[G]\to M_{U^{*}_{i}}
, and the induced embedding of
M_{U^{*}_{i}}
in
M_{U^{*}}
by

k_{U^{*}_{i},U^{*}}:M_{U^{*}_{i}}\to M_{U^{*}}
, which is given by

k_{U^{*}_{i},U^{*}}([f]_{U^{*}_{i}})=j_{U^{*}}(f)(\kappa^{*}_{0},\dots\kappa^{*}_{i-1}).

Therefore
U^{*}_{i}\leq_{RK}U^{*}
. For example,
U^{*}_{1}
is the normal projection of
U^{*}
, which by Theorem
2.1
must be the unique normal measure
U
on
\kappa
in
V[G]
.

We proceed to proof the inductive step of the statement of Proposition
2.25
.
Fix a
\kappa
-complete ultrafilter
U^{*}
in
V[G]
with
\ell(U^{*})=\ell
, and suppose that the statement holds for all ultrafilters
\bar{U}
with
\ell(\bar{U})<\ell
.

Let
E^{*}_{i},j^{*}_{i,i+1}:M^{*}_{i}\to M^{*}_{i+1}\cong\Ult(M^{*}_{i},E^{*}_{i})
, be the normal iteration sequence that generate
j_{U^{*}}\upharpoonright V:V\to M^{*}_{\ell}
.

The following lemma gathers the main properties of
j_{U^{*}}
, given in terms of
E^{*}_{i},M^{*}_{i}
,
U^{*}_{i}
and
k_{U^{*}_{i},U^{*}}
. These will be key in proving that
U^{*}\cong_{RK}U^{\ell}
.

Lemma 2.27
.

The following holds for each
0\leq i<\ell

1.

E^{*}_{i}=E_{i}
,

Notice that having
E^{*}_{j}=E_{j}
for all
j\leq i
this implies that
M^{*}_{i+1}=M_{i+1}
,
j^{*}_{0,i+1}=j_{0,i+1}
, and
\kappa^{*}_{j}=\kappa_{j}
for
0\leq j\leq i+1
.
Also,
\mathbb{P}^{M^{*}_{\ell}}_{\kappa^{*}_{i+1}+1}=\mathbb{P}^{M}_{\kappa^{*}_{i+1}+1}=j_{0,i}(\mathbb{P})
.

2.

H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}^{M^{*}_{\ell}}_{\kappa^{*}_{i}+1}=G^{(i)}

3.

U^{*}_{i+1}=U^{i+1}
for
i<\ell-1

4.

cp(k_{U^{*}_{i+1},U^{*}})\geq\kappa^{*}_{i+1}
for
i<\ell-1
.

The proof of the Lemma will make use of the following notation.

Notation 2.28
.

For
i\leq\ell
,
let
\langle f^{\kappa^{*}_{i},H^{*}}_{\tau}\mid\tau<((\kappa^{*}_{i})^{++})^{M^{*}_{\ell}}\rangle
be the
H^{*}
-induced generic sequence of functions at stage
\kappa^{*}_{i}
of the iteration (i.e., produced by the
H^{*}
generic for
\mathbb{Q}^{M^{*}_{\ell}}_{\kappa^{*}_{i}}
), which is definable from the uniform definition
j_{U^{*}}(\varphi_{F})(\kappa^{*}_{i},x)
(specified by blueprint assumptions).

Proof.

(Lemma
2.27
)

We proof the statements in the lemma by induction on
i<\ell
.

Starting at
i=0
, the FM-blueprint argument shows that
E^{*}_{0}=E_{0}
(Claim
2.18
).
Also, the proof of Lemma
2.14
,
shows that
H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}_{\kappa^{*}_{0}+1}=G=G^{(0)}
.
This also implies that
H^{*}
contains both
j^{*}_{0,\ell}``G
and
G=G^{(0)}
.
Also, since
U^{*}_{1}
is a normal measure, the FM-blueprint argument (
2.19
) implies
U^{*}_{1}=U=U^{1}
.

Next, by Lemma
2.15
, we have that for every
\gamma<\kappa^{++}=((\kappa^{*}_{0})^{++})^{M^{*}_{\ell}}
there is a function
h\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
in
V[G]
such that
\gamma=j_{U^{*}_{1}}(h)(\kappa^{*}_{0})
.
This, together with the proof of Lemma
2.14
, which shows that

G^{(1)}
is generated by
G\vee j^{*}_{0,1}``G
, implies
that for every
\mathbb{P}
-name
\dot{h}
with
h=\dot{h}_{G}
, there is some
p\in G
such that

p\vee j^{*}_{0,1}(p)\Vdash_{j^{*}_{0,1}(\mathbb{P})}j^{*}_{0,1}(\dot{h})(\check{\kappa^{*}_{0}})=\check{\gamma}.

Applying
j^{*}_{1,\ell}:M^{*}_{1}\to M^{*}_{\ell}
, and recalling that its critical point is
\kappa^{*}_{1}>((\kappa^{*}_{0})^{++})^{M^{*}_{1}}>\gamma
, we get that

p\vee j^{*}_{0,\ell}(p)\Vdash_{j^{*}_{0,\ell}(\mathbb{P})}j^{*}_{0,\ell}(\dot{h})(\check{\kappa^{*}_{0}})=\check{\gamma}.

As
H^{*}
contains
G\cup j^{*}_{0,\ell}``G
, we conclude that in
M_{U^{*}}=M^{*}_{\ell}[H^{*}]
,
j_{U^{*}}(h)(\kappa^{*}_{0})=\gamma
.
It follows that
((\kappa^{*}_{0})^{++})^{M^{*}_{1}}\subseteq im(k_{U^{*}_{1},U^{*}})
.
Next, by standard iterated ultrapower arguments and the normality of the iteration leading to
M^{*}_{\ell}
, we have that every
\gamma<\kappa^{*}_{1}
is of the form
j^{*}_{0,\ell}(g)(\gamma_{0})
for some
\gamma_{0}<((\kappa^{*}_{0})^{++})^{M^{*}_{1}}
and
g\in{}^{\kappa}\kappa
in
V
. Combining the findings, we conclude that
\kappa^{*}_{1}\subseteq im(k_{U^{*}_{1},U^{*}})
, hence

\cp(k_{U^{*}_{1},U^{*}})\geq\kappa^{*}_{1}
.

This conclude the verification of the inductive assumptions for
i=0
.

Suppose now that
1\leq i<\ell
is such that the inductive assumptions hold for all
0\leq j\leq i-1
.
In particular, we know that

U^{*}_{i}=U^{i}
and
\cp(k_{U^{*}_{i},U^{*}})\geq\kappa^{*}_{i}
. We proceed to verify the inductive statements for
i
.

Claim 2.29
.

E^{*}_{i}=E_{i}
.

Proof.

Suppose otherwise.
Since
E_{i}
is the unique
(\lambda,\lambda^{++})
- extender in
M^{*}_{i}
(for any
\lambda)
then
E^{*}_{i}\neq E_{i}
implies

(2^{\kappa^{*}_{i}})^{M_{U^{*}}}=((\kappa^{*}_{i})^{++})^{M^{*}_{\ell}}<j^{*}_{i,i+1}(\kappa^{*}_{i})<((\kappa^{*}_{i})^{++})^{M^{*}_{i}}.

As
rank^{M_{U^{*}}}(<^{\kappa^{*}_{i}}_{Sing})<((2^{\kappa^{*}_{i}})^{+})^{M_{U^{*}}}=((\kappa^{*}_{i})^{+3})^{M^{*}_{\ell}}<j^{*}_{i,i+1}(\kappa^{*}_{i})
, we get that
rank^{M_{U^{*}}}(<^{\kappa^{*}_{i}}_{Sing})<((\kappa^{*}_{i})^{++})^{M^{*}_{i}}
.

Let us see that this contradicts our inductive assumption for
i
. By the inductive assumptions,

•

cp(k_{U^{*}_{i},U^{*}})\geq\kappa^{*}_{i}
, which implies
\mathcal{P}(\kappa^{*}_{i})^{M_{U^{*}_{i}}}\subseteq\mathcal{P}(\kappa^{*}_{i})^{M_{U^{*}}}
, and

•

((\kappa^{*}_{i})^{++})^{M_{U^{*}_{i}}}=((\kappa^{*}_{i})^{++})^{M_{U^{i}}}=((\kappa^{*}_{i})^{++})^{M_{i}}=((\kappa^{*}_{i})^{++})^{M^{*}_{i}}
.

Since
M_{U^{*}_{i}}
contains a (generic)
<^{\kappa^{*}_{i}}_{Sing}
-increasing sequence of functions in
{}^{\kappa^{*}_{i}}\kappa^{*}_{i}
, it follows from the last points and Lemma
2.21
that

rank^{M_{U^{*}}}(<^{\kappa^{*}_{i}}_{Sing})\geq((\kappa^{*}_{i})^{++})^{M^{*}_{i}}
, which contradicts the first finding above.
∎

Next, we need to show that
G^{(i)}=H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}_{\kappa^{*}_{i}+1}
.
To see this, note that

k_{U^{*}_{i},U^{*}}``G^{(i)}\subseteq H^{*}
and in particular
G^{(i)}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}_{\kappa^{*}_{i}}\subseteq H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}_{\kappa^{*}_{i}}
. As the first set is already generic over
M^{*}_{\ell}
, it must be that
G^{(i)}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}_{\kappa^{*}_{i}}=H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}_{\kappa^{*}_{i}}
.

It remains to show that
H^{*}
and
G^{(i)}
agree on the generic filter at stage
\kappa^{*}_{i}=\kappa_{i}
of the iteration.

Claim 2.30
.

H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa^{*}_{i}}=G^{(i)}\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa^{*}_{i}}
.

Proof.

As pointed out at the end of Remark
2.23
,

j_{0,i}``[G\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}]
generates the generic filter
G^{(i)}\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}^{M_{i}}_{\kappa_{i}}
over
M_{i}[G^{(i)}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}^{M_{i}}_{\kappa_{i}}]=M^{*}_{i}[H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}^{M_{i}}_{\kappa_{i}}]
. As
E_{i}=E^{*}_{i}
,
M^{*}_{i}
and
M^{*}_{\ell}
agree on
H_{(\kappa^{*}_{i})^{++}}
. Thus
j_{0,i}``[G\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}]=j^{*}_{0,i}``[G\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}]
also generates a generic for
\mathbb{Q}^{M^{*}_{i}}_{\kappa_{i}}
over
M^{*}_{\ell}[H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{P}^{M^{*}_{\ell}}_{\kappa_{i}}]
.
It is therefore sufficient to prove that

j_{0,i}``[G\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}]\subseteq H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}^{M_{i}}_{\kappa_{i}}
.

j_{0,i}``[G\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}]\subseteq H^{*}\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}^{M_{i}}_{\kappa_{i}}
.

Following the notations of the Extended Blueprint
2.22
, let
\vec{S}^{\kappa}=\langle S^{\kappa}_{\tau}\mid\tau<\kappa^{++}\rangle
and
\vec{q}^{\kappa}=\langle q^{\kappa}_{\tau}\mid\tau<\kappa^{++}\rangle
be the sequence of stationary sets and enumeration of
\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
, which are used by
\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
to self code its generic filter (As described in Definition
2.4
).
Similarly, let
\vec{S}^{\kappa_{i}}=j^{*}_{0,i}(\vec{S}^{\kappa})
and
\vec{q}^{\kappa_{i}}=j^{*}_{0,i}(\vec{q}^{\kappa})
. be the sequences used for the self coding of
\mathbb{Q}^{M^{*}_{\ell}}_{\kappa_{i}}
in
M^{*}_{\ell}
.
By the blueprint assumptions for each
\tau<\kappa^{++}
, the stationary set
S^{\kappa}_{\tau}\subseteq\kappa^{+}
consists of ordinals of cofinality
\rho_{\kappa}<\kappa
. Since
j^{*}_{0,i}(\rho_{\kappa})=\rho_{\kappa}
, the same is true for
j^{*}_{0,i}(S^{\kappa}_{\tau})=S^{\kappa_{i}}_{j^{*}_{0,i}(\tau)}
.
Moreover, as the set
j^{*}_{0,i}``\kappa^{+}
is unbounded in
(\kappa_{i}^{+})^{M^{*}_{\ell}}
and closed to limits of its
<\kappa
-increasing sequences, it follows that

V[G]\models S^{\kappa}_{\tau}\text{ is stationary}\iff V[G]\models S^{\kappa_{i}}_{j^{*}_{0,i}(\tau)}\text{ is stationary}\iff M^{*}_{\ell}[H^{*}]\models S^{\kappa_{i}}_{j^{*}_{0,i}(\tau)}\text{ is stationary}

For the direction
(\Longleftarrow)
of the last equivalence, we use the fact that the stationary sets are coded in pairs, where each
S^{\kappa_{i}}_{\nu}
is stationary in
M^{*}_{\ell}[H^{*}]
if and only if
S^{\kappa_{i}}_{\nu^{\prime}}
is not stationary there, for
\nu^{\prime}
which is either
\nu+1
if
\nu
is an even ordinal, or
\nu-1
if
\nu
is odd. Thus, the trivial direction
(\Longrightarrow)
suffices to imply the equivalence.

By the self coding properties of
\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
and elementary properties of
j^{*}_{0,i}
, it follows that for every
\tau<\kappa^{++}
,
q^{\kappa}_{\tau}\in G\upharpoonright\mathbb{Q}_{\kappa}
if and only if
j^{*}_{0,i}(q^{\kappa}_{\tau})\in H^{*}
. The claim follows.
∎

Moving on, we would like to show that

Claim 2.31
.

U^{*}_{i+1}=U^{i+1}
if
i+1<\ell
.

Proof.

Let
