Let us proceed to the case where
\kappa
is singular. Recall that in this case we have
\zeta>\cf(\kappa)
. Fix an increasing, cofinal sequence
\langle\alpha_{j}\colon j<\cf(\kappa)\rangle
in
\kappa
. Make sure that
\alpha_{0}>\alpha^{\prime}
.

Let
\langle\epsilon_{i}\colon i<\zeta\rangle
be the increasing enumeration of very good points of
N_{\eta}
above
\epsilon^{\prime}
, such that for every
i<\zeta
, each
\epsilon_{i}
is a limit of very good points, and
\cf(\epsilon_{i})>\kappa
.
8
8

8

Note that the enumeration of all such very good points is external to
N_{\eta}
.
For every
i<\zeta
, let
j(i)<\cf(\kappa)
be the least index such that:

•

The witness for the fact that
\epsilon_{i}
is a very good point is below
\alpha_{j(i)}
.

•

h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times\left(\alpha_{j(i)}\cup\{\delta\}\right)]
is unbounded in
\epsilon_{i}
(since
\cf(\epsilon_{i})>\kappa
, there exists
j(i)
fulfilling this clause).

•

\langle\epsilon_{j}\colon j\leq i\rangle\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i+1}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha_{j(i+1)}\cup\{\delta\})]
(note that, since
\epsilon_{i+1}
is a limit of very good points, the coherence of the square sequence ensures that
\langle\epsilon_{j}\colon j\leq i\rangle\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i+1}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\kappa\cup\{\delta\})]
. Thus, there exists some
j(i)
fulfilling this clause).

Since
\zeta
is regular above
\cf(\kappa)
, there exists
j^{*}<\cf(\kappa)
such that for unboundedly many
i<\zeta
,
j(i)=j^{*}
. Pick any
\alpha^{*}<\kappa
above
\alpha_{j^{*}}
. Finally, any subsequence of
\langle\epsilon_{i}\colon i<\zeta,j(i)=j^{*}\rangle
of length
\gamma
will be as desired; the least such sequence of length
\gamma
belongs to
N_{\eta}
since
\cf(\eta)=\zeta
.
∎

4
Realizing the “Kunen-Like” Blueprint

In this section, we construct a forcing notion
\mathbb{P}=\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}\colon\alpha\leq\kappa\rangle
which satisfies the “Kunen-like” blueprint
2.22
, over the minimal extender model
V=L[\bar{E}]
. The section is divided into three subsections. In subsection
4.1
we present a forcing that adds a generalized Miller subset to a Mahlo cardinal
\alpha
. Such a forcing was developed by Friedman and Zdomskyy in
[
12
]
, along with an iteration theory, that ensures that the resulting posets satisfy the
C
-Fusion property. In subsection
4.2
we define the iterates
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
, for each Mahlo
\alpha\leq\kappa
. Each such
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is self coding iterate of
\alpha^{++}
-many generalized Miller subsets of
\alpha
.
In subsection
4
we prove that the iteration
\mathbb{P}
satisfies the remaining properties from the “Kunen-like” blueprint, mainly the Iteration-Fusion property and the distributivity of its tails. This subsection takes advantage of the special choice of the coding stationary sets constructed in Section
3
.

4.1
Generalized Miller Forcing

Let
\lambda
be a regular cardinal. We present in this subsection a forcing notion
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
, which is a variation of a generalized Miller forcing due to Friedman and Zdomskyy
[
12
]
. Denote by
[\lambda]^{<\lambda}
the set of increasing sequences of ordinals below
\lambda
of length below
\lambda
. A condition in
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
is a tree
p\subseteq[\lambda]^{<\lambda}
which satisfies–

1.

Closure: for every limit
\alpha<\lambda
and
s\in[\lambda]^{\alpha}
, if every initial segment of
s
belongs to
p
, then
s\in p
as well.

2.

Splitting: For every
s\in p
, the set–

C_{p}(s)=\{\alpha<\lambda\colon s^{\frown}\langle\alpha\rangle\in p\}

is either a singleton, or a co-bounded
9
9

9

In
[
12
]
, ”closed unbounded” replaces ”co-bounded”. For us the current version suffices.
subset of
\lambda
. In the latter case, we say that
s
is a
splitting node
of
p
.

3.

If
s\in p\cap\kappa^{\alpha}
for a regular cardinal
\alpha
then
C_{p}(s)=\{0\}
. In particular, splitting nodes of
p
must have singular height.

4.

Density of splitting nodes: every
s\in p
has an end-extension
t\in p
,
s\subseteq t
, which is a splitting node of
p
.

5.

Splitting at singular closure points: if
\alpha<\lambda
is singular,
s\in p\cap\alpha^{\alpha}
(i.e.,
\alpha
is a closure point of the function
s
) and unboundedly many initial segments of
s
are splitting nodes of
p
, then
s
is a splitting node of
p
as well.

Given a pair of conditions
p,q\in\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
, we say that
p
extends
q
if
p\subseteq q
. For
p\in\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
and
t\in p
, we denote by
(p)_{t}\in\mathbb{M_{\lambda}}
the sub-tree of
p
consisting of all the nodes which are compatible with
t
with respect to the end-extension order.

Claim 4.1
.

\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
is
\lambda
-closed.

Proof.

Let
\langle p_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\delta\rangle
be a
\leq
-increasing sequence in
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
of length
\delta<\lambda
. The natural candidate for an upper bound for the sequence is the tree
q=\bigcap_{\alpha<\delta}p_{\alpha}
. It is therefore suffices to prove
q\in\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
is a condition.

Suppose that
s\in q
. We first show
C_{q}(s)\neq\emptyset
and is either a singleton or a co-bounded subset of
\lambda
.
Since
\lambda
is regular and
\delta<\lambda
, it is clear that if
C_{p_{\alpha}}(s)
is co-bounded in
\kappa
, for all
\alpha<\delta
, then so does
C_{q}(s)
.
In the case that
C_{p_{\alpha}}(s)=\{x\}
for some
\alpha<\delta
, then for all
\beta<\delta
,
s\in p_{\alpha+\beta}
and
p_{\alpha}\subseteq p_{\alpha+\beta}
ensure that
C_{p_{\alpha+\beta}}(s)=\{x\}
. Therefore
C_{q}(s)=\{x\}
.

We see that in either case, every
s\in q
and a proper end extension
s^{\prime}\in q
. Since
q=\bigcap_{\alpha<\delta}p_{\alpha}
must be close to
<\lambda
-limits of its sequences, we conclude that every
s\in q
has end-extensions
s^{\prime}\in q
of arbitrarily long lengths
<\lambda
. The same argument regarding
q=\bigcap_{\alpha<\delta}p_{\alpha}
gives that the set of splitting nodes in
q
is closed under unions of singular heights.

Therefore, it remains to verify the density of the splitting nodes of
q
. Fix some
t\in q
. We argue that there exists a splitting node
s
of
q
with
s\supseteq t
.
To this end, we construct a strictly increasing sequence
\langle t_{i}\colon i<\delta\rangle
of nodes in
q
, such that, for every
\alpha<\delta
, there are unboundedly many indices
i<\delta
such that
t_{i+1}
is a splitting node of
p_{\alpha}
. We also make sure that
\mbox{lh}(t_{0})>\delta
. This would suffice, since then
t^{*}=\bigcup\{t_{i}\colon i<\delta\}
is a splitting node of
p_{\alpha}
for every
\alpha<\delta
, and thus, it is a splitting node of
q
(this uses the assumption that
\mbox{lh}(t_{0})>\delta
, and thus
\mbox{lh}(t^{*})
is singular below
\lambda
, so the closure of splitting nodes at singular heights could be applied).
Take any
t_{0}=t\in q
with length above
\delta
. In limit steps, assume that
i<\delta
is limit, and let
t_{i}=\bigcup\{t_{j}\colon j<i\}
. Since all the trees
\langle p_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\delta\rangle
are closed,
t_{i}\in q
.
Thus, it suffices to concentrate on successor steps. Assume that we fixed in advance a partition
\langle A_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\delta\rangle
of
\delta
to unbounded subsets of
\delta
. Assume that
t_{i}
has been constructed, and let us construct
t_{i+1}
. Let
\alpha<\delta
be the unique such that
i\in A_{\alpha}
. We argue that we can find
t_{i+1}\in q
such that
t_{i}\subsetneq t_{i+1}
and
t_{i+1}
is a splitting node of
p_{\alpha}
.

Indeed, let
t_{i+1}
be the least splitting node of
p_{\alpha}
above
t_{i}
(”least” in the sense that there are no splitting points
s\in p_{\alpha}
such that
t_{i}\subsetneq s\subsetneq t_{i+1}
). Let us prove that for every
\beta\in\delta\setminus\{\alpha\}
,
t_{i+1}\in p_{\beta}
. Fix such
\beta
. By directedness, there exists
\gamma<\delta
such that
p_{\gamma}\subseteq p_{\alpha}\cap p_{\beta}
. But
t_{i}\in p_{\gamma}
, and thus
t_{i}
can be extended to a node of length
\mbox{lh}(t_{i+1})
in
p_{\gamma}\subseteq p_{\alpha}\cap p_{\beta}
. However, by the choice of
t_{i+1}
, the only possible such node is
t_{i+1}
itself. Thus
t_{i+1}\in p_{\beta}
.
This concludes the inductive construction and the proof.
∎

Since
\lambda
-closed posets preserve the stationarity of all stationary subsets
S\subseteq\lambda
in
V
we conclude that
\lambda
remains Mahlo.

Corollary 4.2
.

Assume that
\lambda
is Mahlo. Then
\lambda
remains Mahlo in
V^{\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}}
.

A generic set
G\subseteq\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
over
V
induces a generic branch,

g=\bigcup\{s\in\left[\lambda\right]^{<\lambda}\colon\exists p\in G,(s\in p\land(p)_{s}=p)\}

Clearly,
g\colon\lambda\to\lambda
and
g(\alpha)=0
for every regular cardinal
\alpha<\lambda
.

Claim 4.3
.

For every
f\colon\lambda\to\lambda
in
V
, there exists a club
C\subseteq\lambda
such that for every singular
\alpha\in C
,
f(\alpha)<g(\alpha)
.

Proof.

Given
f\colon\lambda\to\lambda
, let
D\subseteq\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
be the dense subset of conditions
p\in G
such that for every splitting node
s\in p
,
\min\left(C_{p}(s)\right)>f(\mbox{lh}(s))
. Let
q\in G\cap D
. Every initial segment of
g
is a node of
q
. Unboundedly many such initial segments are splitting nodes of
q
, since, otherwise,
q
has a node that doesn’t extend into a splitting node. Since splitting nodes of
q
are closed under singular limits at closure points of
g
, there exists a club
C\in V[G]
in
\lambda
, such that for every singular
\alpha\in C
,
g\upharpoonright\alpha
is a splitting node of
q
, and thus
g(\alpha)>f(\alpha)
.
∎

We now proceed and prove that for a Mahlo cardinal
\lambda
,
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
has the
\lambda
C-Fusion property. For that we need the notion of a Fusion sequence of conditions in
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
.

Definition 4.4
.

1.

Assume that
p\in\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
. For every
\alpha<\lambda
, the set
\mbox{Split}_{\alpha}(p)
consists of nodes
s\in p
such that–

\mbox{otp}\left(\{t\subsetneq s\colon t\mbox{ is a splitting node of }p\}\right)=\alpha

and
s
is minimal with this property
10
10

10

Note that such
s
is a splitting node if and only if
\mbox{lh}(s)
is a singular cardinal.
.

2.

If
p,q
are conditions and
\nu<\lambda
, we say that
p\geq_{\nu}q
if
p\geq q
and
\mbox{Split}_{\nu}(p)=\mbox{Split}_{\nu}(q)
.

3.

Fix
\nu<\lambda
. A
\nu
-fusion sequence of conditions in
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
is an increasing sequence of conditions
\langle p_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\lambda\rangle
, such that:

(a)

For every
\alpha<\lambda
,
p_{\alpha+1}\geq_{\nu+\alpha}p_{\alpha}
.

(b)

For every limit
\delta<\lambda
,
p_{\delta}=\bigcap_{\alpha<\delta}p_{\alpha}
.

A fusion sequence of conditions is a sequence which is a
\nu
-fusion sequence for some
\nu<\lambda
.

Claim 4.5
.

Assume that
\nu<\lambda
and
\langle p_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\lambda\rangle
is a
\nu
-fusion sequence. Then
q=\bigcap_{\alpha<\lambda}p_{\alpha}
is a condition, and, for every
\alpha<\lambda
,
q\geq_{\nu+\alpha}p_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

The property
q\geq_{\nu+\alpha}p_{\alpha}
for
\alpha<\lambda
is a property of trees and makes sense without the assumption of
q
being a condition. This property is clearly holds for the tree
q=\bigcap_{\alpha<\lambda}p_{\alpha}
and we use to verify that
q
is a condition. We focus on verifying that the splitting nodes of
q
are dense in
q
. The verification of the other properties is similar or simpler and left to the reader.

Fix
s\in q
. We split the argument into two cases.

Assume first that

\mbox{otp}\left(\{t\subsetneq s\colon t\mbox{ is a splitting node of }p\}\right)<\nu.

Let
s^{*}
be a minimal splitting node of
p_{0}
above
s
. Then for some
\nu^{\prime}\leq\nu
,
s^{*}\in\mbox{Split}_{\nu^{\prime}}(p_{0})
. But
q\geq_{\nu}p_{0}
, since, for every
\alpha<\lambda
,
p_{\alpha}\geq_{\nu}p_{0}
. Thus,
\mbox{Split}_{\nu^{\prime}}(q)=\mbox{Split}_{\nu^{\prime}}(p_{0})
, and, in particular,
s^{*}\in q
.

Next, assume there is
\alpha<\lambda
such that

\nu+\alpha=\mbox{otp}\left(\{t\subsetneq s\colon t\mbox{ is a splitting node of }p\}\right).

Let
s^{*}\supseteq s
be a minimal splitting point of
p_{\alpha+1}
above
s
. Then for every
\eta\in C_{p_{\alpha+1}}(s^{*})
and
\beta\geq\alpha+1
,
{s^{*}}^{\frown}\langle\eta\rangle\in\mbox{Split}_{\nu+\alpha+1}({p_{\alpha+1}})=\mbox{Split}_{\nu+\alpha+1}(p_{\beta})
. Therefore,
C_{p_{\alpha+1}}(s^{*})=C_{q}(s^{*})
, and in particular,
s^{*}
is a splitting point of
q
.
∎

Lemma 4.6
.

Assume that
\lambda
is Mahlo. Then
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
satisfies the
\lambda
C-Fusion property.

Proof.

We will prove a slightly stronger version of the C-Fusion property that will be used in Section
4.2
11
11

11

E.g., see Lemma
4.8
when we deal with back-to-back iteration of Miller and coding posets. We shall show that for every
\nu<\lambda
, together with C-Fusion parameters of
p\in\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
,
A\subseteq H_{\lambda^{+2}}
, and a sequence of dense open sets
\vec{D}=\langle D_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\lambda\rangle
, there is a condition

q^{*}\geq_{\nu}p

and continuous and internally approachable chain
\vec{X}=\langle X_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\lambda\rangle
of elementary substructures
X_{\alpha}\prec(H_{\lambda^{+2}},\vec{D},A)
that satisfy the requirements of the
\lambda
C-Fusion property (i.e., requirements (a)-(c) in Definition
2.4
).

We start by taking our sequence
\vec{X}=\langle X_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\lambda
to satisfy–

•

p,\mathbb{M}_{\lambda},\vec{D}\in X_{0}
, and
\nu+1\subseteq X_{0}
.

•

For every
\alpha<\lambda
,
\alpha\subseteq X_{\alpha}
.

•

For every
\alpha<\lambda
,
X_{\alpha+1}
is closed under
\alpha
-sequences of its elements. In particular
\alpha+1\subseteq X_{\alpha+1}
.

We construct a
\nu
-fusion sequence
\langle p_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\lambda\rangle
of conditions in
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
, such that, for every
\alpha<\lambda
,
p_{\alpha},p_{\alpha+1}\in X_{\alpha+1}
and, if
t\in\mbox{Split}_{\nu+\alpha}(q_{\alpha+1})
, then for every
\eta\in C_{q_{\alpha+1}}(t)
,

\left(q_{\alpha+1}\right)_{t^{\frown}\langle\eta\rangle}\in D^{*}_{\alpha}:=D_{\alpha}\cap\left(\bigcap\{D\colon D\in X_{\alpha}\mbox{ is a dense open subset of }\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}\}\right).

D^{*}_{\alpha}
is dense open since
|X_{\alpha}|<\lambda
, by
\lambda
-closure of
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
. Also,
D^{*}_{\alpha}\in X_{\alpha+1}
by internal approachability and by the fact that
D_{\alpha}\in X_{\alpha+1}
.

We start the sequence with
p_{0}=p
, and for a limit ordinal
\alpha
, take
p_{\alpha}=\bigcap_{\beta<\alpha}p_{\beta}
. By the Claim
4.1
and the inductive assumption for
\langle p_{\beta}\mid\beta<\alpha\rangle
,
p_{\alpha}
is a condition. We have
p_{\alpha}\in X_{\alpha+1}
since
X_{\alpha+1}
is closed under
\alpha
sequences of its elements. Let us take care of the successor step. Assume that
p_{\alpha}\in X_{\alpha+1}
has been constructed, and construct
p_{\alpha+1}
inside
X_{\alpha+1}
as follows: for every
s\in\mbox{Split}_{\nu+\alpha}(p_{\alpha})
, and for every
\eta\in C_{p_{\alpha}}(s)
, shrink
p_{\alpha}
above
s^{\frown}\langle\eta\rangle
so it enters
D^{*}_{\alpha}
. Let the resulting condition be
p_{\alpha+1}
, Note that if
s
is a splitting points of
p_{\alpha}
then it remains a splitting node of
p_{\alpha+1}
. It is therefore clear that
p_{\alpha+1}\in\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
and
p_{\alpha+1}\geq_{\nu+\alpha}p_{\alpha}
. Since this was done in
X_{\alpha+1}
,
p_{\alpha+1}\in X_{\alpha+1}
.

This concludes the construction. Let
q^{*}=\bigcap_{\alpha<\lambda}p_{\alpha}
. Then
q^{*}\in\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
as the intersection of a Fusion sequence of conditions, and
q^{*}\geq_{\nu}p
. Let
C=C_{1}\cap C_{2}
, where
C_{1},C_{2}
are the following club subsets of
\lambda
:

C_{1}=\{\alpha<\lambda\colon X_{\alpha}\cap\lambda=\alpha\}

\displaystyle C_{2}=\{\alpha<\lambda\colon

\displaystyle\mbox{for every }\beta<\alpha\mbox{ and }t\in\beta^{\beta}\cap q^{*},\mbox{ either }t\mbox{ is a splitting node of }q^{*},

\displaystyle\mbox{or the least splitting node of }(q^{*})_{t}\mbox{ belongs to }\alpha^{<\alpha}\cap q^{*}\}.

Let us prove that
q^{*},\langle X_{\alpha}\colon\alpha<\kappa\rangle,C
are as desired for the
C
-Fusion property:

1.

Let
\alpha\in C
be regular, and assume that
G_{X_{\alpha}}\subseteq X_{\alpha}\cap\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
is
X_{\alpha}
-generic which is compatible with
q^{*}
. Let us prove that
G_{X_{\alpha}}\cup\{q^{*}\}
has an exact upper bound.

Let
s\in\lambda^{<\kappa}
be the initial segment of the generic branch determined by
G_{X_{\alpha}}
. Namely,

s=\bigcup\{stem(p)\mid p\in G_{X_{\alpha}}\}.

We note that
lh(s)=\alpha
. This is because for every
\beta<\alpha
,
G_{X_{\alpha}}
meets the dense open set of conditions deciding the generic branch up to height
\beta
. Also, as
X_{\alpha}\cap\lambda=\alpha
,
G_{X_{\alpha}}
does not include a condition whose stem extends has length
>\alpha
.

Note that as
\alpha
is regular,
C_{p}(s)=\{0\}
for all
p\in G_{X_{\alpha}}
. This, and the fact since
q^{*}
is compatible with
G_{X_{\alpha}}
imply that
s^{\frown}\langle 0\rangle\in q
.

We argue that
s\in\mbox{Split}_{\nu+\alpha}(q^{*})=\mbox{Split}_{\alpha}(q^{*})
12
12

12

Note that
\alpha>\nu
since
\nu\in X_{\alpha}
and
\alpha\in C_{1}
.
. Assume otherwise. Since
\alpha
is regular and
\mbox{lh}(s)=\alpha
, the set splitting nodes of
q^{*}
along
s
is contained in
\beta^{<\beta}
for some
\beta<\alpha
. The regularity of
\alpha
also implies that the set of closure points of
s
(namely, ordinals
\beta<\alpha
such that
s\upharpoonright\beta\in\beta^{\beta}
) is a club in
\alpha
. Thus, we can pick a closure point
\beta^{*}
of
s
such that
\beta^{*}>\beta
. Then
s\upharpoonright\beta^{*}
is not a splitting node of
q^{*}
, and the least splitting node of
(q^{*})_{s\upharpoonright\beta^{*}}
has height above
\alpha
, contradicting the fact that
\alpha\in C_{2}
.

Next, we need to show
G_{X_{\alpha}}\cup\{q^{*}\}
has an exact upper bound, and that this bound belongs to
D_{\alpha}
. Since
\alpha
is regular
(q^{*})_{s}=(q^{*})_{s{}^{\frown}\langle 0\rangle}\in D^{*}_{\alpha}\subseteq D_{\alpha}
. Therefore, it suffices to show that
(q^{*})_{s}
is indeed an exact upper bound of
G_{X_{\alpha}}\cup\{q\}
.

We first argue that
(q^{*})_{s}
extends every element of
G_{X_{\alpha}}
. Fix
q\in G_{X_{\alpha}}
. Consider the dense open set
D\in X_{\alpha}
of conditions which either extend or are incompatible with
q
. Since
\alpha
is a limit ordinal, there exists
\beta<\alpha
such that
D\in X_{\beta}
. By the remarks above, there exists
\beta^{*}\geq\beta
below
\alpha
such that
s\upharpoonright\beta^{*}\in\mbox{Split}_{\nu+\beta}(q^{*})
. By our construction,
(p_{\beta+1})_{s\upharpoonright(\beta^{*}+1)}\in D
, so
(p_{\beta+1})_{s\upharpoonright(\beta^{*}+1)}
either extends
q
or is incompatible with it. Since both
(p_{\beta+1})_{s\upharpoonright\beta^{*}+1}
and
q
are in
G_{X_{\alpha}}
,
(p_{\beta+1})_{s\upharpoonright\beta^{*}+1}
extends
q
. Thus,
(q^{*})_{s}
extends
q
.

Finally, to prove exactness, assume that
q^{\prime}\in\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
is an upper bound of
G_{X_{\alpha}}\cup\{q^{*}\}
. Since
q^{\prime}
bounds
G_{X_{\alpha}}
,
s
is the stem of
q^{\prime}
, namely,
s\in q^{\prime}
and
(q^{\prime})_{s}=q^{\prime}
. This, together with the assumption that
q^{\prime}\geq q^{*}
ensures that
q^{\prime}\geq(q^{*})_{s}
.

2.

It remains to verify that
q^{*}
is a generic condition for
X=\cup_{\alpha<\lambda}X_{\alpha}
. Assume that
G\subseteq\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
is generic over
V
with
q^{*}\in G
. We argue that
G\cap X
is
X
-generic. Assume that
D\in X
is a dense open subset of
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
. Pick a regular
\alpha<\lambda
such that
D\in X_{\alpha}
,
\alpha
is a closure point of the generic branch
g\in\left[\lambda\right]^{\lambda}
decided by
G
, and
g\upharpoonright\alpha\in\mbox{Split}_{\alpha}(q^{*})
. Note that such
\alpha
exists since
\lambda
remains Mahlo in
V[G]
, by Lemma
4.6
. By our construction,
(p_{\alpha+1})_{g\upharpoonright\alpha}=(p_{\alpha+1})_{g\upharpoonright\nu+\alpha}\in D
. Therefore,
(q^{*})_{g\upharpoonright\alpha}\in D\cap G\cap X
.

∎

Corollary 4.7
.

Assume that
\lambda
is Mahlo. Then the forcing
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
preserves cardinals.

Proof.

Cardinals up to
\lambda
are preserved by
\lambda
-closure of
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
. Also, since
|\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}|=\lambda^{+}
, cardinals above
\lambda^{+}
are preserved. So it suffices to prove that
\lambda^{+}
is preserved.
Assume that
p\in\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
and
\dot{f}
is a name for an increasing function from
\lambda
to
\lambda^{+}
. By
2.8
, there exists
p^{*}\geq p
, a function
F\colon\lambda\to[\lambda^{+}]^{<\lambda}
and a name
\dot{C}^{*}
for a club in
\lambda
, such that

p^{*}\Vdash\forall\alpha\in\dot{C}^{*}\cap Reg,\dot{f}(\alpha)\in F(\alpha).

In
V
, let
\xi^{*}=\sup(\cup\mbox{Im}(F))+1<\lambda^{+}
. We argue that
p^{*}\Vdash\mbox{Im}(f)\subseteq\xi^{*}
. Indeed, assume that
G\subseteq\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
is generic over
V
with
p^{*}\in G
. For every
\alpha<\lambda
, let
\alpha^{*}=\min((C^{*}\cap Reg)\setminus\alpha)
. Note that
\lambda
is Mahlo in
V[G]
, so
\alpha^{*}
exists for every
\alpha<\lambda
. But then
f(\alpha)\leq f(\alpha^{*})\leq\sup(F(\alpha^{*}))<\xi^{*}
.
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Assume that
\lambda
is Mahlo,
p\in\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
,
\nu<\lambda
and
\dot{\zeta}
is a
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
-name for an ordinal. Then there exists
q\geq_{\nu}p
and a set
x\in V
with
|x|<\lambda
such that
q\Vdash\dot{\zeta}\in\check{x}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}
in
\lambda
-closed and satisfies the
C
-fusion property, we can apply Lemma
2.8
on the constant function
f\colon\lambda\to\{\zeta\}
in
V^{\mathbb{M}_{\lambda}}
, to find
q\geq p
and a ground model function
F\colon\lambda\to[\mbox{Ord}]^{<\lambda}
such that, for some
\xi<\lambda
,
q\Vdash\dot{f}(\xi)\in\check{F}(\xi)
. The set
x=F(\xi)
is as desired. Going back to the proof of Lemma
2.8
, the condition
q
is obtained from
p
using the
C
-fusion property; by the proof of Lemma
4.6
, we can pick
q
such that
q\geq_{\nu}p
.
∎

4.2
Self-Coding Iteration of Miller Forcings

Fix a Mahlo cardinal
\alpha\leq\kappa
in
L[\bar{E}]
. In this section, we define a forcing notion
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
that will play the role of the
\alpha
-th forcing in the “Kunen-like” blueprint
2.22
. We follow the following conventions throughout this section.

•

\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
is defined in a ground model
V
which is a generic extension of
L[\bar{E}]
by a poset
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
that satisfies the “Kunen-like” blueprint. In particular,
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
satisfies the
\alpha
Iteration-Fusion property,
\alpha
remains a Mahlo cardinal in
V
by Lemma
2.10
and
(2^{\alpha})^{V}=\alpha^{+}
by Lemma
2.11
.

•

There exists in
L[\bar{E}]
a sequence

\vec{S}^{\alpha}=\langle\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)\colon\tau<\alpha^{++},\eta<\alpha^{+},i<2\rangle

of almost disjoint stationary subsets of
\alpha^{+}
, all of them contained in the stationary set
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}\subseteq\alpha^{+}\cap\cf(\omega_{1})
that was defined over
L[\bar{E}]
in proposition
3.3
. We further assume that

–

\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
does not reflect.

–

For each
\tau<\alpha^{++}
the sets in the subsequence
\langle\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)\colon\eta<\alpha^{+},i<2\rangle
are pairwise disjoint.

–

For each
\tau<\alpha^{++}
,
\eta<\alpha^{+}
and
i<2
,
\min\left(\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)\right)>\eta
.

–

The sequence
\langle\vec{S}^{\alpha}\colon\alpha\leq\kappa\mbox{ is Mahlo}\rangle
is uniformly definable over
L[\bar{E}]
, in the sense that there exists a formula
\varphi_{S}(\alpha,x)
which defines it in every inner model
M
containing
H_{\alpha^{++}}
.

•

We assume that the forcing
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
preserves the stationarity of stationary subsets of
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
(this will be proved in Lemma
4.32
). So we can assume that
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)
remains stationary in
V
for every
\tau<\alpha^{++}
,
\eta<\alpha^{+}
and
i<2
.

Let us sketch some of the main ideas behind the definition of
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
in this subsection. Recall that according to the “Kunen-like” blueprint,
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
should satisfy the following properties in
V
:

•

If
\alpha<\kappa
,
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}\in V_{\kappa}
,
13
13

13

This implies that there exists a
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa}
-name for
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
in
(V_{\kappa})^{L[\bar{E}]}
.

•

|{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}|=\alpha^{++}
.

•

\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
adds an increasing sequence in
<^{\alpha}_{sing}
of length
\alpha^{++}
.

•

{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is
\alpha
-distributive, satisfies the
\alpha^{++}
.c.c, and preserves the Mahloness of
\alpha
,

•

{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
has the
\alpha
C-Fusion property,

•

{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is self coding.

In order to ensure that the poset
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
adds an increasing sequence in
<^{\alpha}_{sing}
of length
\alpha^{++}
, it includes an
\alpha^{++}
-iteration of the Miller forcing
\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}
. By Claim
4.3
, the sequence of generic subsets of
\alpha
produced this way will be
<^{\alpha}_{sing}
-increasing.

In order to achieve
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
which is self-coding, we interleave coding posets between the Miller posets, such that each coding poset codes the initial segment of the iteration up to its stage by adding a club which is disjoint from certain sets
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)\subseteq\alpha^{+}
from the given list.
14
14

14

The interleaved structure of the iteration seems to be necessary since a coding poset that adds a club to
\alpha^{+}
will collapse
2^{\alpha}
to
\alpha^{+}
.

Our construction of
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
with the these two features relies on the iteration theory of higher Miller posets from
[
12
]
. The main arguments in this section combine fusion methods from
[
12
]
with closure arguments of club shooting posets involving IA chains of models, that will guarantee
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
has the C-Fusion property.
The poset
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
will be equivalent to a back-to-back
\leq\alpha-
support iteration of Miller and coding posets. The back-to-back iteration will be denoted by
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}
. The reason we do not take
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
to be the “full” back-to-back iteration is that proper initial segments of this iteration have size
\alpha^{++}
, which is larger than needed and create various issues with the coding mechanism. Instead, we show that each initial segment
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
,
\tau<\alpha^{++}
, of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}
has a dense subset
\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
of size
|\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}|=\alpha^{+}
.

We proceed to define by induction on
\tau\leq\alpha^{++}
an iteration

\langle\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau},\dot{\mathbb{R}}_{\tau}\mid\tau<\alpha^{++}\rangle

together with dense subsets

\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}.

For each
\eta\leq\alpha^{++}
,

\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\eta}=\langle\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau},\dot{\mathbb{R}}_{\tau}\mid\tau<\eta\rangle

is a
\leq\alpha
-support iteration of length
\eta
, where
for every
\tau<\eta
,
{\mathbb{R}}_{\tau}\in V^{\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}}
has the form

\mathbb{R}_{\tau}={\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}*{\mathbb{C}}^{\alpha}_{\tau},

with
\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}
being the Miller poset on
\alpha
from Section
4.1
, and
\mathbb{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau}\in V^{\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}*\dot{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}}
is a coding poset that codes a generic set of size
\alpha^{+}
by adding a certain closed unbounded subset of
\alpha^{+}
(see Definition
4.9
for more details).

Therefore, conditions
p\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
are sequences

p=\langle p(\xi)\mid\xi\in\supp(p)\rangle

with
\supp(p)\in[\tau]^{\leq\alpha}
. For each
\xi\in\supp(p)
,

p\upharpoonright\xi=\langle p(\eta)\mid\eta\in\supp(p)\cap\xi\rangle\in\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\xi}

and

p\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash_{\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi}}p(\xi)\in\dot{\mathbb{R}}_{\xi}.

We further write

p(\xi)=(\dot{T}^{p}_{\xi},\dot{c}^{p}_{\xi}),

where

p\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{p}_{\xi}\in\mathbb{M}_{\alpha},\text{ and }p\upharpoonright\xi{}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{p}_{\xi}\in\dot{\mathbb{C}}^{\alpha}_{\xi}.

As mentioned above, we will introduce a dense subset
\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}

of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
of size
|\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}|=\alpha^{+}
(Lemma
4.20
). Assuming
\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
is given, we define the coding posets
\mathbb{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
in
V^{{\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}}*\dot{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}}
. Since
\alpha
is fixed in this subsection, we will use the notation
\mathbb{C}_{\tau}
instead of
\mathbb{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
until the end of the subsection.

Definition 4.9
.

Let
\tau<\alpha^{++}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{\tau}^{\alpha}\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
be a dense subset of size
|\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}|=\alpha^{+}
.

Let
\langle q^{\alpha}_{\tau}(\eta)\mid\eta<\alpha^{+}\rangle
be the well-order of of
\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
induced by the constrcutible ordering
L[\bar{E}]
of their
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-names in
L[\bar{E}]
.
15
15

15

where the names are taken minimal with respect to the constructible order.
Let
{G}({\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}})
denote the generic filter of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\tau}_{\alpha}
.

Let
\mathbb{C}_{\tau}
(or
\mathbb{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
, whenever
\alpha
is not clear from the context) be the
{\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}}*{\dot{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}}
-name of all closed bounded subsets
c
of
\alpha^{+}
which satisfy that for every
\eta<\alpha^{+}
,

c\cap\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,0)=\emptyset\iff q^{\tau}_{\alpha}(\eta)\in G(\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}),

and

c\cap\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,1)=\emptyset\iff q^{\tau}_{\alpha}(\eta)\not\in G(\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}).

A closed set
d\in\mathbb{C}_{\tau}
extends
c
if it end-extends
c
, i.e., if
d\cap\max(c)=c
.

Our first goal is to show that the forcing
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
is
\sigma
-closed, and preserves the stationarity of
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)
for every
\eta<\alpha^{+}
and
i<2
.

Lemma 4.10
.

For every
\tau\leq\alpha^{++}
,
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
is
\sigma
-closed.

Proof.

Assuming that
\langle p_{i}\colon i<\omega\rangle
is an increasing sequence of conditions, let
p^{*}=\bigvee_{i<\omega}p_{i}
be the condition such that
\supp(p^{*})=\bigcup_{i<\omega}\supp(p_{i})
, and, for every
\xi\in\supp(p^{*})
,

p^{*}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}=\bigcap_{i<\omega,\xi\in\supp(p_{i})}\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}

and–

{p^{*}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}=\bigcup_{i<\omega}\dot{c}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\cup\Big\{\sup\left(\bigcup_{i<\omega}\dot{c}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\right)\big\}.

We argue that
p^{*}\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. Fix
\xi\in\supp(p^{*})
and assume that
p^{*}\upharpoonright\xi\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi}
. First,
p^{*}\upharpoonright\xi
forces that
\dot{T}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}\in\dot{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
by
\alpha
-closure of
\dot{\mu_{\alpha}}
. Next, we show that
{p^{*}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}
forces that
\dot{c}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}\in\dot{\mathbb{C}}_{\xi}
. Assuming that
\dot{c}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}\in\dot{\mathbb{C}}_{\xi}
is not equal to one of the conditions
\dot{c}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
for
i<\omega
,
\sup\left(\bigcup_{i<\omega}\dot{c}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\right)
is an ordinal of countable cofinality, and thus doesn’t belong to any of the stationary sets
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,j)
for
\eta<\alpha^{+}
and
j<2
. Hence,
{p^{*}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}\in\dot{\mathbb{C}}_{\xi}
.
∎

Lemma 4.11
.

\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
preserves the stationarity of
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)
for every
\eta<\alpha^{+}
and
i<2
.

Proof.

Let
S
be one of the sets
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta^{*},i^{*})
for some
\tau^{*}<\alpha^{+},i^{*}<2
. Assume that
\dot{C}
is a
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
-name for a club subset of
\alpha^{+}
and
p\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. We argue that there exists
q\geq p
such that
q\Vdash\dot{C}\cap S\neq\emptyset
.

Since the sets in
\vec{\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha}
are pairwise almost disjoint, we have for every
\tau^{\prime}<\tau,\eta<\alpha^{+},i<2
an ordinal
\gamma(\tau^{\prime},\eta,i)<\alpha^{+}
such that

S\cap\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau^{\prime},\eta,i)\subseteq\gamma(\tau^{\prime},\eta,i).

Denote
\vec{\gamma}=\langle\gamma(\tau^{\prime},\eta,i)\colon\tau^{\prime}<\tau,\eta<\alpha^{+},i<2\rangle
.

Let
X\prec H_{\theta}
(for
\theta
large enough) be an elementary substructure such that:

•

\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau},\dot{C},p,\vec{\gamma},\alpha,\tau\in X
.

•

X
is closed under countable sequences of its elements.

•

\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+}):=\sup(X\cap\alpha^{+})\in S
.

•

\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})\subseteq X
.

Such
X
can be constructed by standard methods, by building a continuous chain
\langle X_{i}\colon i<\alpha^{+}\rangle
of elementary substructures of
H_{\theta}
, each of them of size
\alpha
, where
X_{i+1}
contains
\chi_{X_{i}}(\alpha^{+})
and all the countable sequences of elements of
X_{i}
for every
i<\alpha^{+}
. Since
S\subseteq\alpha^{+}
is stationary and
\langle\chi_{X_{i}}(\alpha^{+})\colon i<\alpha^{+}\rangle
is a club in
\alpha^{+}
, we can find limit
i<\alpha^{+}
such that
X=X_{i}
satisfies the above properties.

Assume that
\tau^{\prime}\in X\cap\tau
. For every
\eta<\chi_{X}(\alpha+)
and
i<2
, we have
\gamma(\tau^{\prime},\eta,i)\in X\cap\alpha^{+}
, and thus
\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})>\gamma(\tau^{\prime},\eta,i)
, namely
\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})\notin S\cap\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau^{\prime},\eta,i)
. For every
\eta\geq\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})
and
i<2
, we have
\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau^{\prime},\eta,i)
since
\min\left(\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau^{\prime},\eta,i)\right)>\eta
. Over all, we proved that for every
\tau^{\prime}\in X\cap\tau
,

\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})\in S\setminus\left(\bigcup_{\eta<\alpha^{+},i<2}\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau^{\prime},\eta,i)\right).

Having this in mind, we proceed and fix a cofinal sequence
\vec{\delta}=\langle\delta_{i}\colon i<\omega_{1}\rangle
in
\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})
. By the above properties, each initial segment of
\vec{\delta}
belongs to
X
. We build a sequence of conditions
\vec{p}=\langle p_{i}\colon i<\omega_{1}\rangle
such that:

•

Every initial segment of
\vec{p}
belongs to
X
.

•

For every
i<\omega_{1}
,
p_{i+1}
decides
\min(\dot{C}\setminus(\delta_{i}+1))
.

•

For every
i<\omega_{1}
and
\xi\in\supp(p_{i+1})
,
{p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}\Vdash\max(\dot{c}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi})>\delta_{i}
.

We start by picking
p_{0}=p
, and, in limit steps,
p_{i}=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}
. By
\sigma
-closure and the fact that
\langle p_{j}\colon j<i\rangle\in X
,
p_{i}
is a legitimate condition of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
which belongs to
X
.

At successor steps, assuming that
p_{i}
was defined, extend it to
p^{\prime}_{i+1}\in X
such that
p^{\prime}_{i+1}
decides
\min(\dot{c}\setminus\delta_{i})
. Finally, extend
p_{i+1}\geq p^{\prime}_{i+1}
in
X
without extending the support or the Miller coordinates, such that, for every
\xi<\supp(p^{\prime}_{i+1})
,

{p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}\Vdash\max\left(\dot{c}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}\right)>\delta_{i}.

This concludes the construction. Denote
q=\bigvee_{i<\omega_{1}}p_{i}
. We first argue that
q\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. Since the Miller components are
\alpha
-closed, the only potential problem might occur in the coding coordinates. Recall that for every
\xi\in\supp(q)
,

{q\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}=\bigcup_{i<\omega_{1}}\dot{c}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\cup\Big\{\sup\left(\bigcup_{i<\omega_{1}}\dot{c}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\right)\Big\}

and, by our construction,

\sup\left(\bigcup_{i<\omega_{1}}\dot{c}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\right)=\sup_{i<\omega_{1}}\delta_{i}=\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+}).

Thus, in order to prove that
{q\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}\in\dot{\mathbb{C}}_{\xi}
, it suffices to prove that
\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\xi,\eta^{\prime},i^{\prime})
for every
\eta^{\prime}<\alpha^{+}
and
i^{\prime}<2
.

Let
i<\omega_{1}
be the least such that
\xi\in\supp(p_{i})
. Since
X\vDash|\supp(p_{i})|\leq\alpha
, there exists a surjection
f\in X_{i}
,
f\colon\alpha\to X
, and as
\alpha\subseteq X
,
\supp(p_{i})=f^{\prime\prime}\alpha\subseteq X
. It follows that
\xi\in X\cap\tau
, and, as we already established, this implies

\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})\notin\bigcup_{\eta<\alpha^{+},i<2}\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\xi,\eta,i).

This concludes the proof that
q\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. Finally,
q
forces that
\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})
is a limit point of
\dot{C}
, and thus
q\Vdash\dot{C}\cap S\neq\emptyset
, as desired.
∎

Our next goal is to prove that for every
\tau\leq\alpha^{++}
,
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
has desirable properties such as
\alpha
-distributivity and the
C
-fusion property. For this we introduce a number of fusion related notions and IA chains.

Definition 4.12
.

Let
\tau\leq\alpha^{++}
.

1.

Assume that
F\in[\tau]^{\leq\alpha}
and
\nu<\kappa
. We define an order
\leq_{F,\nu}
on
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
as follows: for
p,q\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
, set
p\leq_{F,\nu}q
if and only if
p\leq q
, and, for every
\xi\in F\cap\supp(p)
,
q\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{p}_{\xi}\leq_{\nu}\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}
.
16
16

16

The order
\leq_{\nu}
on
\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}
is given in Definition
4.4
.

2.

Let
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
be the set of conditions
p\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
for which there exists a sequence

\vec{\mu}=\langle\mu_{\xi}\colon\xi\in\supp(p)\rangle\subseteq\alpha^{+}

such that, for every
\xi\in\supp(p)
,

{p\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p}_{\xi}\Vdash_{{\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}}*\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}}\max(\dot{c}^{p}_{\xi})<\check{\mu}_{\xi}.

We call such a sequence
\vec{\mu}
a
local bounding function
for
p
.

3.

A
pacing chain
is a continuous, internally approachable sequence
\vec{X}=\langle X_{i}\colon i\leq\alpha\rangle
elementary substructures of
H_{\theta}
, for
\theta
large enough, such that:

(a)

\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau},\tau,\alpha\in X_{0}
.

(b)

For every
i<\alpha
,
|X_{i}|<\alpha
(in particular,
|X_{\alpha}|=\alpha
).

(c)

For every
i<\alpha
,
{}^{i}{X_{i}}\subseteq X_{i+1}
.

(d)

For every
i\leq\alpha
,
\chi_{X_{i}}(\alpha^{+}):=\sup(X_{i}\cap\alpha^{+})\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
.

4.

Let
\vec{X}
be a pacing chain and fix
\gamma\leq\alpha
. A sequence of conditions
\vec{p}=\langle p_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle
is
paced
with respect to
\vec{X}
if there exists a weakly increasing continuous sequence
\langle\tau_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle\subseteq\tau+1
,
17
17

17

A typical case will be
\tau_{i}=\tau
for every
i<\gamma
. The only point in which this will not be the case is Lemma
4.15
.
in
X_{0}
, such that:

(a)

For every
i<\gamma
,
p_{i}\upharpoonright\tau_{i}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau_{i}}
.

(b)

For every
i<\gamma
, if
\tau_{i}<\tau
then
p_{i}\upharpoonright\tau_{i}\Vdash p_{i}\setminus\tau_{i}=p_{0}\setminus\tau_{i}
.

(c)

For every
i<\gamma
,
\vec{p}\upharpoonright i+1\in X_{i+1}
.

(d)

For every
i<j<\gamma
,
p_{i}\leq p_{j}
.

(e)

For every
i<\gamma
and
\xi\in\supp(p_{i})\cap\tau_{i}
,
{p_{i}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\Vdash\check{\chi}_{X_{i}}(\alpha^{+})\in\dot{c}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
.

(f)

For every limit
i<\gamma
,
p_{i}=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}
, namely,
\supp(p_{i})=\bigcup_{j<i}\supp(p_{j})
, and for every
\xi\in\supp(p_{i})
,

p_{i}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}=\bigcap_{j<i}\dot{T}^{p_{j}}_{\xi}

and–

(p_{i}\upharpoonright\xi)^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}=\left(\bigcup_{j<i}\dot{c}^{p_{j}}_{\xi}\right)\cup\Big\{\sup\left(\bigcup_{j<i}\dot{c}^{p_{j}}_{\xi}\right)\Big\}

5.

Let
\vec{X}
be a pacing chain. A sequence of length
\alpha
,
\vec{p}=\langle p_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
, is a
paced fusion sequence
with respect to
\vec{X}
, if it is a paced sequence with respect to
\vec{X}
, and, in addition, there exists a
\subseteq
-increasing sequence of sets
\vec{F}=\langle F_{i}\colon i\leq\alpha\rangle\subseteq[\tau]^{<\alpha}
and some
\nu<\alpha
such that:

(a)

For every
i<\alpha
,
\vec{F}\upharpoonright i+1\in X_{i+1}
.

(b)

For every
i<\alpha
,
F_{i}\subseteq\supp(p_{i})
.

(c)

For every
i<j<\alpha
,
p_{i}\leq_{F_{i},\nu+i}p_{j}
.

(d)

For every limit
i<\alpha
,
F_{i}=\bigcup_{j<i}F_{j}
.

(e)

F_{\alpha}=\bigcup_{i<\alpha}F_{i}=\bigcup_{i<\alpha}\supp(p_{i})
.

We will elaborate below how pace chains, paced sequences and paced fusion sequences are constructed. First, let us verify that paced sequences and paced fusion sequences have an exact upper bound.

Claim 4.13
.

Let
\gamma\leq\alpha
be a limit ordinal. Assume that
\vec{X}=\langle X_{i}\colon i\leq\gamma\rangle
is a pace chain. Assume that
\vec{p}=\langle p_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
is a paced sequence with respect to
\vec{X}
. Let
p^{*}=\bigvee_{i<\gamma}p_{i}
.

1.

If
\gamma<\alpha
, then
p^{*}
is an exact upper bound of
\{p_{i}\colon i<\gamma\}
.

2.

If
\gamma=\alpha
and
\vec{p}
is a paced fusion sequence with respect to
\vec{X}
, then
p^{*}
is an exact upper bound of
\{p_{i}\colon i<\alpha\}
.

Proof.

We concentrate on the proof that
p^{*}
is a condition of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
which extends
p_{i}
for every
i<\gamma
. Once we prove that, the exactness easily follows.

Work by induction on
\xi\in\supp(p^{*})
, assume that
p^{*}\upharpoonright\xi\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi}
is an upper bound of
\langle p_{i}\upharpoonright\xi\colon i<\gamma\rangle
. Let us argue that

p^{*}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}\in\dot{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha},\quad\dot{T}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}\geq_{\dot{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}}\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi},\text{ and }

{p^{*}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}\in\mathbb{C}_{\xi}\mbox{ and for every }i<\gamma,\dot{c}^{p^{*}}_{\xi}\geq_{\dot{\mathbb{C}}_{\xi}}\dot{c}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}.

We remark that if
\langle\tau_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle
witnesses the fact that
\vec{p}
is a paced sequence, and
\xi\geq\sup\{\tau_{i}\colon i<\gamma\}
, the above inductive step is clear since
p\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash p^{*}\setminus\xi=p_{0}\setminus\xi
. Thus, we can consider only the case
\xi<\tau^{*}
where
\tau^{*}
denotes
\sup\{\tau_{i}\colon i<\gamma\}
.

1.

Assume that
\gamma<\alpha
. First,
T^{p^{*}}_{\xi}=\bigcap T^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\in\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}
by
\alpha
-closure of
\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}
. Moving to the coding component name in
V^{{\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi}}*\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}}
, for every
i<\gamma
, let
\vec{\mu}^{i}=\langle\mu^{i}_{\eta}\colon\eta\in\supp(p_{i})\cap\tau_{i}\rangle
be a local bounding function witnessing the fact that
p_{i}\upharpoonright\tau_{i}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau_{i}}
. Since
p_{i}\in X_{i+1}
, we may assume
\mu^{i}_{\xi}<\chi_{X_{i+1}}(\alpha^{+})
for every
i<\gamma
. Also, there exists
i_{0}<\gamma
such that
\xi<\tau_{i_{0}}
, and for every
i_{0}\leq i<\gamma
,
\chi_{X_{i}}(\alpha^{+})\in c^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
. Combining the above facts, it follows that
\bigcup_{i<\gamma}c^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
is an unbounded subset of
\chi_{X_{\gamma}}(\alpha^{+})
. Thus,

c^{p^{*}}_{\xi}=\left(\bigcup_{i<\gamma}c^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\right)\cup\{\chi_{X_{\gamma}}(\alpha^{+})\}

and
c^{p^{*}}_{\xi}
is indeed a condition that extends each
c^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
since
\chi_{X_{\gamma}}(\alpha^{+})\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
.

2.

Assume
\gamma=\alpha
. Let
\vec{F}=\langle F_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
be a
\subseteq
-increasing sequence of sets witnessing the fact that
\vec{p}
is a paced fusion sequence. Let
i_{0}<\alpha
be the least such that
\xi\in F_{i_{0}}
. In
V^{\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi}}
,
\langle T^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\colon i_{0}\leq i<\alpha\rangle
is a fusion sequence of conditions in
\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}
. In particular,
T^{p^{*}}_{\xi}:=\bigcap T^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\in\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}
. The argument concerning the coding component being a condition is the same as the one given just above.

∎

Claim 4.14
.

There exists a pace chain of elementary substructures of
H_{\theta}
for large enough
\theta
.

Proof.

Let
X^{*}
be an elementary substructure of
H_{\theta}
such that:

•

|X^{*}|=\alpha
.

•

\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau},\tau,\alpha\in X^{*}
.

•

X^{*}
is closed under
<\alpha
-sequences of its elements.

•

\cf(\chi_{X^{*}}(\alpha^{+}))=\alpha
(In particular,
\chi_{X^{*}}(\alpha^{+})\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
, since
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
consists of points of cofinality
\omega_{1}
).

Such
X^{*}
can be constructed by standard methods, by building a continuous chain of elementary substructures
\langle X^{*}_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
of
H_{\theta}
, each of them of size
\alpha
, where
X^{*}_{i+1}
contains all the
<\alpha
-sequences of elements of
X^{*}_{i}
for every
i<\alpha
. Let
X^{*}:=\bigcup_{i<\alpha}X^{*}_{i}
. Then
\cf(\chi_{X^{*}}(\alpha^{+}))=\alpha
and
X^{*}
is indeed closed under
<\alpha
-sequences of its elements.

As
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
does not reflect
there is a club
C\subseteq\rho:=\chi_{X^{*}}(\alpha^{+})
such that
otp(C)=\cf(\rho)=\alpha
and
C\cap\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}=\emptyset
. Fix such a club
C
as well as an enumeration
\langle a_{i}\mid i<\alpha\rangle
of
X^{*}
.
We now construct in
V
a continuous, internally approachable sequence
\langle X_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
of elementary substructures of
X^{*}
such that, for every
i<\alpha
,

•

X_{i}\in X^{*}
.

•

|X_{i}|<\alpha
.

•

\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau},\tau,\alpha\in X_{i}
.

•

\chi_{X_{i}}(\alpha^{+})\in C
(and, in particular,
\chi_{X_{i}}(\alpha^{+})\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
).

•

\vec{X}\upharpoonright i,a_{i}\in X_{i+1}
and

{}^{i}X_{i+1}\subseteq X_{i+1}

This will suffice, since
\langle X_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle^{\frown}\langle X^{*}\rangle

will be the desired pace chain of substructures of
H_{\theta}
.
For the construction of
X_{0}
, pick a continuous, elementary chain
\langle X^{j}_{0}\colon j<\alpha\rangle\in V
of substructures of
X^{*}
, such that each structure in the sequence belongs to
X^{*}
and
\langle\chi_{X^{j}_{0}}(\alpha^{+})\colon j<\alpha\rangle
is a club
D
in
\rho
. In order to make sure that
D
is indeed a club in
\rho
, we prescribe a cofinal sequence of order type
\alpha
in
\rho
, and make sure that
\chi_{X^{j+1}_{0}}(\alpha^{+})
is above the
j
-th element in it. Since
X^{j+1}_{0}
belongs to
X^{*}
, its ordinals do not exceed
\rho
. This ensures that
D=\langle\chi_{X^{j}_{0}}(\alpha^{+})\colon j<\alpha\rangle
is as desired, and thus there exists
j<\alpha
such that
\chi^{j+1}_{0}(\alpha^{+})\in C\cap D
. Take
X_{0}:=X^{j+1}_{0}
and proceed as follows:

At successor steps, repeat the argument: assuming that
i<\alpha
and
X_{i}\prec X^{*}
has been chosen, construct (in
V
) a continuous, elementary chain
\langle X^{j}_{i+1}\colon j<\alpha\rangle
of substructures of
X^{*}
such that
\langle X_{i^{\prime}}\colon i^{\prime}\leq i\rangle\in X^{0}_{i+1}
(note that, since
X^{*}
is closed under
<\alpha
-sequences of its elements,
\langle X_{i^{\prime}}\colon i^{\prime}\leq i\rangle\in X^{*}
),
X^{0}_{i+1}
contains all the
\leq i
-sequences of elements of
X_{i}
, and
\langle\chi_{X^{j}_{i+1}}(\alpha^{+})\colon j<\alpha\rangle
forms a club
D_{i+1}
in
\rho
. Then, pick
j\in D_{i+1}\cap C
and let
X_{i+1}:=X^{j}_{i+1}
.

At limit steps, we take unions: for
i<\alpha
limit, let
X_{i}=\bigcup_{i^{\prime}<i}X_{i^{\prime}}
, and note that, by induction,
\langle\chi_{X_{i^{\prime}}}(\alpha^{+})\colon i^{\prime}<i\rangle
is a
<\alpha
sequence of elements of
C
, and thus its union
\chi_{X_{i}}(\alpha^{+})
belongs to
C
.
∎

We are now ready to state the main properties of the forcings
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
for
\tau\leq\alpha^{++}
, in the following sequence of Lemmas.

Lemma 4.15
.

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
is a dense subset of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. Furthermore, for every
F\in[\tau]^{<\alpha}
and
\nu<\alpha
,
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
is a
\leq_{F,\nu}
-dense subset of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
.

Lemma 4.16
.

\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
is
\alpha
-distributive. Furthermore, for every
F\in[\tau]^{<\alpha}
and
\nu<\alpha
,
\langle\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau},\leq_{F,\nu}\rangle
is
\alpha
-distributive.

Lemma 4.17
.

\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
preserves the Mahloness of
\alpha
.

Lemma 4.18
.

\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
satisfies the
\alpha

C
-Fusion property.

Lemma 4.19
.

Suppose that
p\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
,
F\in[\tau]^{<\alpha}
,
\nu<\alpha
and
\dot{\zeta}
is a
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
-name for an ordinal. Then there exists
q\geq_{F,\nu}p
and a set
x\in V
with
|x|\leq\alpha
such that
q\Vdash\dot{\zeta}\in x
.

Lemma 4.20
.

Assume that
\tau<\alpha^{++}
. There exists a dense subset
\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
of size
|\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}|=\alpha^{+}
.

We will prove lemmas
4.15
-
4.20
by induction on
\tau\leq\alpha^{++}
. Thus, we assume that the Lemmas are true for every
\tau^{\prime}<\tau
, and prove that they are true for
\tau
.

Proof.

(Lemma
4.15
)

Let
p\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. We argue that
p
can be
\leq_{F,\nu}
-extended to a condition in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. We divide the proof into several cases:

Case 1:

\tau=\tau^{\prime}+1
is a successor ordinal. In this case, apply Corollary
4.8
to choose
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau^{\prime}}
-names
\dot{S}
for a condition in
\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}
which
\leq_{\nu}
extends
\dot{T}^{p}_{\tau^{\prime}}
, and
\dot{\mu}
for an ordinal below
\alpha^{+}
such that–

p\upharpoonright\tau^{\prime}\Vdash\left(\dot{S}\Vdash_{\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}}\sup(\dot{c}^{p}_{\tau^{\prime}})<\dot{\mu}\right)

By the induction hypothesis (assuming that the current Lemma combined with Lemma
4.19
hold for
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau^{\prime}}
), we can
\leq_{F\setminus\{\tau^{\prime}\},\nu}
-extend
p\upharpoonright\tau^{\prime}
to a condition
q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau^{\prime}}
such that, for some
\mu^{*}<\alpha^{+}
,
q\Vdash\dot{\mu}<\mu^{*}
. Then
q^{\frown}\dot{S}^{\frown}{\dot{c}^{p}_{\tau^{\prime}}}
is a
\leq_{F,\nu}
extension of
p
in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
.

Case 2:

\tau
is limit and
\cf(\tau)>\alpha
. Let
\tau^{\prime}<\tau
be an upper bound on
\supp(p)
. By the induction hypothesis, there exists
q\geq_{F\cap\tau^{\prime},\nu}p
such that
q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau^{\prime}}
. Viewing
q
as a condition of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
,
p\leq_{F,\nu}q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
.

Case 3:

\tau
is limit and
\cf(\tau)<\alpha
. Denote
\gamma=\cf(\tau)
. Fix a continuous, increasing, cofinal sequence
\langle\tau_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle
in
\tau
, and a pacing chain
\langle X_{i}\colon i\leq\alpha\rangle
such that
p,\langle\tau_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle,F,\nu\in X_{0}
. We construct a paced sequence
\langle p_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle
with respect to
\vec{X}
.
Let
p_{0}=p
. We assume for simplicity that
\tau_{0}=0
, so we have
p_{0}\upharpoonright\tau_{0}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau_{0}}
.
At limit steps,
p_{i}=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}
. Note first that
p_{i}\in X_{i+1}
since
X_{i+1}
contains every sequence of length
i
of elements of
X_{i}
. Since
\langle p_{j}\colon j<i\rangle
is by itself a paced sequence of conditions,

p_{i}\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. We should verify that
p_{i}\upharpoonright\tau_{i}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau_{i}}
: for every
j<i
, let

\vec{\mu}^{j}=\langle\mu^{j}_{\xi}\colon\xi\in\supp(p_{j})\cap\tau_{j}\rangle

be a local bounding function for
p_{j}\upharpoonright{\tau_{j}}
. Define
\vec{\mu}=\langle\mu_{\xi}\colon\xi\in\supp(p_{j})\cap\tau_{i}\rangle
by taking, for each
\xi\in\supp(p_{i})\cap\tau_{i}
,

\mu_{\xi}=\sup(\{\mu^{j}_{\xi}\colon j<i,\xi\in\supp(p_{j})\cap\tau_{j}\})+1.

It is not hard to verify that
\vec{\mu}
is a local bounding function for
p_{i}\upharpoonright\tau_{i}
.

We proceed to the successor case. Assume that
p_{i}
has been constructed and
p_{i}\in X_{i+1}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
p_{i}\upharpoonright\tau_{i+1}
can be
\leq_{F,\nu}
extended inside
X_{i+1}
to a condition
q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau_{i+1}}
. We define
p_{i+1}\geq_{F,\nu}p_{i}
as follows: first, let
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\tau_{i+1}
be an extension of
q
with the same support, such that, for every
\xi\in\supp(q)
,

p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}=\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}

and

{p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}=\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}\cup\{\chi_{X_{i+1}}(\alpha^{+})\}.

Note that
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\tau_{i+1}\geq q
is a legitimate condition because
\chi_{X_{i+1}}(\alpha^{+})\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
. Second, choose
p_{i+1}\setminus\tau_{i+1}
such that
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\tau_{i+1}\Vdash p_{i+1}\setminus\tau_{i+1}=p_{0}\setminus\tau_{i+1}
.

Note that if
\vec{\mu}=\langle\mu_{\xi}\colon\xi\in\supp(q)\cap\tau_{i+1}\rangle
is a local bounding function for
q
in
X_{i+1}
, then
\vec{\mu}^{*}=\langle\mu^{*}_{\xi}\colon\xi\in\supp(p_{i+1})\cap\tau_{i+1}\rangle
is a local bounding function for
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\tau_{i+1}
, where
\mu^{*}_{\xi}=\max(\mu_{\xi},\chi_{X_{i+1}}(\alpha^{+})+1)
. Also note that
p_{i+1}
is chosen inside
X_{i+2}
, by internal approachability.

This concludes the inductive construction. Since
\langle p_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle
is a paced sequence with respect to
\vec{X}
,
q=\bigvee_{i<\gamma}p_{i}
is a condition in
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. Arguing as in the limit step, it’s not hard to verify that
q
has a local bounding function, so
q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
.

Case 4:

\tau
is limit and
\cf(\tau)=\alpha
. Fix a continuous, increasing, cofinal sequence
\langle\tau_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
in
\tau
. We will construct a fusion pace sequence
\langle p_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
with respect to a pacing chain
\vec{X}
, witnessed by some
\subseteq
-increasing sequence
\langle F_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
. We will need to integrate into the construction a bookkeeping argument in order to ensure that
\bigcup_{i<\alpha}F_{i}=\bigcup_{i<\alpha}\supp(p_{i})
. For that, we prescribe in advance an arbitrary well order
W(x)
of order type
\leq\alpha
of
x
, for every set
x\in[\tau]^{\leq\alpha}
. We also fix a partition
\vec{A}=\langle A_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
of
\alpha
to sets of size
\alpha
. Fix a pace chain
\langle X_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
with

p,\langle\tau_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle,\langle W(x)\colon x\in[\tau]^{\leq\alpha}\rangle,\vec{A},F,\nu\in X_{0}.

For the construction of the paced fusion sequence, first let
p_{0}=p
,
F_{0}=F
. In limit steps,
p_{i}=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}
and
F_{i}=\bigcup_{j<i}F_{j}
for every limit
i<\alpha
. Note that
p_{i},F_{i}\in X_{i+1}
. Let us concentrate on successor steps. Assume that
p_{i},F_{i}
have been constructed in
X_{i+1}
, and let us construct
p_{i+1},F_{i+1}
.

We begin by defining
F_{i+1}
. Denote by
i^{*}<\alpha
the unique index such that
i\in A_{i^{*}}
. If
i^{*}>i
, let
F_{i+1}=F_{i}
. Assume
i^{*}\leq i
(so that
p_{i^{*}}
is already defined at this point of the construction). Let
\xi^{*}
be the
W(\supp(p_{i^{*}}))
-minimal element of
\supp(p_{i^{*}})\setminus\bigcup_{j\leq i}F_{j}
and set
F_{i+1}=F_{i}\cup\{\xi^{*}\}
. Note that
F_{i+1}\in X_{i+1}
.

The definition of
p_{i+1}
is similar to the case where
\cf(\tau)<\alpha
: choose first a condition
q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau_{i+1}}
in
X_{i+1}
, such that

q\geq_{F_{i}\cap\tau_{i+1},\nu+i}p_{i}\upharpoonright\tau_{i+1}.

This is possible since
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau_{i+1}}
is
\leq_{F_{i}\cap\tau_{i+1},\nu+i}
-dense inside
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau_{i+1}}
by the induction hypothesis. Then, let
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\tau_{i+1}
be an extension of
q
with the same support, such that for every
\xi\in\supp(q)
,
{p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\xi}\Vdash\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}=\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}
and

{p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}\Vdash\chi_{X_{i+1}}(\alpha^{+})\in\dot{c}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}.

Finally, take
p_{i+1}\setminus\tau_{i+1}=p_{0}\setminus\tau_{i+1}
.
The condition
p_{i+1}
obtained this way can be chosen inside
X_{i+2}
by internal approachability. Note also that
p_{i+1}\geq_{F_{i},\nu+i}p_{i}
.

This concludes the definition of
\langle p_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
. We only need to make sure that
\bigcup_{i<\alpha}F_{i}=\bigcup_{i<\alpha}\supp(p_{i})
.

Assume that
\xi\in\bigcup_{i<\alpha}\supp(p_{i})
. Let
i^{*}<\alpha
be the least such that
\xi\in\supp(p_{i^{*}})
. Then
S_{i^{*}}\setminus i^{*}
is an unbounded subset of
\alpha
of order type
\alpha
, and so, we will have some
i\in S_{i^{*}}\setminus i^{*}
such that
\xi^{*}\in F_{i+1}
(indeed, if
\xi^{*}
is the
\gamma
-th element in
\supp(p_{i^{*}})
according to
W(\supp(p_{i^{*}}))
, then
\xi^{*}
will be in
F_{i+1}
for
i
which is the
\gamma
-th element of
S_{i^{*}}\setminus i^{*}
).

This shows that
\langle p_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
is a paced fusion sequence, and thus it has an exact upper bound
p^{*}=\bigvee_{i<\alpha}p_{i}
. Since
p_{i}\upharpoonright\tau_{i}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau_{i}}
for every
i<\alpha
, we get
p^{*}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
.
∎

Proof.

(Lemma
4.16
)

We will prove that
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
is
\alpha
-distributive. The proof that
\langle\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau},\leq_{F,\nu}\rangle
is
\alpha
-distributive is similar.

Let
\gamma<\alpha
and
\vec{D}=\langle D_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle
be a sequence of dense open subsets of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. Fix a pacing chain
\vec{X}=\langle X_{i}\colon i\leq\alpha\rangle
with
\vec{D}\in X_{0}
, and let us construct a paced sequence of conditions
\langle p_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle
, such that, for every
i<\gamma
,
p_{i+1}\in D_{i}
. Regarding the sequence
\langle\tau_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle
required in the definition of a paced sequence, we take
\tau_{i}=\tau
for every
i<\gamma
. In other words, we require, for every
i<\gamma
,
p_{i}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. This is possible by Lemma
4.15
.

For the construction, we pick
p_{0}
to be an arbitrary element of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. At limit steps, let
p_{i}=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}
.

p_{i}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
by Claim
4.13
.
At successor steps, assume that
p_{i}
has been constructed. Choose
p_{i+1}\geq p_{i}
in
X_{i+1}
such that
p_{i+1}\in D_{i}\cap\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. By extending
p_{i+1}
without adding points to the support or changing the Miller coordinates, we can assume that, for every
\xi\in\supp(p_{i+1})
,
{p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}\Vdash\chi_{X_{i+1}}(\alpha^{+})\in\dot{c}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}
. Note that such a change can be made so it keeps
p_{i+1}
inside
D_{i}\cap\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
(just add
\chi_{X_{i+1}}(\alpha^{+})
to each coding coordinate in the support of
p_{i+1}
).

This concludes the inductive construction. Let
p^{*}=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{i}
. Then
p^{*}\in\bigcap_{j<i}D_{i}
, as desired.
∎

Proof.

(Lemma
4.17
)

Assume that
\dot{C}
is a
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
-name for a club subset of
\alpha
. We argue that every condition
p\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
can be extended to a condition
p^{*}
which forces that
\dot{C}
contains a regular cardinal below
\alpha

.
Let
\vec{X}=\langle X_{i}\colon i\leq\alpha\rangle
be pacing chain with
\dot{C},p\in X_{0}
. Since
\alpha
is Mahlo in
V
, we can find a regular cardinal
\gamma<\alpha
such that
X_{\gamma}\cap\alpha=\gamma
. Let us argue that
p
can be extended to a condition
p^{*}
that forces that
\gamma\in\dot{C}
.

For every
i<\gamma
, let
D_{i}
be the dense open set of conditions that decide the
i
-th element of
\dot{C}
. Then
D_{i}\in X_{i+1}
, and, repeating the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
4.16
,
we can construct a paced sequence
\langle p_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
with respect to
\vec{X}\upharpoonright\gamma+1
, such that, for every
i<\gamma
,
p_{i+1}\in D_{i}
.

This concludes the inductive construction. Let
p^{*}=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{i}
. Then
p^{*}
decides the first
\gamma
-many elements of
\dot{C}
. Also, for every
i<\gamma
,
p^{*}
forces that the
i
-th element of
\dot{C}
is below
\gamma
, since
\chi_{X_{\gamma}}(\alpha)=\gamma
and
p_{i+1}\in X_{i+2}
. Thus,
p^{*}
forces that the
\gamma
-th element of
\dot{C}
is
\leq\gamma
, so it is exactly
\gamma
.
∎

We now proceed towards the proof of Lemma
4.18
, that is,
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
satisfies the
C
-fusion property. We follow a technique due to Friedman and Zdomskyy
[
12
]
.

Definition 4.21
.

Let
p\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
and
F\in[\tau]^{<\alpha}
,
F\subseteq\supp(p)
. Let
\sigma\colon F\to\alpha^{<\alpha}
. We define, if possible, an extension
(p)_{\sigma}\geq p
, with the same support, in the following inductive way: assume that
\xi\in\supp(p)
and
(p)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi
has been defined.

•

If
\xi\notin F
, let
(p)_{\sigma}(\xi)=p(\xi)
.

•

If
\xi\in F
and
(p)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\sigma(\xi)\in\dot{T}^{p}_{\xi}
, let
(p)_{\sigma}(\xi)={\left(\dot{T}^{p}_{\xi}\right)_{\sigma(\xi)}}^{\frown}\ \dot{c}^{p}_{\xi}
.

•

Else,
(p)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi+1
is not defined, and, in particular,
(p)_{\sigma}
is not defined.

We say that
\sigma

lies
on
p
if
(p)_{\sigma}
is defined.

Remark 4.22
.

We will actually prove the following strengthening of the
\alpha
C-Fusion property: for every
p\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
,
F\in[\tau]^{<\alpha}
,
\nu<\alpha
and sequence
\vec{D}=\langle D_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
of dense open subsets on
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
, there exist
q\geq_{F,\nu}p
, a pace chain
\vec{X}=\langle X_{i}\colon i\leq\alpha\rangle
and a club
C\subseteq\alpha
, such that:

(a)

q
is the exact upper bound of a
\leq_{F,\nu}
-increasing sequence of extensions of
p
,
\vec{p}=\langle p_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
, which is a paced fusion sequence with respect to
\vec{X}
, witnessed by some sequence
\vec{F}=\langle F_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
.

(b)

For every regular
i\in C
, and an
X_{i}
-generic set
G_{X_{i}}\subseteq X_{i}\cap\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
(in
V
) whose members are compatible with
q
, define a function

\sigma_{G_{X_{i}}}\colon F_{i}\to i^{i}
that maps each
\xi\in F_{i}
to the generic branch induced by
G_{X_{i}}
at coordinate
\xi
. I.e.,

\sigma_{G_{X_{i}}}(\xi)=\bigcup\{t\in i^{<i}\colon\exists r\in G_{X_{i}},r\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash t=\mbox{stem}(\dot{T}^{r}_{\xi})\}.

Then
\sigma_{G_{X_{i}}}
lies on
q
and
(q)_{\sigma_{G_{X_{i}}}}
is the exact upper bound of
G_{X_{i}}\cup\{q\}
, and
(q)_{\sigma_{G_{X_{i}}}}\in D_{i}
.

(c)

q
is a generic condition for
X_{\alpha}
.

(d)

\supp(q)=\bigcup_{i<\alpha}F_{i}\subseteq X_{\alpha}\cap\alpha^{++}
.
18
18

18

In fact, we can further have equality, see also footnote
22
.

(e)

q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. Moreover, the constant function
\vec{\mu}=\langle\mu_{\xi}\colon\xi\in\supp(q)\rangle
defined by setting for every
\xi\in\supp(q)
,
\mu_{\xi}=\chi_{X_{\alpha}}(\alpha^{+})
, is a local bounding function for
q
.

(f)

X_{\alpha}\cap\alpha^{+}=\chi_{X_{\alpha}}(\alpha^{+})
.

This stronger version of the
\alpha
C-Fusion property will be used in the proof of Lemmas
4.19
and
4.20
.

Proof.

(Lemma
4.18
)

Assume that
\vec{D}=\langle D_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle

\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}

,

,
\langle F_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle

.

19

19

.

As in the proof of case 4 in lemma

.

First, let

be a pace chain such that

.
20
20

20

Note that
X_{\alpha}
satisfies that
\chi_{X_{\alpha}}(\alpha^{+})\subseteq X_{\alpha}
, since, for every

for every

above the maximum of

holds.
Let

,

. In limit steps, we will take

and

Such

and

. In particular, for every

{r\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{r}_{\xi}\Vdash\sup(\dot{c}^{r}_{\xi})>\gamma

,
{p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}\Vdash\chi_{X_{i+1}}(\alpha^{+})\in\dot{c}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}
