Claim 4.23
.

Assume that
\sigma\colon F_{i}\to\alpha^{\alpha}
belongs to
X_{i+1}
, and there exists
r\geq p_{i+1}
such that:

1.

\sigma
lies on
r
.

2.

(r)_{\sigma}=r
.

3.

For every
\xi\in F_{i}
,

r\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash"\sigma(\xi)\mbox{ is an immediate successor of an element in }\mbox{Split}_{\nu+i}\left(\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}\right)".

If
\mbox{lh}(\sigma(\xi))
is regular for every
\xi\in F_{i}
, we can replace this assumption with the simpler assumption that for every
\xi\in F_{i}
,

r\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash"\sigma(\xi)\in\mbox{Split}_{\nu+i}\left(\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}}_{\xi}\right)"
.

Then
\sigma
lies on
p_{i+1}
,
r=(p_{i+1})_{\sigma}
satisfies properties 1-3 above with respect to
\sigma
, and moreover,

\left(p_{i+1}\right)_{\sigma}\in D^{*}_{i}:=D_{i}\cap\left(\bigcap\{D\in X_{i}\colon D\mbox{ is a dense-open subset of }\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}\}\right).

Proof.

Let
E
be the set of conditions
s\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
for which one of the following hold:

1.

There is no
r\geq s
that satisfies properties
1-3
above, with respect to
\sigma
.

2.

\sigma
lies on
s
and
(s)_{\sigma}
extends a condition
r\geq p_{i+1}
satisfying properties 1-3 above, and
r
belongs to
D^{*}_{i}
.

E\in X_{i+1}
as
X_{i}\in X_{i+1}
, so it is sufficient to prove that
E
is
\leq_{F_{i},\nu+i}
-dense open above
p_{i}
. To see that this suffices, note that
E\in X_{i+1}
and
p_{i+1}\geq_{F_{i},\nu+i}p_{i}
is
\leq_{F_{i},\nu+i}
-generic over
X_{i+1}
, and thus
p_{i+1}\in E
. Therefore, by the assumptions of the claim, we have that
(p_{i+1})_{\sigma}
satisfies properties 1-3 with respect to
\sigma
, and
(p_{i+1})_{\sigma}\in D^{*}_{i}
, as desired.

It’s not hard to see that
E
is open. Thus, let us concentrate on the proof of the
\leq_{F_{i},\nu+i}
-density of
E
. Assume that
s
is a condition,
s\geq_{F_{i},\nu+i}p_{i}
, and let us argue that there exists
s^{*}\geq_{F_{i},\nu+i}s
in
E
. Assume that there exists
r\geq s
such that properties
1-3
above hold. Since
D^{*}_{i}
is dense open (by
\alpha
-distributivity of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
), we can find
r^{*}\geq r
in
D^{*}_{i}
. Note that
\sigma
lies on
r^{*}
and
(r^{*})_{\sigma}=r^{*}
. Now, let
s^{*}\geq_{F_{i},\nu+i}s
be the amalgamation between
r^{*},s
, defined as follows:
s^{*}
has the same support as
r^{*}
, and, for every
\xi
in this support,

s^{*}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{s^{*}}_{\xi}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left(\dot{T}^{s}_{\xi}\setminus\left(\dot{T}^{s}_{\xi}\right)_{\sigma(\xi)}\right)\cup\dot{T}^{r^{*}}_{\xi}&\mbox{if }\xi\in F_{i}\mbox{ and }r^{*}\upharpoonright\xi\in\dot{G}(\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi})\\
\dot{T}^{r^{*}}_{\xi}&\mbox{if }\xi\notin F_{i}\mbox{ and }r^{*}\upharpoonright\xi\in\dot{G}(\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi})\\
\dot{T}^{s}_{\xi}&\mbox{if }r^{*}\upharpoonright\xi\notin\dot{G}(\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi})\end{array}\right.

where, in the notations above,
G(\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi})
denotes the generic set up to coordinate
\xi
. Also, set–

{s^{*}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{s^{*}}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{s^{*}}_{\xi}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\dot{c}^{r^{*}}_{\xi}&\mbox{if }r^{*}\upharpoonright\xi\in\dot{G}(\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi})\\
\dot{c}^{s}_{\xi}&\mbox{else}\end{array}\right.

Let us justify that indeed
s^{*}\geq_{F_{i},\nu+i}s
. Assume that
\xi\in F_{i}
. We argue that
s^{*}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{s^{*}}_{\xi}\geq_{\nu+i}\dot{T}^{s}_{\xi}
. This is clear if
r^{*}\upharpoonright\xi\notin G(\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi})
, so assume
r^{*}\upharpoonright\xi\in G(\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi})
. In this case, the tree
\dot{T}^{s^{*}}_{\xi}
is obtained by shrinking
\dot{T}^{s}_{\xi}
only above a successor of its splitting node in
\mbox{Split}_{\nu+i}\left(\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\right)=\mbox{Split}_{\nu+i}\left(\dot{T}^{s}_{\xi}\right)
. Thus,
this is an
\leq_{\nu+i}
-extension.
21
21

21

If
\sigma(\xi)
is regular for every
\xi\in F_{i}
, we can shrink
\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
above
\sigma(\xi)
(instead shrinking above one of its immediate successors) and get a
\leq_{\nu+i}
-extension, since
\sigma(\xi)
is not a splitting node.

It remains to check that
\sigma
lies on
s^{*}
, and
(s^{*})_{\sigma}\geq r^{*}
. Since
D^{*}_{i}
is open and contains
r^{*}
, this also ensures that
(s^{*})_{\sigma}\in D^{*}_{i}
.
We argue by induction that for every
\xi\in\supp(s^{*})
, if
(s^{*})_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi
is defined, then it extends
r^{*}\upharpoonright\xi
, and
(s^{*})_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\sigma(\xi)\in\dot{T}^{s^{*}}_{\xi}
. Since this is clear for
\xi\notin F_{i}
, we can assume
\xi\in F_{i}
. Take any
\eta<\xi
. By induction,
(s^{*})_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\eta
is defined and extends
r^{*}\upharpoonright\eta
. So
(s^{*})_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\eta
forces that
r^{*}\upharpoonright\eta\in\dot{G}(\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\eta})
, and, whether or not
\eta\in F_{i}
holds, we have, by our construction,
(s^{*})_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\eta\Vdash\left(\dot{T}^{s^{*}}_{\eta}\right)_{\sigma(\eta)}\subseteq\dot{T}^{r^{*}}_{\eta}
, and
{(s^{*})_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\eta}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{(s^{*})_{\sigma}}_{\eta}\Vdash\dot{c}^{s^{*}}_{\eta}=\dot{c}^{r^{*}}_{\eta}
. Since this is true for every
\eta<\xi
, we get that
(s^{*})_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi
extends
r^{*}\upharpoonright\xi
. Having this, it follows that
(s^{*})_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash r^{*}\upharpoonright\xi\in\dot{G}(\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi})
, and thus
(s^{*})_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\sigma(\xi)\in\dot{T}^{s^{*}}_{\xi}
, as desired.
∎

This concludes the construction of the sequences
\vec{p}=\langle p_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
,
\vec{F}=\langle F_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
. By the parallel argument from the proof of case 4 of Lemma
4.6
,
\bigcup_{i<\alpha}\supp(p_{i})=\bigcup_{i<\alpha}F_{i}
. So
\vec{p}
is indeed a paced fusion sequence with respect to
\vec{X}
, witnessed by
\vec{F}
.

Let
q=\bigvee_{i<\alpha}p_{i}
.
q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
as a limit of a paced fusion sequence of conditions. We are now ready to finish the proof of Lemma
4.18
with the following two claims: the first isolates a club
C\subseteq\alpha
, and the second shows that
q,\vec{X},C
are witnesses for the
\alpha
C-Fusion property.

Claim 4.24
.

There exists a club
C\subseteq\alpha
such that, for every
i\in C
,

1.

X_{i}\cap\alpha=i
.

2.

F_{i}\subseteq X_{i}
.
22
22

22

We can actually strengthen this to
F_{i}=X_{i}\cap\alpha^{++}
, since
\{i<\alpha\colon F_{i}=X_{i}\cap\alpha^{++}\}
is a club in
\alpha
. To see this, note first that for every
i<\alpha
,
X_{i}\cap\alpha^{++}\subseteq\supp(p_{i+1})
by
\leq_{F_{i},\nu+i}
-genericity of
p_{i+1}
over
X_{i}
. Since
|X_{i}|<\alpha
and
\bigcup_{j<i}\supp(p_{j})=\bigcup_{j<i}F_{j}
, we deduce that for some
j<\alpha
,
X_{i}\cap\alpha^{++}\subseteq F_{j}
. Now, assuming that we have established that
\{i<\alpha\colon F_{i}\subseteq X_{i}\}
is a club, shrink it by intersecting with the set of closure points of the map sending
i
to the least
j
with
X_{i}\cap\alpha^{++}\subseteq F_{j}
.

3.

For every
j<i
,
\xi\in X_{j}
and
t\in j^{j}
,

\displaystyle p_{i}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash"

\displaystyle\mbox{if }t\in\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\mbox{ then either }t\mbox{ is a splitting node of }\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}

\displaystyle\mbox{or the least splitting node of }\left(\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\right)_{t}\mbox{ belongs to }i^{<i}".

Proof.

It’s enough to make sure that each clause of the three could be fulfilled on a club. The first clause is standard. For the second clause, consider the map sending each
j<\alpha
to the least
i(j)<\alpha
such that
F_{j}\subseteq X_{i(j)}
. If this map is a well defined map from
\alpha
to
\alpha
, then every closure point
i
of it satisfies
F_{i}\subseteq X_{i}
. To justify that this map is well defined, assume that
j<\alpha
. For every
\xi\in F_{j}
, let
\gamma(\xi)<\alpha
be its index in the well order
W(F_{j})
. Then
F_{j}\subseteq X_{i(j)}
where
i(j)=\max\left(j,\sup_{\xi\in F_{j}}\gamma(\xi)\right)+1<\alpha
.

Finally, we justify that the last clause holds on a club. Fix some
j<\alpha
. For every
\xi\in X_{j}
let

\displaystyle E(\xi)=\{r\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}\colon

\displaystyle\mbox{there exists }i<\alpha\mbox{ such that }r\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash"\mbox{if }t\in\dot{T}^{p_{j}}_{\xi}\cap j^{j},\mbox{ then either }

\displaystyle t\mbox{ is a splitting node of }\dot{T}^{p_{j}}_{\xi},\mbox{ or the least splitting node of }

\displaystyle\left(\dot{T}^{p_{j}}_{\xi}\right)_{t}\mbox{ belongs to }i^{<i}."\}

By using the inductive assumption of Lemma
4.19
for
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi}
to determine a suitable
i<\alpha
in
V
, we see that
E(\xi)
is
\leq_{F_{j},\nu+j}
-dense open above
p_{j}
. By distributivity,
E=\bigcap_{\xi\in X_{j}}E(\xi)
is
\leq_{F_{j},\nu+j}
-dense open and belongs to
X_{j+1}
. Thus
p_{j+1}
meets
E
. Let
i^{*}(j)<\alpha
be minimal
i
such that for every
\xi\in X_{j}
and
t\in j^{j}
,
p_{j+1}\upharpoonright\xi
forces that if
t\in\dot{T}^{q_{j}}_{\xi}
, then
t
is either a splitting node, or the least splitting node above
t
belongs to
i^{*}(j)^{<i^{*}(j)}
. We argue that every closure point
i
of the map
j\mapsto i^{*}(j)
satisfies the third clause.

Indeed, fix such a closure point
i<\alpha
. Assume that
j<i
,
\xi\in X_{j}
,
t\in j^{j}
and
G(\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\xi})
is
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi}
-generic over
V
with
p_{i}\upharpoonright\xi\in G(\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\xi})
. Assume that in
V[G(\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\xi})]
,
t\in{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
is not a splitting node. Since
t\in j^{j}
and
T^{p_{j}}_{\xi}\leq_{\nu+j}T^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
, we have
t\in{T}^{p_{j}}_{\xi}
. Since
p_{i}\geq p_{j+1}
and
p_{j+1}\in E(\xi)
, we have
p_{i}\in E(\xi)
. This, combined with the fact that
p_{i}\upharpoonright\xi\in G(\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\xi})
, implies that in
V[G(\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\xi})]
, the least splitting node above
t
in
{T}^{p_{j}}_{\xi}
belongs to
i(j)^{<i(j)}
. The same splitting node is the least splitting node above
t
in
{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
(again, because
T^{p_{j}}_{\xi}\leq_{\nu+j}T^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
) and indeed it belongs to
i^{<i}
.
∎

Claim 4.25
.

The following properties hold for
q=\bigvee_{i<\alpha}p_{i}
:

1.

For every regular
i\in C
and an
X_{i}
-generic set
G_{X_{i}}
whose members are compatible with
q
,
G_{X_{i}}\cup\{q\}
has an exact upper bound, and this exact upper bound belongs to
D_{i}
.

2.

q
is a generic condition for
X_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

1.

Let
\sigma=\sigma_{G_{X_{i}}}\colon F_{i}\to i^{i}
be a function, such that for every
\xi\in F_{i}
,
\sigma_{G_{X_{i}}}(\xi)
is the initial segment of the generic branch decided by
G_{X_{i}}
at coordinate
\xi
. Namely,

\sigma_{G_{X_{i}}}(\xi)=\bigcup\{t\colon\exists r\in G_{X_{i}},r\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\left(\dot{T}^{r}_{\xi}\right)_{t}=\dot{T}^{r}_{\xi}\}.

Since
G_{X_{i}}
is
X_{i}
-generic and
X_{i}\cap\alpha=i
, we have for every
\xi\in F_{i}
,
\sigma(\xi)\in i^{i}
. Since every sequence of length
i
whose elements are in
X_{i}
belongs to
X_{i+1}
,
\sigma\in X_{i+1}
.

We argue that
\sigma
lies on
q
, and
(q)_{\sigma}
belongs to
D_{i}
and is an exact upper bound of
G_{X_{i}}\cup\{q\}
.

The fact that
q
is compatible with all members of
G_{X_{i}}
ensures that for every
\xi\in F_{i}
, all initial segments of
\sigma(\xi)
are forced to be in
T^{q}_{\xi}
. Since
T^{q}_{\xi}
is closed the same holds for
\sigma(\xi)
, and thus
\sigma
lies on
q
.

In order to prove that
(q)_{\sigma}\in D_{i}
, we prove first that
\sigma
lies on
p_{i+1}
, and
r=(p_{i+1})_{\sigma}
satisfies the assumptions of Claim
4.23
.

•

\sigma
lies on
p_{i+1}
: this follows from the fact
\sigma\colon F_{i}\to i^{i}
lies on
q
, and
q\geq_{F_{i},\nu+i}p_{i+1}
.

•

(r)_{\sigma}=r
: immediate from the fact that
r=(p_{i+1})_{\sigma}
.

•

For every
\xi\in F_{i}
,
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\sigma(\xi)\in\mbox{Split}_{\nu+i}\left(\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}\right)
: assume that
\xi\in F_{i}
and, by contradiction, there exists some
j<i
and an extension
s
of
r\upharpoonright\xi
that forces that the splitting nodes of
\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
along
\sigma(\xi)
are contained in
j^{j}
. By extending
j
if necessary, we can assume that
j
is a closure point of
\sigma(\xi)
, and denote
t=\sigma(\xi)\upharpoonright j\in j^{j}
. So
s
extends
p_{i}\upharpoonright\xi
, forces that
t\in\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
and
t
is not a splitting point. Since
i\in C
, it follows that the least splitting node of
\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
above
t
belongs to
i^{<i}
. This contradicts the fact that
r\upharpoonright\xi
forces that all the splitting nodes of
\dot{T}^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
along
\sigma(\xi)
are initial segments of
t
.

By Claim
4.23
,
(p_{i+1})_{\sigma}\in D_{i}
. Since
D_{i}
is open, it follows that
(q)_{\sigma}\in D_{i}
. It remains to verify that
(q)_{\sigma}
is the exact upper bound of
G_{X_{i}}\cup\{q\}
.

In order to argue that it is an upper bound, we show that for every
s\in G_{X_{i}}
,
s\leq(q)_{\sigma}
. Indeed, given such
s
, the dense set
D(s)
of conditions which are either incompatible or extend
s
belongs to
X_{i}
. Thus,
D(s)\subseteq D^{*}_{j}
for some
j<i
with
X_{j}\cap\alpha=j
.
23
23

23

Since
i
is regular,
\langle X_{k}\colon k<i\rangle
is continuous and
X_{i}\cap\alpha=i
, there exists such
j<i
.
By repeating a similar argument as above,
\sigma^{\prime}
lies on
p_{j+1}
, where, for every
\xi\in F_{j}
,
\sigma^{\prime}\colon F_{j}\to\alpha^{\alpha}
is given by taking
\sigma^{\prime}(\xi)
to be the initial segment of
\sigma(\xi)
which is the immediate successor of an element in
\mbox{Split}_{j}\left(\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}\right)
. Appealing to Claim
4.23
,
(p_{j+1})_{\sigma^{\prime}}\in D(s)
, and as both
(p_{j+1})_{\sigma^{\prime}},s
belong to
G_{X_{i}}
, they must be compatible. Thus,
(p_{j+1})_{\sigma^{\prime}}
extends
s
, and, in particular,
(q)_{\sigma}
extends
s
.

The only thing left to be checked is that
(q)_{\sigma}
is an exact upper bound of
G_{X_{i}}\cup\{q\}
. Assume that
q^{\prime}
is an upper bound of
G_{X_{i}}\cup\{q\}
. Since
q^{\prime}
extends each condition in
G_{X_{i}}
,
\sigma
lies on
q^{\prime}
and
(q^{\prime})_{\sigma}=q^{\prime}
. Since, in addition,
q^{\prime}
extends
q
, we can deduce that
q^{\prime}\geq(q)_{\sigma}
, as desired.

2.

Assume
G\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
is generic over
V
with
q\in G
. We argue that
G\cap X_{\alpha}
is
X_{\alpha}
-generic. Let
D\in X_{\alpha}
be a dense open set. In
V[G]
, denote by
E
the set of all
i<\alpha
such that for every
\xi\in F_{i}
,
i
is a closure point of the function in
\alpha^{\alpha}
given by the generic branch of
G
at coordinate
\xi
. It is not hard to verify that
E
is a club in
\alpha
, and, since
\alpha
remains Mahlo in
V[G]
(by Lemma
4.17
) we can find a regular
i\in C\cap E
with
D\in X_{i}
.

Let
\sigma\colon F_{i}\to i^{i}
be the function which maps each
\xi\in F_{i}
to the generic branch induced by
G
at coordinate
\xi
, restricted to
i
. Then
\sigma\in X_{i+1}
, and, by the above construction,
(p_{i+1})_{\sigma}\in D\cap G\cap X_{\alpha}
.

∎

This concludes the proof of Lemma
4.18
.
∎

Proof.

(Lemma
4.19
)

Fix
F,\nu
,
p\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
, and a
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
-name
\zeta
for an ordinal. Since
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
is
\alpha
-distributive and satisfies the
\alpha
C-fusion property, we can apply Lemma
2.8
(on the constant function
f\colon\alpha\to\{\zeta\}
in
V^{\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}}
), to find
q\geq p
and a set
x
of ordinals of size
|x|<\alpha
, such that
q\Vdash\dot{\zeta}\in\check{x}
. Moreover, as our proof of Lemma
4.18
can be applied with respect to the suborder
\geq_{F,\nu}
, we may assume that
q\geq_{F,\nu}p
.
∎

Definition 4.26
.

We define
\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
to be the set of conditions
q\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
for which there exists a sequence
\langle F_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle\subseteq[\supp(q)]^{<\alpha}
, such that:

1.

q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
, witnessed by a local bounding function
\vec{\mu}=\langle\mu_{\xi}\colon\xi\in\supp(q)\rangle
.

2.

Given
\xi\in\supp(q)
,
t\in\alpha^{<\alpha}
,
\beta<\mu_{\xi}
and
r\geq q
, such that
r\upharpoonright\xi\parallel\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}
and
{r\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{r}_{\xi}\parallel\check{\beta}\in\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}
, there exists a regular cardinal
i<\alpha
and
\sigma\colon F_{i}\to i^{i}
such that:

(a)

\sigma
lies on
q
.

(b)

(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi
decides the
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi}
-statements
``\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}"

(c)

{(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{(q)_{\sigma}}_{\xi}
decides the
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi}*\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}
-statement
``\check{\beta}\in\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}"
.

(d)

(q)_{\sigma},r
are compatible.

3.

For every
q^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
, if
q\geq q^{\prime}
and
q^{\prime}\geq q
then the least
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-name for
q
is lexicographically below the least
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-name for
q^{\prime}
, where on each coordinate
\xi<\alpha
, the coordinates are ordered with respect to the canonical well-order of
L[\bar{E}]
.

Proof.

(Lemma
4.20
)

We argue that
\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
is a dense subset of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
, and
|\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}|=\alpha^{+}
.

1.

\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
is dense: assume that
p\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
. We argue that there exists
q\geq p
in
\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
.

Apply the
C
-fusion property (on an arbitrary sequence
\vec{D}=\langle D_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
of dense open sets) to find an extension
q\geq p
,
q\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
and some club in
\alpha
, witnessing the
\alpha
C-Fusion property in the stronger sense of Remark
4.22
. In particular,
q=\bigvee_{i<\alpha}q_{i}
, where
\langle q_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
is a paced fusion sequence with respect to a pacing chain
\vec{X}=\langle X_{i}\colon i\leq\alpha\rangle
and some sequence
\vec{F}=\langle F_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle
.

We argue that
q
satisfies clauses
1-3
above. The first clause was already verified. We proceed to the second clause.

Fix
\xi\in\supp(q)
,
t\in\alpha^{<\alpha}
,
\beta<\chi_{X_{\alpha}}(\alpha^{+})
and some
r\geq q
as in clause 2 of Definition
4.26
. Pick
j<\alpha
high enough so that:

•

t\in j^{<j}
.

•

\xi\in X_{j}\cap F_{j}
(this holds for large enough
j<\alpha
since
\supp(q)=\bigcup_{i<\alpha}F_{i}\subseteq X_{\alpha}\cap\alpha^{++}
).

•

\beta\in X_{j}\cap\chi_{X_{j}}(\alpha^{+})
(this holds for large enough
j<\alpha
since
\chi_{X_{\alpha}}(\alpha^{+})\subseteq X_{\alpha}
).

Let
G\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
be a generic set with
r\in G
. By Lemma
2.7
, combined with the fact that
\alpha
remains Mahlo in
V[G]
, there exists a regular
i<\alpha
above
j
such that
G_{X_{i}}:=G\cap X_{i}\in V
is
X_{i}
-generic and
(q)_{\sigma}
is an exact upper bound of
(G_{X_{i}})\cup\{q\}
, where
\sigma=\sigma_{G_{X_{i}}}
is as in Remark
4.22
.
(q)_{\sigma}
and
r
are compatible, since both belong to
G
. Also,
(q)_{\sigma}
meets every dense open subset of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
which belongs to
X_{i}
. In order to verify that
q
satisfies clause 2 in the definition of
\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
, it remains to check that
(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi\parallel\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q_{i}}_{\xi}
and
{(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{(q)_{\sigma}}_{\xi}\parallel\check{\beta}\in\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}
.

We first verify that
(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi\parallel\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q_{i}}_{\xi}
. By the discussion above, for every
j<j^{\prime}<i
,
(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi\parallel\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q_{j^{\prime}}}_{\xi}
. If there exists
j<j^{\prime}<i
such that
(q)_{\sigma}\Vdash\check{t}\notin\dot{T}^{q_{j^{\prime}}}_{\xi}
, then
(q)_{\sigma}\Vdash\check{t}\notin\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}
. Assume otherwise. Then
(q)_{\sigma}\Vdash\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q_{i}}_{\xi}
.

Let us argue that there exists
j<j^{*}
and
t^{*}\in(j^{*})^{j^{*}}
which extends
t
, such that
(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\check{t}^{*}\in\mbox{Split}_{j^{*}}\left(\dot{T}^{q_{j^{*}}}_{\xi}\right)
. This will suffice, since
(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{q_{j^{*}}}_{\xi}\leq_{j^{*}}\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}
and thus it will follow that
(q)_{\sigma}\Vdash\check{t}^{*}\in\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}
, and in particular,
(q)_{\sigma}\Vdash\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}
.

To show the existence of
t^{*},j^{*}
as above, we construct in
X_{i}
an increasing sequence
\langle t_{n}\colon n<\omega\rangle
of extensions of
t
in
T^{p_{i}}_{\xi}
, a sequence of ordinals
\langle j_{n}\colon n<\omega\rangle
below
i
, and an increasing sequence of conditions
\langle p_{n}\colon n<\omega\rangle\subseteq G\cap X_{i}
, such that
j_{n}\geq\mbox{lh}(t_{n})
, and
p_{n+1}\Vdash\check{t}_{n+1}\in\mbox{Split}_{j_{n}}(T^{q_{j_{n}}}_{\xi})\cap X_{i}
. Start with
j_{0}=j
,
t_{0}=t
. Assuming
t_{n},j_{n},p_{n}
were picked, consider the set–

E=\{r\in\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}\colon\mbox{for some }s\in\alpha^{\alpha}\mbox{ which extends }t_{n},\ r\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\check{s}\in\mbox{Split}_{j_{n}}\left(T^{q_{j_{n}}}_{\xi}\right)\}\in X_{i}

which is a dense open subset of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
above
p_{n}
, and thus, it is met by some condition
p_{n+1}\in G\cap X_{i}
above
p_{n}
. Thus, we can pick
t_{n+1}\in X_{i}
to be a witness
s
for the fact that
p_{n+1}\in E
. So
p_{n+1}\Vdash\check{t}_{n+1}\in\mbox{Split}_{j_{n}}\left({T}^{q_{j_{n}}}_{\xi}\right)
. Finally, take
j_{n+1}<i
above
j_{n}
such that
t_{n+1}\in{j_{n+1}}^{j_{n+1}}
. This describes the construction of the sequence
\langle t_{n}\colon n<\omega\rangle
. Setting
t^{*}=\cup_{n<\omega}t_{n}\in X_{i}
and
j^{*}=\sup_{n<\omega}j_{n}<i
, we have
\mbox{lh}(t^{*})=j^{*}
,
and, since
(q)_{\sigma}
extends all the conditions
\langle p_{n}\colon n<\omega\rangle
,
(q)_{\sigma}\Vdash\check{t}^{*}\in\mbox{Split}_{j^{*}}\left(T^{q_{j^{*}}}_{\xi}\right)
. This concludes the proof that
(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi\parallel\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q_{i}}_{\xi}
.

Next, let us verify that
{(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{(q)_{\sigma}}_{\xi}\parallel\check{\beta}\in\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}
. The dense open subset of
{\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\xi}}*\dot{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}
consisting of conditions deciding whether
\check{\beta}\in\dot{c}^{q_{j}}_{\xi}
belongs to
X_{i}
, since
\beta,\xi,q_{j}\in X_{i}
. Thus
{(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{(q)_{\sigma}}_{\xi}\parallel\check{\beta}\in\dot{c}^{q_{j}}_{\xi}
. But

{(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{(q)_{\sigma}}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{q_{j}}_{\xi}=\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}\cap\max{\dot{c}^{q_{j}}_{\xi}}\mbox{ and }\check{\beta}<\chi_{X_{j}}(\alpha^{+})\leq\max{\dot{c}^{q_{j}}_{\xi}}

and thus we have
{(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{(q)_{\sigma}}_{\xi}\parallel\check{\beta}\in\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}
.

Finally, let us check that clause
3
is fulfilled. If there exists
q^{\prime}
such that
q^{\prime}\leq q\leq q^{\prime}
and
q^{\prime}
has a
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-name which is enumerated below the minimum
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-name for
q
(with respect to the constructibility order of
L[\bar{E}]
), replace
q
with
q^{\prime}
. We should argue that
q^{\prime}
still satisfies the previous clauses. First, note that
q^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
with the same local bounding function
\vec{\mu}
, since
q^{\prime}\leq q
. Next, it’s not hard to see that for every regular
i<\alpha
and
\sigma\colon F_{i}\to i^{i}
lying on
q
,
\sigma
lies on
q^{\prime}
as well, and
(q^{\prime})_{\sigma}\leq(q)_{\sigma}\leq(q^{\prime})_{\sigma}
. In particular, every statement forced by
(q)_{\sigma}
is also forced by
(q^{\prime})_{\sigma}
. Also, every condition
r
which is compatible with
(q)_{\sigma}
, is also compatible with
(q^{\prime})_{\sigma}
. This shows that
q^{\prime}
satisfies the second clause.

2.

|\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}|=\alpha^{+}
: we argue that every
q\in\mathbb{D}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
is uniquely determined by the following parameters:

•

The support of
q
,
\supp(q)\in[\tau]^{\leq\alpha}
.

•

A local bounding function for
q
,
\vec{\mu}=\langle\mu_{\xi}\colon\xi\in\supp(q)\rangle\in(\alpha^{+})^{\supp(q)}
.

•

A sequence
\vec{F}=\langle F_{i}\colon i<\alpha\rangle\in([\supp(q)]^{<\alpha})^{\alpha}
.

•

For every
\xi\in\supp(q)
and
t\in\alpha^{<\alpha}
, the function
F_{\xi,t}
whose domain is
\Sigma:=\bigcup_{i<\alpha}\left(i^{i}\right)^{F_{i}}
, where, for every
\sigma\in\Sigma
,

F_{\xi,t}(\sigma)=\begin{cases}0&\sigma\mbox{ lies on }q\mbox{ and }(q)_{\sigma}\Vdash\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}\\
1&\sigma\mbox{ lies on }q\mbox{ and }(q)_{\sigma}\Vdash\check{t}\notin\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}\\
2&\sigma\mbox{ does not lie on }q,\mbox{ or }\sigma\mbox{ lies on }q\mbox{ and }\neg(q)_{\sigma}\parallel\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}.\\
\end{cases}

•

For every
\xi\in\supp(q)
and
\beta<\mu_{\xi}
, the function
G_{\xi,\beta}
whose domain is
\Sigma
, where, for every
\sigma\in\Sigma
,

G_{\xi,\beta}(\sigma)=\begin{cases}0&\sigma\mbox{ lies on }q\mbox{ and }(q)_{\sigma}\Vdash\check{\beta}\in\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}\\
1&\sigma\mbox{ lies on }q\mbox{ and }(q)_{\sigma}\Vdash\check{\beta}\notin\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}\\
2&\sigma\mbox{ does not lie on }q,\mbox{ or }\sigma\mbox{ lies on }q\mbox{ and }\neg(q)_{\sigma}\parallel\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}.\\
\end{cases}

Since
\tau<\alpha^{++}
and
2^{\alpha}=\alpha^{+}
holds in
V
, the set of all sequences of possible parameters as above has size
\alpha^{+}
.

Let us argue that given
q,q^{\prime}\in\mathbb{D}^{*\alpha}_{\tau}
with the same parameters (support, local bounding function,
\vec{F}
, and functions
F_{\xi,t},G_{\xi,\beta}
),
q=q^{\prime}
. We prove this by showing that
q\leq q^{\prime}
and
q^{\prime}\leq q
.

Let us show that
q\geq q^{\prime}
, and by symmetry, we deduce that
q^{\prime}\geq q
as well. We prove by induction on
\xi\in\supp(q)=\supp(q^{\prime})
that
q\upharpoonright\xi\geq q^{\prime}\upharpoonright\xi
implies–

•

q\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}\geq\dot{T}^{q^{\prime}}_{\xi}
.

•

{q\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}\geq\dot{c}^{q^{\prime}}_{\xi}
.

Fix such
\xi
. Assume by contradiction that there exist
r\geq q
and
t\in\alpha^{<\alpha}
such that
r\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}\setminus\dot{T}^{q^{\prime}}_{\xi}
. Since
q\in\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\xi}
, there are regular
i<\alpha
and
\sigma\colon F_{i}\to i^{i}
which lies on
q
, such that
(q)_{\sigma}
and
r
are compatible, and
(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}
. But
q,q^{\prime}
share the same function
F_{\xi,t}(\sigma)
, so the above
\sigma
lies on
q^{\prime}
, and
(q^{\prime})_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q^{\prime}}_{\xi}
. But, since
q\upharpoonright\xi\geq q^{\prime}\upharpoonright\xi
we get
(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi\geq(q^{\prime})_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi
. So
(q)_{\sigma}\Vdash\check{t}\in\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}\cap\dot{T}^{q^{\prime}}_{\xi}
. This contradicts the fact that
r,(q)_{\sigma}
are compatible.

It’s left to show that
{q\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}\geq\dot{c}^{q^{\prime}}_{\xi}
. Assume by contradiction that there exists
r\geq q
and an ordinal
\beta
such that
{r\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{r}_{\xi}\Vdash\beta\leq\max\left(\dot{c}^{q^{\prime}}_{\xi}\right)\mbox{ and }\beta\in\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}\triangle\dot{c}^{q^{\prime}}_{\xi}
. Note that such
\beta
must satisfy
\beta<\mu_{\xi}
. Let
i<\alpha
be regular and
\sigma\colon F_{i}\to i^{i}
lying on
q
, such that
{(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{(q)_{\sigma}}_{\xi}
is compatible with
r
and decides whether
\check{\beta}\in\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}
. Since
G_{\xi,\beta}(\xi)\neq 2
,
\sigma
lies on
q^{\prime}
and
{(q^{\prime})_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{(q^{\prime})_{\sigma}}_{\xi}
also decides whether
\beta\in\dot{c}^{q^{\prime}}_{\xi}
, in the same way. As we already proved that
q\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{q}_{\xi}\geq\dot{T}^{q^{\prime}}_{\xi}
, we have
{(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{(q)_{\sigma}}_{\xi}\geq{(q^{\prime})_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{(q^{\prime})_{\sigma}}_{\xi}
. Thus
{(q)_{\sigma}\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{(q)_{\sigma}}_{\xi}\Vdash\check{\beta}\notin\dot{c}^{q}_{\xi}\triangle\dot{c}^{q^{\prime}}_{\xi}
, contradicting the fact that
(q)_{\sigma},r
are compatible.

∎

This concludes the inductive proof of Lemmas
4.15
-
4.20
.

Finally, let us define the dense subset We conclude this subsection by proving that
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
satisfies all the properties from the “Kunen-like” Blueprint
2.22
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\alpha^{++}}
be the set of all the conditions in
\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
for some
\tau<\alpha^{++}
. Then:

•

|\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}|=\alpha^{++}
and adds a
<^{\alpha}_{Sing}
-increasing sequence of length
\alpha^{++}
,

•

{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is
\alpha
-distributive, satisfies the
\alpha^{++}
.c.c, and preserves the Mahloness of
\alpha
,

•

{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
has the
\alpha
C-Fusion property,

•

{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is self coding, by the almost disjoint sequence of stationary sets

\vec{\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha}=\langle\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)\colon\tau<\alpha^{++},\eta<\alpha^{+},i<2\rangle\subseteq\alpha^{+}\cap\cf(\omega_{1}).

Furthermore, the self coding is uniformly definable over
(H_{\alpha^{++}})^{L[\bar{E}]}
, in the sense that there exists a parameter-free formula
\varphi_{\mathbb{Q}}(\alpha,x,y)
that defines the following over every inner model containing
(H_{\alpha^{++}})^{L[\bar{E}]}
:

–

an enumeration
\langle\dot{q}_{\eta}\colon\eta<\alpha^{+}\rangle
of
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-names for the conditions in
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
.

–

the sequence
\vec{\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha}
of stationary subsets of
\alpha^{+}
, used for coding.

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
is a dense subset of
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\alpha^{++}}
, it adds a
<^{\alpha}_{Sing}
-increasing sequence of Miller subset of
\alpha
; furthermore, it preserves the Mahloness of
\alpha
, and doesn’t new add
<\alpha
-sequences of ordinals. Thus,
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
is
\alpha
-distributive.
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
satisfies the
\alpha
-fusion property, since, as seen in the proof of Lemma
4.20
, every exact upper bound of a paced fusion sequence belongs to it (regardless of the initial sequence
\vec{D}
of dense open sets).

We argue that
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
satisfies the
\alpha^{++}-c.c.
. Assume that
\langle p_{\gamma}\colon\gamma<\alpha^{++}\rangle
is a sequence of conditions in
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
. By applying the
\Delta
-System Lemma (which is possible since
2^{\alpha}=\alpha^{+}
over
V
), we can assume that
\langle\supp(p_{\gamma})\colon\gamma<\alpha^{++}\rangle
is a delta-system with root
s\in[\alpha^{++}]^{\leq\alpha}
. Let
\tau=\sup(s)
. Then
\langle q_{\gamma}\upharpoonright\tau\colon\gamma<\alpha^{++}\rangle\subseteq\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
and
|\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}|=\alpha^{+}
. Thus, by picking
\gamma\neq\gamma^{\prime}
with
q_{\gamma}\upharpoonright\tau,q_{\gamma^{\prime}}\upharpoonright\tau
compatible, we get that
q_{\gamma},q_{\gamma^{\prime}}
are compatible as well.

It remains to check that
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
is self coding. For every
p\in\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
, let
\tau<\alpha^{++}
be the least such that
p\in\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
, and let
\eta<\alpha^{+}
be the enumeration of
p
in the well-order on
\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
induced by minimal
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-names, as described in Definition
4.9
. Those minimal
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-names belong to
(H_{\alpha^{++}})^{L[\bar{E}]}
(this follows since conditions in
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
belong to
(H_{\alpha^{++}})^{V}
, and since
|\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}|=\alpha^{+}
, they have
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-names in
(H_{\alpha^{++}})^{L[\bar{E}]}
). This shows that the list minimal
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-names for conditions of
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
can be enumerated in a definable way over
(H_{\alpha^{++}})^{L[\bar{E}]}
. Also, the stationary sets used for coding, and their correspondence with elements of
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
, are all definable over
H_{\alpha^{++}}
. This shows that In order to complete the self-coding verification, it’s enough to argue that–

0_{\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}}\Vdash p\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha})\iff\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,0)\mbox{ is nonstationary}

and, similarly–

0_{\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}}\Vdash p\notin G(\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha})\iff\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,1)\mbox{ is nonstationary}.

Assuming that
p\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha})
, the generic coding poset at coordinate
\tau
adds club disjoint from
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,0)
. Thus, we concentrate on the other direction and prove that–

0_{\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}}\Vdash p\notin G(\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha})\implies\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,0)\mbox{ is stationary}.

First, by Lemma
4.11
,
\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}
preserves the stationarity of
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,0)
. Once we will prove that
\mathbb{R}_{\tau}:=\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}*\dot{\mathbb{C}}_{\tau}
preserves the stationarity of
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,0)
, the same argument of Lemma
4.11
shows that
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,0)
is preserved in
V^{\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}}
. Thus, it suffices to prove that, over
V^{\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}}
,
\mathbb{R}_{\tau}
preserves the stationarity of
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,0)
. To show this, assume that
r\in\mathbb{R}_{\tau}
and
\dot{C}
is a
\mathbb{R}_{\tau}
-name for a club subset of
\alpha^{+}
. We argue that
r
can be extended to a condition which forces that
\dot{C}\cap\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,0)\neq\emptyset
.

Take an elementary substructure
X\prec(H_{\theta})^{V^{\hat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\alpha}_{\tau}}}
(for
\theta
large enough) such that:

•

\mathbb{R}_{\tau},r,\dot{C},\alpha,\tau\in X
.

•

|X|=\alpha
.

•

X
is closed under countable sequences of its elements.

•

\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})\in\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,0)
.

•

\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})\subseteq X
.

Such
X
can be constructed as in the proof of Lemma
4.11
. Note that
\alpha
remains Mahlo in
V^{\mathbb{Q}^{\alpha}_{\tau}}
, so the closure under countable sequences can be ensured.

Fix a cofinal sequence
\langle\delta_{i}\colon i<\omega_{1}\rangle
in
\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})
. In
X
, construct an increasing sequence
\langle r_{i}=(T_{i},\dot{c}_{i})\colon i<\omega_{1}\rangle
of conditions in
\mathbb{R}_{\tau}
: take
r_{0}=r
, and at every limit stage
i<\omega_{1}
, use the
\sigma
-closure of
\mathbb{R}_{\tau}
and the closure under countable sequences of
X
, to pick an upper bound
p_{i}\in X
for
\langle p_{j}\colon j<i\rangle
. At successor stages, make sure that
r_{i+1}\parallel\min(\dot{C}\setminus\delta_{i})
and
T_{i+1}\Vdash\max(\dot{c}_{i+1})>\delta_{i}
.

This concludes the inductive construction. Let
r^{*}=(T^{*},\dot{c}^{*})
, where
T^{*}=\bigcap_{i<\omega_{1}}T_{i}
, and
\dot{c}^{*}=\left(\bigcup_{i<\omega_{1}}\dot{c}_{i}\right)\cup\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})
. We argue that
r^{*}\in\mathbb{R}
. First,
T^{*}\in\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}
by
\alpha
-closure of
\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}
. So it suffices to justify that
T^{*}\Vdash\dot{c}^{*}\in\dot{\mathbb{C}}_{\tau}
. Indeed,

\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta^{\prime},j)
whenever
\eta^{\prime}\neq\eta
or
(\eta^{\prime}=\eta\land j=1)
, since
\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})\in\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,0)
and
\langle\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta^{\prime},j)\colon\eta^{\prime}<\alpha^{+},j<2\rangle
are pairwise disjoint.

Finally,
r^{*}
forces that
\chi_{X}(\alpha^{+})
is a limit point of
\dot{C}
. Thus
r^{*}\Vdash\dot{C}\cap\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,0)\neq\emptyset
, as desired.
∎

4.3
The Final Forcing
\mathbb{P}=\mathbb{P}_{\kappa+1}
and its Properties

In this section we conclude the construction of the forcing notion
\mathbb{P}
over
L[\bar{E}]
which realizes the “Kunen-like” blueprint.

We define a nonstationary support iteration of length
\kappa+1
,
\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}\colon\alpha\leq\kappa+1\rangle
. For every non-Mahlo
\alpha<\kappa
,
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is taken to be the trivial forcing. For every Mahlo
\alpha\leq\kappa
,
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
is taken to be the forcing given in the statement of Theorem
4.1
. Recall that such a forcing
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
depends on a sequence
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
of almost disjoint stationary sets in
V^{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}}
. Moreover, as required in the definition of the “Kunen-like” blueprint, the forcings
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
(and, consequently, the stationary sets
\vec{\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha}
) should be uniformly defined, in the sense of Clause 4 in Definition
2.12
.

Once we will provide the proper definition of the above stationary sets, we will let
\mathbb{P}=\mathbb{P}_{\kappa+1}
.

This section is devoted to the proof that the properties of the Friedman-Magidor and “Kunen-like” blueprints are fulfilled by
\mathbb{P}
. The structure of this section is as follows:

•

In Lemma
4.27
we will define the stationary sets
\vec{\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha}
for every Mahlo
\alpha\leq\kappa
. By that, we complete the definition of
\mathbb{P}_{\kappa+1}
. We remark that the sets are stationary in
L[\bar{E}]
. In Lemma
4.32
we prove that, for every
\alpha\leq\kappa
Mahlo, the stationarity of the sets
\vec{\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha}
is preserved by the forcing
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
.

•

In Lemma
4.30
we prove that, for every Mahlo
\lambda\leq\kappa
,
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
satisfies the
\lambda
Iteration-Fusion property.

•

In Lemma
4.31
we prove that for every
\lambda\leq\kappa
,
\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}}\left(\mathbb{P}/\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}\right)
is
\lambda
-distributive.

•

With the Lemmas established, we prove the our main Theorem
1.1
.

Lemma 4.27
.

For every
\alpha\leq\kappa
, there exists in
L[\bar{E}]
a sequence

\vec{\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha}=\langle\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)\colon\tau<\alpha^{++},\eta<\alpha^{+},i<2\rangle

of almost disjoint stationary subsets of
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
(see Proposition
3.3
) such that,

1.

For every
\tau<\alpha^{++}
,
\langle\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)\colon\eta<\alpha^{+},i<2\rangle
are fully pairwise disjoint.

2.

For every
\tau<\alpha^{++},\eta<\alpha^{+}
and
i<2
,
\min(\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i))>\eta
.

3.

The sequence is uniformly defined, in the sense that there exists a formula
\varphi_{\mathcal{S}}(x,y)
such that
\varphi_{\mathcal{S}}(\alpha,y)
defines
\vec{\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha}
in every inner model that contains
H_{\alpha^{++}}
.

Proof.

Recall that for every
\alpha\leq\kappa
,
L[\bar{E}]
contains a
\Diamond\left(\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}\right)
-sequence which is definable over
H_{\alpha^{++}}
from
\alpha
. Denote the
least such a sequence in the canonical well ordering of
L[\bar{E}]
by
\langle A^{\alpha}_{\xi}\colon\xi<\alpha^{+}\rangle
. For every subset
B\subseteq\alpha^{+}
, let

\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(B)=\{\xi\in\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}\colon B\cap\xi=A^{\alpha}_{\xi}\}.

Then
\langle\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(B)\colon B\in\mathcal{P}\left(\alpha^{+}\right)\rangle
is a sequence of almost disjoint stationary subsets of
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
. The almost disjointness follows since, for every pair of subsets
B\neq B^{\prime}
of
\alpha^{+}
,
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(B)\cap\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(B^{\prime})\subseteq\min B\triangle B^{\prime}
.

Finally, pick the least constructible bijection from
(\mathcal{P}(\alpha^{+}))^{L[\bar{E}]}
to
\alpha^{++}\times\alpha^{+}\times 2
, and use it to re-index the sequence
\langle\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(B)\colon B\subseteq\alpha^{+}\rangle
in a definable way, in the form
\langle\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)\colon\tau<\alpha^{++},\eta<\alpha^{+},i<2\rangle
. We can further assume that for every
\tau<\alpha^{++}
,

\langle\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)\colon\eta<\alpha^{+},i<2\rangle

are pairwise disjoint and for each
\eta<\alpha^{+},i<2
,
\min(\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i))>\eta
, by definably modifying the sequence. More formally, replace each
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)
with
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)\setminus\left(\gamma(\tau,\eta,i)\cup(\eta+1)\right)
, where–

\gamma(\tau,\eta,i)=\sup\Big\{\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i)\cap\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta^{\prime},j)\colon(\eta^{\prime},j)<_{lex}(\eta,i)\Big\}+1

is an ordinal below
\alpha^{+}
by almost disjointness.
∎

We now proceed towards the proofs of Lemmas
4.30
,
4.31
. Let us fix some notations. Assume that
p\in\mathbb{P}
(or
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
for some
\lambda\leq\kappa+1
) and
\alpha\in\supp(p)
is Mahlo. Then

p\upharpoonright\alpha\Vdash p(\alpha)\in\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}.

In particular, using the notations of the previous section, for every
\xi<\alpha^{++}
,

p\upharpoonright\alpha\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}}\left(p(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash_{\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}\upharpoonright\xi}\ p(\alpha)(\xi)=\langle\dot{T}^{p(\alpha)}_{\xi},\dot{c}^{p(\alpha)}_{\xi}\rangle\right)

where
\dot{T}^{p(\alpha)}_{\xi}
is forced to be a condition in the generalized Miller forcing in
V^{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}*{\dot{Q}^{\alpha}\upharpoonright\xi}}
, namely–

{p\upharpoonright\alpha}^{\frown}p(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{p(\alpha)}_{\xi}\in\dot{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}

and
\dot{c}^{p(\alpha)}_{\xi}
is forced to be a condition in a coding forcing in
V^{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}*{\dot{Q}^{\alpha}\upharpoonright\xi*\dot{\mathbb{M}}_{\alpha}}}
, namely–

{p\upharpoonright\alpha}^{\frown}\left({p(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p(\alpha)}_{\xi}\right)\Vdash\dot{c}^{p(\alpha)}_{\xi}\in\dot{\mathbb{C}}^{\alpha}_{\xi}

Definition 4.28
.

Assume that
\lambda\leq\kappa+1
and
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
. A
local bounding function
for
p
is a sequence
\vec{\mu}^{p}=\langle\mu^{p}_{\alpha}\colon\alpha\in\supp(p)\rangle\in\prod_{\alpha\in\supp(p)}\alpha^{+}
, such that for every Mahlo
\alpha\in\supp(p)
,

p\upharpoonright\alpha\Vdash\forall\xi\in\supp(p(\alpha)),{p(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p(\alpha)}_{\xi}\Vdash\sup\left(\dot{c}^{p(\alpha)}_{\xi}\right)<\mu^{p}_{\alpha}.

In other words, for every
\alpha\in\supp(p)
,
p\upharpoonright\alpha
forces that the constant function whose value is
\mu^{p}_{\alpha}
is a local bounding function for
p(\alpha)\in\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
, in the sense of Definition
4.12
. In the following Lemma, we argue that the subset of conditions equipped with a local bounding function is dense.

Lemma 4.29
.

Assume that
\lambda\leq\kappa+1
. Let
\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda}\subseteq\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
be the set of conditions having a local bounding function. Then
\bar{P}_{\lambda}
is dense.

Proof.

We work by induction on
\lambda
.

Assume that
\lambda=\alpha+1
is successor. Let
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
. In
V^{\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}}
,
p(\alpha)\in\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
, and, by Lemma
4.20
,
p(\alpha)
has a local bounding function in the sense of Definition
4.12
. In particular, there exists a
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
-name for an ordinal
\dot{\mu}<{\alpha}^{+}
such that, for every
\xi\in\supp(p(\alpha))
,

{p\upharpoonright\alpha}^{\frown}{p(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p(\alpha)}_{\xi}\Vdash\sup\left(\dot{c}^{p(\alpha)}_{\xi}\right)<\dot{\mu}.

Now extend
q\geq p\upharpoonright\alpha
such that
q\in\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha}
and
q
decides the value of
\mu
. Then
q^{\frown}p(\alpha)
extends
p
and belongs to
\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda}
.

Assume that
\lambda
is limit. We first address the case where
\lambda
is a regular cardinal, and then make the needed adjustments to the singular case.

We construct sequences–

•

\langle\alpha_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
an increasing, continuous, cofinal sequence in
\lambda
.

•

\langle p_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
a fusion sequence of conditions in
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
, namely, increasing in the order of
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
, and satisfying that for every
i<j
,
p_{j}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i}=p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i}
.

•

\langle C_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
, decreasing with respect to inclusion, each
C_{i}
is a club disjoint from the support of
p_{i}
.

We will also make sure during the construction that, for every
i<\lambda
,
p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i}\in\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha_{i}}
,
\alpha_{i}\notin\mbox{supp}(p_{i+1})
and
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i+1}\Vdash p_{i+1}\setminus\alpha_{i+1}=p\setminus\alpha_{i+1}
.

Start with
p_{0}=p
,
C_{0}
any club disjoint from its support, and
\alpha_{0}=0
.

Successor steps: assume that the
p_{i},C_{i},\alpha_{i}
were defined. Let
\alpha_{i+1}
be the first element in
C_{i}
strictly above
\alpha_{i}
. We define
p_{i+1}
. First, define
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i}=p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i}
.
\alpha_{i}
itself is outside the support of
p_{i+1}
. Denote
I_{i}=\left(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i+1}\right)
, and take
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright I_{i}
to be an extension of
p_{i}\upharpoonright I_{i}
, such that, for every
\beta
in its support, there exists an ordinal
\mu^{p_{i+1}}_{\beta}<\beta^{+}
such that–

p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\beta\Vdash\forall\xi\in\supp\left(p_{i+1}(\beta)\right),\ {p_{i+1}(\beta)\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}(\beta)}_{\xi}\Vdash\sup\left(\dot{c}^{p_{i+1}(\beta)}_{\xi}\right)<\mu^{p_{i+1}}_{\beta}.

This is possible by the induction hypothesis. The sequence of bounds
\langle\mu^{p_{i+1}}_{\beta}\colon\beta\in\supp(p_{i+1})\cap I_{i}\rangle
is a
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i}+1}
-name, but since
|\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i}+1}|=(\alpha_{i})^{++}
, we can assume as well that the sequence is known in
V
(by replacing each
\mu^{p_{i+1}}_{\beta}
with the supremum of all the possible values of
\dot{\mu}^{p_{i+1}}_{\beta}
as forced by conditions in
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i}+1}
). Finally, choose
p_{i+1}\setminus\alpha_{i+1}
such that
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i+1}\Vdash p_{i+1}\setminus\alpha_{i+1}=p\setminus\alpha_{i+1}
. This concludes the definition of
p_{i+1}
, and, by standard arguments, it has a nowhere stationary support. Let
C_{i+1}
be obtained from
C_{i}
by shrinking it so it is disjoint from this support.

We move on to the limit case. Assume that
i<\alpha
and
\langle p_{j},C_{j},\alpha_{j}\colon j<i\rangle
were defined. Let
\alpha_{i}=\sup\{\alpha_{j}\colon j<i\}
. We define
p_{i}
. First, take
p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i}=\bigcup_{j<i}p_{j}\upharpoonright\alpha_{j}+1
.
\alpha_{i}
itself will be outside the support of
p_{i}
, as well as all the ordinals
\langle\alpha_{j}\colon j<i\rangle
. Finally, set
p_{i}\setminus\alpha_{i}=p\setminus\alpha_{i}
. Then
p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i}\in\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha_{i}}
, since, for every
j<i
, we have by induction
p_{j}\upharpoonright\alpha_{j}\in\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha_{j}}
. Finally, take
C_{i}=\bigcap_{j<i}C_{j}
.

This concludes the construction. We finally take
p^{*}
to be the natural limit of the fusion sequence, namely
p^{*}=\bigcup_{i<\lambda}p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i}+1
. Then
p^{*}\in\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda}
, as desired.

For the case where
\lambda
is singular, fix an increasing, cofinal sequence
\langle\lambda_{i}\colon i<\cf(\lambda)\rangle
in
\lambda
. Repeat the above argument, only construct sequences of length
\cf(\lambda)
rather than
\lambda
, and choose the sequence of ordinals
\langle\alpha_{i}\colon i<\cf(\lambda)\rangle
such that, for every
i<\cf(\lambda)
,
\alpha_{i+1}
is the least element of
C_{i}
above
\max{\alpha_{i},\lambda_{i}}
.
∎

Lemma 4.30
.

For every
\lambda\leq\kappa
Mahlo,
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
satisfies the
\lambda
Iteration-Fusion property.

Proof.

Assume otherwise, and let
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
be a condition and
\vec{D}=\langle D_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
be a sequence such that
\langle p,\vec{D}\rangle
is the
least counterexample. In other words,
\langle p,\vec{D}\rangle
is chosen constructibly minimal such that
\vec{D}
is a sequence of dense open subsets of
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
, for which there are no
p^{*}\geq p
and club
C\subseteq\lambda
of coordinates
i\in C
such that the set–

\{s\in\mathbb{P}_{i+1}\colon s^{\frown}p^{*}\setminus i+1\in D_{i}\}

is a dense open subset of
\mathbb{P}_{i+1}
.

All the relevant parameters
\lambda,\mathbb{P}_{\lambda},\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda},p,\vec{D}
belong to
J_{\lambda^{+3}}
,
24
24

24

Actually, all the relevant parameters above already belong to
J^{\bar{E}}_{\lambda^{++}}
. We chose
J^{\bar{E}}_{\lambda^{+3}}
is order to use Proposition
3.7
.
and are lightface definable there:
\lambda
itself is definable as the third-to-last cardinal; the forcings
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda},\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda}
are definable from
\lambda
and from the sequence of canonical stationary sets
\langle\mathcal{S}^{\mu}\colon\mu\leq\lambda\mbox{ is Mahlo}\rangle
, which is lighface definable; finally, the pair
(p,\vec{D})
is definable as the constructible-least counterexample for the Iteration-Fusion property. Let
2\leq n<\omega
be such that the above parameters, and the fact that
(p,\vec{D})
is a counterexample for the Iteration-Fusion property, are
\Sigma_{n}
definable over
J^{\bar{E}}_{\lambda^{+3}}
without parameters.

Apply Proposition
3.7
to find an ordinal
\eta\in(\lambda,\lambda^{+})
such that
\eta\in\mathcal{S}^{\lambda,\lambda,n}
. In other words,
\cf(\eta)=\lambda
,
n_{\eta}\geq n
, and the collapsing structure
N_{\eta}
can be weakly
r\Sigma_{n+1}
-embedded into
J^{\bar{E}}_{\lambda^{+3}}
by an embedding
\pi
, such that all the above parameters belong to
\mbox{Im}(\pi)
. Let
\delta\in(\lambda,\lambda^{+})
be such that
p^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}=\{\delta\}
.

Let
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\lambda},\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}_{\lambda},\vec{D}^{\prime}=\langle D^{\prime}_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle,p^{\prime}\in N^{\bar{E}}_{\eta}
be the preimages under
\pi
of
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda},\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda},\vec{D},p
, respectively. Note that
p^{\prime}=p
as
\mbox{crit}(\pi)>\lambda
. Also,
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
is a nonstationary support iteration of the form
\langle\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\alpha},\dot{\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}_{\alpha}\colon\alpha\leq\kappa^{\prime}\rangle
in
N_{\eta}
. Since
\mbox{crit}(\pi)\geq\lambda
, we have, for every
\alpha<\lambda
,
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\alpha}=\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
. It follows that
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\subseteq\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
.

Our goal will be to produce a condition
p^{*}\in\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
extending
p
and a club
C\subseteq\lambda
, such that for every
i\in C
, the set
\{s\in\mathbb{P}_{i+1}\colon s^{\frown}p^{*}\setminus i+1\in D_{i}\}
is a dense subset of
\mathbb{P}_{i+1}
. This will contradict our initial assumptions.

In order to produce such
p^{*}
, We construct sequences
\vec{p}=\langle p_{i}\colon i\leq\lambda\rangle
,
\vec{C}=\langle C_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
,
\vec{\alpha}=\langle\alpha_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
,
\vec{\alpha}^{*}=\langle\alpha^{*}_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
and
\vec{\epsilon}=\langle\epsilon_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
, each strict initial segment of those sequences is inside
N_{\eta}
, such that:

1.

The sequences
\langle\alpha_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
,
\langle\alpha^{*}_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
are increasing sequences of ordinals, both cofinal in
\lambda
. Moreover,

(a)

The sequence
\langle\alpha_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
is also continuous.

(b)

The sequences interleave, in the sense that for every
i<\lambda
,
\alpha_{i}\leq\alpha^{*}_{i}<\alpha_{i+1}
.

(c)

For every
\alpha\geq\alpha^{*}_{i}
in
\supp(p_{i})
,
\alpha\in h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
.

In particular, By Lemma
3.20
,
25
25

25

By picking
\alpha_{0}
high enough, we will make sure that the transitive collapse of
h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times\delta]
belongs to
h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha_{0}\cup\{\delta\})]
, which is required in order to apply Lemma
3.20
.
there exists
\eta(\alpha)\in(\alpha,\alpha^{+})
such that
h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
transitively collapses to a structure
N_{\eta(\alpha)}
.

2.

The sequence
\langle\epsilon_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
is an increasing sequence of very good points in
N_{\eta}
, such that:

(a)

For every
i<\lambda
, and for every
\alpha\geq\alpha^{*}_{i}
in
\supp(p_{i})
,
\epsilon_{i}
defines a corresponding very good point
\epsilon_{i}(\alpha)
of
N_{\eta(\alpha)}
, which is associated to a club point
\eta_{i}(\alpha)\in c^{\alpha}_{\eta(\alpha)}\setminus\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
.

(b)

For every
i<\lambda
and
\alpha<\lambda
with
\alpha\in h^{N^{\bar{E}}_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
,

\langle\epsilon_{j}\colon j<i\rangle,\langle\eta_{j}(\alpha)\colon j\leq i\rangle\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i+1}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})].

3.

The sequence
\langle p_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
is a sequence of conditions in
\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
, such that:

(a)

For every
i<j<\lambda
,
p_{i}\leq p_{j}
and
p_{j}\upharpoonright(\alpha_{i}+1)=p_{i}\upharpoonright(\alpha_{i}+1)
.

(b)

For every
i<\lambda
and Mahlo
\alpha>\alpha_{i}
in
\supp(p_{i})
,

p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\alpha\Vdash\ \ \forall\xi<\alpha^{++},\ \ {p_{i+1}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\Vdash\eta_{i}(\alpha)\in\dot{c}^{p_{i+1}(\alpha)}_{\xi}.

(c)

For every
i<\lambda
,
\langle p_{j}\colon j\leq i\rangle\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n+1}[\omega\times(\alpha^{*}_{i}\cup\{\delta\})]

4.

The sequence
\langle C_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
is decreasing, continuous sequence of club subsets of
\lambda
, such that:

(a)

For every limit
i<\lambda
,
C_{i}=\bigcap_{j<i}C_{j}
.

(b)

For every
i<\lambda
,
C_{i+1}
is the constructibly least club subset of
C_{i}
disjoint from
\mbox{supp}(p_{i+1})
.

(c)

For every
i<j
,
\alpha_{j}\in C_{i}
.

Begin by picking
p_{0}
being the constructibly least extension of
p
in
\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
. Take
\alpha_{0}<\lambda
be the least such that the transitive collapse of
h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times\delta]
belongs to
h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha_{0}\cup\{\delta\})]
. Let
C_{0}
be the constructibly least club disjoint from
\supp(p_{0})
. By Corollary
3.27
, there exists
\alpha^{*}<\lambda
and a good point
\epsilon
such that:

•

For every
\alpha\geq\alpha^{*}
in
\supp(p_{0})
,
\alpha\in h^{N_{\eta}}_{n+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
. In particular,
h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon}_{n+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
is collapsed to a structure

for some

.

•

For every
\alpha
as above, the image of
\epsilon
