\epsilon(\alpha)
of
N_{\eta(\alpha)}
, which induces a club point
\eta_{0}(\alpha)\in c^{\alpha}_{\eta(\alpha)}\setminus\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
.

•

p_{0}\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon}[\omega\times(\alpha^{*}\cup\{\delta\})]

•

\epsilon
is a limit of more than
\omega_{1}
-many very good points of
N_{\eta}
.
26
26

26

This clause can be ensured since
\eta\in\mathcal{S}^{\lambda,\lambda,n}
, and in particular
\cf(\eta)>\omega_{1}
.

Let
\alpha^{*}_{0}\in C_{0}
be the least such
\alpha^{*}
, and let
\epsilon_{0}
be the least very good point
\epsilon
as above with respect to
\alpha_{0}
. Denote by
\eta_{0}(\alpha)
the club point induced by
\epsilon_{0}(\alpha)
.

We now proceed to the successor case. Assume that
p_{i},C_{i},\alpha_{i},\alpha^{*}_{i},\epsilon_{i}
were defined. Define first
\alpha_{i+1}=\min\left(C_{i}\setminus(\alpha^{*}_{i}+1)\right)
.
Let
\vec{\mu}^{i}=\langle\mu^{i}_{\alpha}\colon\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\rangle
be the constructibly least local bounding function for
p_{i}
. Since
p_{i}\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha^{*}_{i}\cup\{\delta\})]
, we have
\vec{\mu}^{i}\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha^{*}_{i}\cup\{\delta\})]
, and in particular, for every
\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\setminus\alpha_{i}
,
\mu^{i}_{\alpha}\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]\cap\alpha^{+}
, namely
\mu^{i}_{\alpha}<\eta_{i}(\alpha)
. Thus, we can define an extension
p^{\prime}_{i}\geq p_{i}
with the same support and the same Miller coordinates, by adding
\eta_{i}(\alpha)
to each of the coding components of
p_{i}(\alpha)
, for every
\alpha>\alpha_{i+1}
in
\supp(p_{i})
. More formally,
\supp(p^{\prime}_{i})=\supp(p_{i})
,
p^{\prime}_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i+1}+1=p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i+1}+1
, and, for every
\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\setminus\alpha_{i+1}+1
, it is forced by
p^{\prime}_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha
that for every
\xi<\alpha^{++}
,

{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi}\Vdash\dot{T}^{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}=\dot{T}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}

and–

{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}=\dot{c}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\cup\{\eta_{i}(\alpha)\}.

Note that
p^{\prime}_{i}\in\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
since for every
\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\setminus\alpha_{i+1}+1
,
\eta_{i}(\alpha)\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
, and
\langle\eta_{i}(\alpha)\colon\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\setminus\alpha_{i+1}+1\rangle\in N_{\eta}
. Furthermore,
\langle\eta_{i}(\alpha)\colon\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\setminus\alpha_{i+1}+1\rangle\in h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha_{i+1}\cup\{\delta\})]
and
p^{\prime}_{i}\in h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha_{i+1}\cup\{\delta\})]
, since
\alpha_{i+1}
itself belongs to
\supp(p_{i})\setminus\alpha^{*}_{i}
.
. Finally, let
p_{i+1}\geq p^{\prime}_{i}
be the constructibly least extension of
p^{\prime}_{i}
, such that
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i+1}+1=p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i+1}+1
, and–

p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i+1}+1\Vdash\exists s\in G(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i+1}+1}),\ s^{\frown}p_{i+1}\setminus(\alpha_{i+1}+1)\in D^{\prime}_{i}.

This concludes the construction of
p_{i+1}
. To finish the successor step, let
C_{i+1}
be the constructibly minimal club disjoint from
\supp(p_{i+1})
, and pick the least
\alpha^{*}_{i+1}\geq\alpha_{i+1}
and a very good point
\epsilon_{i+1}>\epsilon_{i}
such that:

•

For every
\alpha\geq\alpha^{*}_{i+1}
in
\supp(p_{i+1})
,
\alpha\in h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
. In particular,
h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
is collapsed to a structure
N_{\eta(\alpha)}
for some
\eta(\alpha)\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}
.

•

The image of
\epsilon_{i+1}
under the above collapse is a very good point
\epsilon_{i+1}(\alpha)
of
N_{\eta(\alpha)}
, which is a limit of (more than
\omega_{1}
) very good points.

•

\epsilon_{i+1}(\alpha)
induces a club point
\eta_{i+1}(\alpha)\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
.

•

p_{i+1},\langle\epsilon_{j}\colon j\leq i\rangle\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i+1}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha^{*}_{i+1}\cup\{\delta\})]
.

Additionaly, since
\epsilon_{i+1}
is a limit of very good points, we get, by coherence of the canonical square sequence, that each
\epsilon_{j}
(for
j\leq i
) collapses to a very good point in the transitive collapse of
h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i+1}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha^{*}_{i+1}\cup\{\delta\})]
. In particular, for every
\alpha\in\supp(p_{i+1})\setminus\alpha^{*}_{i+1}+1
,

\langle\eta_{j}(\alpha)\colon j\leq i\rangle\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i+1}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times\left(\alpha\cup\{\delta\}\right)].

Finally, let us proceed to the limit case. Assume that
i<\lambda
and

\langle p_{j}\colon j<i\rangle,\langle C_{j}\colon j<i\rangle,\langle\alpha_{j},\alpha^{*}_{j}\colon j<i\rangle\text{ and }\langle\epsilon_{j}\colon j<i\rangle

have all been constructed, and let us construct
p_{i},C_{i},\alpha_{i},\epsilon_{i}
. First take
\alpha_{i}=\sup\{\alpha_{j}\colon j<i\}
. We define
p_{i}
as follows:

•

\supp(p_{i})=\bigcup_{j<i}\supp(p_{j})
. In particular,
\alpha_{i}\notin\supp(p_{i})
as
\alpha_{i}\in\bigcap_{j<i}C_{j}
.

•

For every
j<i
,
p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha_{j}+1=p_{j}\upharpoonright\alpha_{j}+1
.

•

For every
\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\setminus\alpha_{i}+1
,

p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha\Vdash p_{i}(\alpha)=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}(\alpha)

where
\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}(\alpha)
is the least upper bound of
\langle p_{j}(\alpha)\colon j<i\rangle
in the forcing
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
, as defined in Definition
4.12
.

Let us justify that the above requirements define a legitimate condition in
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
. It’s clear that
p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i}+1\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i}+1}
, so let us concentrate on
p_{i}\setminus\alpha_{i}+1
.
Assume
\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\setminus\alpha_{i}+1
. Then

p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha\Vdash\forall\xi<\alpha^{++},p_{i}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}=\bigcap_{j<i,\alpha\in\supp(p_{j})}\dot{T}^{p_{j}(\alpha)}_{\xi}

where
\dot{T}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}
is a legitimate condition since
\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}
is
\alpha
-closed, and
\alpha>i
. Also,

\displaystyle p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha\Vdash\forall\xi<\alpha^{++},p_{i}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}=

\displaystyle\left(\bigcup_{j<i,\alpha\in\supp(p_{j})}\dot{c}^{p_{j}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\right)\cup\Big\{\sup\left(\bigcup_{j<i}\dot{c}^{p_{j}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\right)\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\bigcup_{j<i,\alpha\in\supp(p_{j})}\dot{c}^{p_{j}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\right)\cup\Big\{\sup_{j<i}\eta_{j}(\alpha)\Big\}.

Let us justify that for every
\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\setminus\alpha_{i}+1
,
\dot{c}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}
is a legitimate condition in the
\alpha
-th coding coordinate. First, note that
\langle\eta_{j}(\alpha)\colon j<i\rangle
is bounded in
\eta(\alpha)
: if this wasn’t the case, we would get that
\langle\eta_{j}\colon j<i\rangle
is unbounded in
\eta
, where
\eta_{j}\in c^{\kappa}_{\eta}
is the club point induced by the very good point
\epsilon_{j}
of
N_{\eta}
. But this as a contradiction to the choice of
\eta
in
\mathcal{S}^{\lambda,\lambda,n}
(we have
\cf(\eta)=\lambda
). Now, since
\langle\eta_{j}(\alpha)\colon j<i\rangle
is bounded in
\eta(\alpha)
, we have
\sup_{j<i}\eta_{j}(\alpha)\in c^{\alpha}_{\eta(\alpha)}
. By the definition of
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
, the only possible element of
\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
which is a limit point of
c^{\alpha}_{\eta(\alpha)}
, is the
\omega_{1}
-th limit point of
c^{\alpha}_{\eta(\alpha)}
(see Corollary
3.19
). However, each
\epsilon_{j}
(for
j<i
) was chosen such that it is a limit point of more than
\omega_{1}
-many very good points. So
\sup_{j<i}\eta_{j}(\alpha)\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
.

We conclude the limit step, as in the previous steps, by picking
\alpha^{*}_{i},\epsilon_{i}
such that:

•

For every
\alpha\geq\alpha^{*}_{i}
in
\supp(p_{i})
,
\alpha\in h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
. In particular,
h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
is collapsed to a structure
N_{\eta(\alpha)}
for some
\eta(\alpha)\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}
.

•

The image of
\epsilon_{i}
under the above collapse is a very good point
\epsilon_{i}(\alpha)
of
N_{\eta(\alpha)}
, which is a limit of more than
\omega_{1}
-many very good points.

•

\epsilon_{i}(\alpha)
induces a club point
\eta_{i}(\alpha)\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
.

•

p_{i},\langle\epsilon_{j}\colon j<i\rangle\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha^{*}_{i}\cup\{\delta\})]
.

This concludes the inductive construction. The sequence
\langle p_{i}\colon i<\lambda\rangle
is external to
N_{\eta}
, but each of its elements is a condition in
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\subseteq\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
. Thus, Let
p^{*}\in\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
be a condition such that
\supp(p^{*})=\bigcup_{i<\lambda}\supp(p_{i})
, and for every
i<\lambda
,
p^{*}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i}+1=p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i}+1
. Note that
\{\alpha_{i}\colon i<\lambda\}
form a club disjoint from
\supp(p^{*})
.

For every
i<\lambda
,
N_{\eta}
sees the condition
p_{i+1}
as the condition with the property that

\{s\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i}+1}\colon s^{\frown}p^{*}\setminus\alpha_{i}+1\in D^{\prime}_{i}\}

is a dense subset of
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\alpha_{i}+1}
. In particular, by applying
\pi\colon N_{\eta}\to L[\bar{E}]
, the same condition
p_{i+1}=\pi(p_{i+1})
, viewed as a condition in
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
, has the property that

\{s\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i}+1}\colon s^{\frown}p_{i+1}\setminus\alpha_{i}+1\in D_{i}\}

is a dense subset of
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i}+1}
.

Since
p^{*}
extends each condition
p_{i}
(
i<\lambda
),
p^{*}
has the property that for every
i<\lambda
,

\{s\in\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i}+1}\colon s^{\frown}p^{*}\setminus\alpha_{i}+1\in D_{i}\}

is a dense subset of
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{i}+1}
. This, contradicts our initial assumption by contradiction.
∎

Lemma 4.31
.

Assume that
\lambda\leq\kappa+1
is a cardinal and
\nu<\lambda
. Then
0_{\mathbb{P}_{\nu}}\Vdash\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}\setminus\mathbb{P}_{\nu}
is
\nu
-distributive.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
\lambda
that for every
\nu\leq\lambda\leq\kappa+1
,
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}\setminus\mathbb{P}_{\nu}
is
\nu
-distributive.

We begin by justifying the successor case. Assume that
\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}+1
. If
\lambda^{\prime}=\nu
,
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}\setminus\nu
is the forcing
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\nu}
over
V^{\mathbb{P}_{\nu}}
, which is known to be
\nu
-distributive. If
\lambda^{\prime}>\nu
,
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}\setminus{\nu}
is
\nu
-distributive since it can be factored to the form
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\setminus\nu*\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
, where
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\setminus\nu
is
\nu
-distributive (by induction) and
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
is
\lambda^{\prime}
-distributive.

Next, we take care of the case where
\lambda
is a limit ordinal which is not a limit of Mahlo cardinals. In this case,
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}=\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
where
\lambda^{\prime}<\lambda
is the supremum of the Mahlo cardinals below
\lambda
, and the induction hypothesis can be applied to show that
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\setminus\nu
is
\nu
-distributive.

We proceed to the main case and assume that
\lambda
is a limit of Mahlo cardinals. Assume by contradiction that
0_{\mathbb{P}_{\nu}}
does not force that
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}\setminus\nu
is
\nu
-distributive. So there are
s\in\mathbb{P}_{\nu}
, a cardinal
\gamma<\nu
and
\vec{\dot{D}}=\langle\dot{D}_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle
which form a counterexample for distributivity, in the sense that
s
forces that
\langle\dot{D}_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle
is a sequence of dense open subsets of
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}\setminus\nu
with an empty intersection. Assume that the triple
\langle s,\gamma,\vec{\dot{D}}\rangle
is the constructibly minimal such counterexample in
L[\bar{E}]
. Let
2\leq n<\omega
be such thay
\lambda,\mathbb{P}_{\lambda},\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda},s,\vec{D}
are
\Sigma_{n}
-definable over
J_{\lambda^{+3}}
without parameters, and the statement that
(s,\gamma,\vec{D})
is a counterexample for distributivity is
\Sigma_{n}
-expressible. Let
\delta\in(\lambda,\lambda^{+})
be such that
p^{N_{\eta}}_{n+1}=\{\delta\}
.

Let
\zeta
be a regular cardinal in the interval
(\gamma,\lambda)
. In the case where
\lambda
is singular, choose
\zeta
such that
\cf(\lambda)<\zeta
. Apply Proposition
3.7
to find
\eta\in\mathcal{S}^{\lambda,\zeta,n}
such that its collapsing structure
N^{\bar{E}}_{\eta}
can be
r\Sigma_{n}
-embedded into
J_{\lambda^{+3}}
, where
\lambda,\mathbb{P}_{\lambda},\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\lambda},s,\vec{D}
belong to the range of the embedding. Let
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\lambda},\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}_{\lambda},s,\vec{\dot{D}}^{\prime}
be the preimages of
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda},\bar{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda},s,\vec{\dot{D}}
under the above embedding.

Our goal is to construct in
N_{\tau}
a condition of
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\setminus\nu
which belongs to
\bigcap_{i<\gamma}\dot{D}^{\prime}_{i}
, using a similar argument as in Lemma
4.30
. This was previously done by constructing an increasing sequence of conditions
\langle p_{i}\colon i\leq\gamma\rangle
, where each
p_{i+1}
is obtained from
p_{i}
by adding points to the coding components of
p_{i}(\alpha)
, for every
\alpha
above a carefully chosen
\alpha^{*}_{i}
. However, for the current lemma, it will be important for us that the sequence
\langle\alpha^{*}_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle
is bounded in
\lambda
, and this cannot be easily guaranteed for singular
\lambda
.
27
27

27

We remark that, for the “Kunen-like” blueprint, the only case that matters to us is where
\lambda
is Mahlo. However, the inductive nature of the distributivity proof forces us to deal also with the cases where
\lambda
is singular.
To overcome this obstacle, we replace the sequence
\langle\alpha^{*}_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle
with a single ordinal, by applying Lemma
3.28
to find a sequence of very good points
\langle\epsilon_{i}\colon i\leq\gamma\rangle
in
N_{\eta}
and a single
\alpha^{*}\in(\nu,\lambda)
, such that–

•

For every nowhere stationary
Z\in h^{N^{\bar{E}}_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha^{*}\cup\{\delta\})]
of inaccessible cardinals and
\alpha\in Z\setminus\alpha^{*}
,
\alpha\in h^{N^{\bar{E}}_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
. In particular,
h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
transitively collapses to
N_{\eta(\alpha)}
for some
\eta(\alpha)\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}
.

By increasing
\alpha^{*}
if necessary, we can assume that
\alpha^{*}\in h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha^{*}\cup\{\delta\})]
.

•

For every
\alpha<\lambda
satisfying
\alpha\in h^{N_{\eta}}_{n+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
, there are sequence
\langle\epsilon_{i}(\alpha)\colon i\leq\gamma\rangle
and
\langle\eta_{i}(\alpha)\colon i\leq\gamma\rangle
, such that the former sequence consists of very good points of
N_{\eta(\alpha)}
, and the latter sequence consists of the club points induced by them. Moreover:

–

Each
\epsilon_{i}
is a limit of more than
\omega_{1}
-many very good points.

–

\eta_{i}(\alpha)\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
for every
i\leq\gamma
.

–

For every
i<\gamma
,
\langle\epsilon_{j}\colon j<i\rangle\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n+1}[\omega\times(\alpha^{*}\cup\{\delta\})]
.

Construct an increasing sequence
\langle p_{i}\colon i<\gamma\rangle\subseteq\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
, such that:

1.

For every
i<\gamma
,
\langle p_{j}\colon j\leq i\rangle\in h^{N^{\bar{E}}_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n+1}[\omega\times(\alpha^{*}\cup\{\delta\})]
.

2.

For every
i<\gamma
,
p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha^{*}=p_{0}\upharpoonright\alpha^{*}
.

3.

For every
i<\gamma
, there exists a dense open set
D^{*}_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{P}_{\left[\alpha_{0},\alpha^{*}\right)}
above
p_{0}\upharpoonright\left[\alpha_{0},\alpha^{*}\right)
, such that, for every
q\in D^{*}_{i}
,
q^{\frown}p_{i+1}\setminus\alpha^{*}\in\dot{D}^{\prime}_{i}
.

4.

For every
i<\gamma
,
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\alpha
forces that for every
\xi<\alpha^{++}
,

{p_{i+1}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\Vdash{\eta}_{i}(\alpha)\in\dot{c}^{p_{i+1}(\alpha)}_{\xi}.

5.

For every limit
i\leq\gamma
,
p_{i}=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}
, namely
p_{i}
is the condition such that
\supp(p_{i})=\bigcup_{j<i}\supp(p_{j})
, and, for every
\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})
,

p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha\Vdash p_{i}(\alpha)=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}(\alpha)

where
\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}(\alpha)
is the least upper bound of
\langle p_{j}(\alpha)\colon j<i\rangle
in the forcing
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
, as defined in Definition
4.12
.

For simplicity, let us denote for every
i\leq\gamma
,
X_{i}=h^{N^{\bar{E}}_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha^{*}\cup\{\delta\})]
.

We begin the construction by picking the constructibly least condition
p_{0}\in\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
such that
p_{0}\upharpoonright\nu=s
. Assuming that
p_{i}\in\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\setminus\alpha^{*}
has been constructed and belongs to
X_{i}
, let us construct
p_{i+1}\in X_{i+1}
. First note that
\supp(p_{i})\in X_{i}
is a nowhere stationary set of inaccessible cardinals, and thus, for every
\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\setminus\alpha^{*}
,
\alpha\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
. This, together with the fact that
\langle\epsilon_{i}\colon i\leq\gamma\rangle\in X_{i+1}
, shows that the sequence
\langle\eta_{i}(\alpha)\colon\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\setminus\alpha^{*}\rangle
could be entirely defined inside
X_{i+1}
.
28
28

28

This follows from the coherence of the square sequence.

Let
\vec{\mu}^{p_{i}}=\langle\mu^{p_{i}}_{\alpha}\colon\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\rangle
be the constructibly-least local bounding function of
p_{i}
. Then
\vec{\mu}^{p_{i}}\in X_{i}
. This, combined with the fact that for every
\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\setminus\alpha^{*}
,
\alpha\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
, implies that for every such
\alpha
,
\mu^{p_{i}}_{\alpha}\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]\cap\alpha^{+}
, namely
\mu^{p_{i}}_{\alpha}<\eta_{i}(\alpha)
. Thus, we can extend
p_{i}
to a condition
p^{\prime}_{i}
by adding the points
{\eta}_{i}(\alpha)
to the coding coordinates at each
\alpha\geq\alpha^{*}
. More formally, we define
p^{\prime}_{i}\geq p_{i}
with the same support, such that
p^{\prime}_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha^{*}=p\upharpoonright\alpha^{*}
, and, for every
\alpha\geq\alpha^{*}
,
p^{\prime}_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha
forces that for every
\xi<\alpha^{++}
,

p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}=\dot{T}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}

and

{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}T^{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}=\dot{c}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\cup\{\eta_{i}(\alpha)\}.

The name
\dot{c}^{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}
defined above is forced to be a legitimate condition of
\mathbb{C}^{\alpha}_{\xi}
since
\eta_{i}(\alpha)\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
. As mentioned above,
\langle\eta_{i}(\alpha)\colon\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\rangle
belongs to
X_{i+1}
, and thus
p^{\prime}_{i}\in X_{i+1}
.

Let
p_{i+1}\geq p^{\prime}_{i}
be an extension such that
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\alpha^{*}=p_{0}\upharpoonright\alpha^{*}
, and

p_{0}\upharpoonright\alpha^{*}\Vdash p_{i+1}\setminus\alpha^{*}\in\dot{D}^{\prime}_{i}\cap\left(\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\setminus\alpha^{*}\right).

Such
p_{i+1}
exists by Lemma
4.29
and since
\dot{D}^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\setminus\alpha^{*}
is dense open. We can also pick
p_{i+1}
inside
X_{i+1}
, being constructibly minimal with the above properties.

Finally, let
D^{*}_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{P}_{\left[\nu,\alpha^{*}\right)}
be the set of conditions
r
extending
p_{0}\upharpoonright\left[\nu,\alpha^{*}\right)
, such that
p_{0}\upharpoonright\nu\Vdash r^{\frown}p_{i+1}\in\dot{D}^{\prime}_{i}
. It’s not hard to see that
D^{*}_{i}
is a dense open subset of
\mathbb{P}_{\left[\nu,\alpha^{*}\right)}
. This concludes the successor step.

Assume now that
i\leq\gamma
is limit, and
\langle p_{j}\colon j<i\rangle
have been defined. Let
p_{i}=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}
, namely
p_{i}
is the condition such that
\supp(p_{i})=\bigcup_{j<i}\supp(p_{j})
, and, for every
\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})
,

p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha\Vdash p_{i}(\alpha)=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}(\alpha)

where
\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}(\alpha)
is the least upper bound of
\langle p_{j}(\alpha)\colon j<i\rangle
in the forcing
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
, as defined in Definition
4.12
.
In other words,

p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha\Vdash\forall\xi<\alpha^{++},p_{i}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}=\bigcap_{j<i,\alpha\in\supp(p_{j})}\dot{T}^{p_{j}(\alpha)}_{\xi}

where
\dot{T}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}
is a legitimate condition since
\mathbb{M}_{\alpha}
is
\alpha
-closed, and–

p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha\Vdash\forall\xi<\alpha^{++},p_{i}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}=\left(\bigcup_{j<i,\alpha\in\supp(p_{j})}\dot{c}^{p_{j}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\right)\cup\Big\{\bigcup_{j<i}\eta_{j}(\alpha)\Big\}

where
\dot{c}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}
is a legitimate condition since
\sup_{j<i}\eta_{j}(\alpha)=\eta_{i}(\alpha)\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
.

Finally, we need to verify
\langle p_{j}\colon j\leq i\rangle\in X_{i}
. It suffices to prove that
\langle p_{j}\colon j<i\rangle\in X_{i}
, since the above construction of
p_{i}
from
\langle p_{j}\colon j<i\rangle
can be done inside
X_{i}
. For the proof that
\langle p_{j}\colon j<i\rangle\in X_{i}
, we rely on the fact that
\langle\epsilon_{j}\colon j<i\rangle\in X_{i}
, and, in each step in the construction,
p_{j+1}
is chosen in a constructible way from
p_{j}
,
\eta_{j}(\alpha)
and
D_{j}
, which are all available inside
X_{i}
.

This concludes the inductive construction. Consider the condition
p_{\gamma}\in\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
obtained in the final limit step.
p_{\gamma}\upharpoonright\nu=s
forces that
\langle\dot{D}^{*}_{i}\colon i\leq\gamma\rangle
are dense open subsets of
\mathbb{P}_{\left[\nu,\alpha^{*}\right)}
above
p_{\gamma}\upharpoonright\left[\nu,\alpha^{*}\right)
. By applying the induction hypothesis for the forcing
\mathbb{P}_{\left[\nu,\alpha^{*}\right)}
, it is forced by
p_{\gamma}\upharpoonright\nu
that there exists
r\in\bigcap_{i<\gamma}D^{*}_{i}
which extends
p_{\gamma}\upharpoonright\left[\nu,\alpha^{*}\right)
. Now, extend
p_{\gamma}
in the interval
\left[\nu,\alpha^{*}\right)
to such
r
, and let
p^{*}
be the resulting condition. Then
p^{*}\upharpoonright\nu=s
, and forces that
p^{*}\setminus\nu\in\bigcap_{i<\gamma}\dot{D}^{\prime}_{i}
, contradicting our initial assumption.∎

Lemma 4.32
.

For every
\lambda\leq\kappa
Mahlo and regular
\delta<\lambda
, the forcing
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
preserves the stationarity of stationary subsets of
\mathcal{S}^{\lambda}\subseteq\lambda^{+}\cap\cf(\delta)
.

Proof.

Assume otherwise. Let
(p,S,\dot{C})
be the constructibly least counterexample, in the sense that
S\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{\lambda}
is a stationary set in
L[\bar{E}]
,
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
and
\dot{C}
is
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
-name for a club subset of
\lambda
, which is forced by
p
to be disjoint from
S
.

Pick
\eta\in S
. Let
\pi\colon N_{\eta}\to J^{\bar{E}}_{\lambda^{+3}}
with
\mbox{crit}(\pi)>\lambda
be
r\Sigma_{n+1}
-preserving, where
n=n_{\eta}\geq 2
. Then
\lambda,\mathbb{P}_{\lambda},\bar{P}_{\lambda},p,\dot{C}
belong to
\mbox{Im}(\pi)
. Let
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{\lambda},\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}_{\lambda},p^{\prime},\dot{C}^{\prime}
be the images of the relevant parameters under the transitive collapse. Note that
\dot{C}^{\prime}
is unbounded in
\eta
since
\eta=(\alpha^{+})^{N_{\eta}}
.

We construct a sequences
\langle p_{i}\colon i<\omega_{1}\rangle
of conditions of
\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
,
\langle\alpha^{*}_{i}\colon i<\omega_{1}\rangle
of ordinals below
\lambda
, and
\langle\epsilon_{i}\colon i<\omega_{1}\rangle
of very good points of
N_{\eta}
, such that:

1.

For every
i<j<\omega_{1}
,
p_{i}\leq p_{j}
and
p_{j}\upharpoonright(\alpha^{*}_{i}+1)=p_{i}\upharpoonright(\alpha^{*}_{i}+1)
.

2.

For every
i<\omega_{1}
and
\alpha\geq\alpha^{*}_{i}
in
\supp(p_{i})
,
\alpha\in h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
.

3.

For every
i<\omega_{1}
,
\langle p_{j}\colon j\leq i\rangle\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n+1}[\omega\times\left(\alpha^{*}_{i}\cup\{\delta\}\right)]
.

4.

For every
i<\lambda
and Mahlo
\alpha>\alpha_{i}
in
\supp(p_{i})
,

p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\alpha\Vdash\ \ \forall\xi<\alpha^{++},\ \ {p_{i+1}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p_{i+1}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\Vdash\eta_{i}(\alpha)\in\dot{c}^{p_{i+1}(\alpha)}_{\xi}

where
\eta_{i}(\alpha)\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
is the club-point associated with a very good point
\epsilon_{i}(\alpha)
, which is the image of
\epsilon_{i}
under the transitive collapse of
h^{N_{\eta}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]
to
N_{\eta(\alpha)}
.

5.

For every
i<\omega_{1}
,
p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\alpha^{*}_{i+1}
forces that
p_{i+1}\setminus\alpha^{*}_{i+1}
decides that
\eta_{i}
-th element of
\dot{C}^{\prime}
, where
\eta_{i}
is the club point that corresponds to the very good point
\epsilon_{i}
.

Let
p_{0}=p^{\prime}
. Let
\alpha_{0},\epsilon_{0}
be chosen using Corollary
3.27
such that
p_{0}\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{0}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times\left(\alpha_{0}\cup\{\delta\}\right)]
.

Assuming that
p_{i},\alpha_{i},\epsilon_{i}
were chosen, construct
p^{\prime}_{i}
with the same support, such that
p^{\prime}_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha^{*}_{i}=p\upharpoonright\alpha^{*}_{i}
, and, for every
\alpha\geq\alpha^{*}_{i}
,
p^{\prime}_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha
forces that for every
\xi<\alpha^{++}
,

p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi\Vdash\dot{T}^{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}=\dot{T}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}

and

{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}T^{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}=\dot{c}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\cup\{\eta_{i}(\alpha)\}.

The name
\dot{c}^{p^{\prime}_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}
defined above is forced to be a legitimate condition of
\mathbb{C}^{\alpha}_{\xi}
since
\eta_{i}(\alpha)\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
.
\eta_{i}(\alpha)
could be added above
\dot{c}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}
(for each
\xi<\alpha^{++}
) since the least local bounding function
\vec{\mu}^{p_{i}}
for
p_{i}
belongs to
h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n+1}[\omega\times(\alpha^{*}_{i}\cup\{\delta\})]
; in particular, for every
\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})\setminus\alpha^{*}_{i}
,
\vec{\mu}^{p_{i}}(\alpha)\in\alpha^{+}\cap h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n+1}[\omega\times(\alpha\cup\{\delta\})]=\eta_{i}(\alpha)
. Finally, let
p_{i+1}
be the constructibly least extension of
p^{\prime}_{i}
in
N_{\eta}
, that agrees with
p^{\prime}_{i}
up to
\alpha^{*}_{i+1}
, and such that
p_{i+1}\setminus\alpha_{i+1}
decides the
\eta_{i}
-th element of
\dot{C}^{\prime}
. Once
p_{i+1}
has been constructed, use Corollary
3.27
to find
\alpha_{i+1}>\alpha_{i}
and
\epsilon_{i+1}>\epsilon_{i}
such that
p_{i+1}\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i+1}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha_{i+1}\cup\{\delta\})]
. This concludes the successor step in the construction.

In limit steps, use the fact that
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
is
\omega_{1}
-closed to take an upper bound. More specifically, assume that
i\leq\omega_{1}
is limit, and
\langle p_{j}\colon j<i\rangle
have been defined. Let
p_{i}=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}
, namely
p_{i}
is the condition such that
\supp(p_{i})=\bigcup_{j<i}\supp(p_{j})
, and, for every
\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})
,

p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha\Vdash p_{i}(\alpha)=\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}(\alpha)

where
\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}(\alpha)
is the least upper bound of
\langle p_{j}(\alpha)\colon j<i\rangle
in the forcing
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
, as defined in Definition
4.12
. By Lemma
4.10
,
\bigvee_{j<i}p_{j}(\alpha)
exists. Once
p_{i}
has been constructed, choose
\alpha_{i}\geq\sup_{j<i}\alpha_{j}
and
\epsilon_{i}>\sup_{j<i}\epsilon_{j}
such that
\epsilon_{j}
is a very good point fulfilling the conditions of Corollary
3.27
, chosen high enough such that
p_{i}\in h^{N_{\eta},\epsilon_{i}}_{n_{\eta}+1}[\omega\times(\alpha_{i}\cup\{\delta\})]
.

This concludes the construction. Let
\alpha^{*}=\sup_{i<\omega_{1}}\alpha_{i}
. Let
p^{*}=\bigcup_{i<\omega_{1}}p_{i}
(
p^{*}
is external to
N_{\eta}
). We first argue that
p^{*}\in\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
. For every
\alpha<\alpha^{*}
,
p^{*}\upharpoonright\alpha=p_{i}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i}
, where
i<\omega_{1}
is the least such that
\alpha_{i}>\alpha
. Thus, it suffices to check that
p^{*}\setminus\alpha^{*}
is a legitimate condition. Note that, since
\eta\in\mathcal{S}^{\lambda}
, the very good points
\langle\epsilon_{i}\colon i<\omega_{1}\rangle
are cofinal in
N^{(n_{\eta})}_{\eta}\cap\mbox{Ord}
. In other words,
\eta(\alpha)=\sup_{i<\omega_{1}}\eta_{i}(\alpha)
, for every
\alpha\in\supp(p^{*})\setminus\alpha^{*}
. It follows that, for every
\alpha\geq\alpha^{*}
,
p^{*}\upharpoonright\alpha
forces that
p^{*}_{\alpha}
is a condition, such that for every
\xi<\alpha^{++}
in its support,

{p^{*}(\alpha)\upharpoonright\xi}^{\frown}\dot{T}^{p^{*}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\Vdash\dot{c}^{p^{*}(\alpha)}_{\xi}=\left(\bigcup_{i<\omega_{1},\alpha\in\supp(p_{i})}\dot{c}^{p_{i}(\alpha)}_{\xi}\right)\cup\Big\{\eta(\alpha)\Big\}

which is a legitimate condition since
\eta(\alpha)\notin\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}
by Lemma
3.20
.

This shows that
p^{*}\in\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
. By our construction, for every
i<\omega_{1}
,
p^{*}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i+1}=p_{i+1}\upharpoonright\alpha_{i+1}
forces that
p_{i+1}\setminus\alpha_{i+1}
decides the
\eta_{i}
-th element in
\dot{C}
. Let
\gamma_{i}
be the least ordinal below
\alpha^{+}
such that
p_{i+1}\Vdash\dot{C}(\check{\eta}_{i})<\check{\gamma_{i}}
. This is a a
\Pi_{2}
-statement that defines
\gamma_{i}
, with parameters in the image of
\pi
. Since
\pi
is weakly
r\Sigma_{n+1}
-preserving for some
n\geq 2
, and the parameters
\alpha,p_{i+1},\check{\eta_{i}}
belong to the image of
\pi
(since
\mbox{crit}(\pi)=\eta
), we have that
\gamma_{i}\in\mbox{Im}(\pi)
. But
\mbox{Im}(\pi)\cap\alpha^{+}=\eta
, so
\gamma_{i}<\eta
.

It follows that
p^{*}
forces that the
\eta_{i}
-th element of
\dot{C}
is less than
\eta
, for every
i<\omega_{1}
. Therefore,
p^{*}
forces that
\eta\in\dot{C}
, a contradiction.∎

We are ready to prove the main theorem of this work.

Proof.

(Theorem
1.1
)

Let
L[\bar{E}]
be a minimal extender model with a
(\kappa,\kappa^{++})
-extender
E
on a measurable cardinal
\kappa
. In particular, the sequence
\bar{E}
is a set and
E
is its last extender.
Let
\mathbb{P}=\mathbb{P}_{\kappa+1}
be the nonstationary support iteration described at the beginning of this section (
4.3
).
We need to verify that it satisfies the “Kunen-like” blueprint, from Definition
2.22
, which extends the Friedman-Magidor blueprint from Definition
2.12
.
It is clear from the definition of
\mathbb{P}
that it satisfies assumption (FM1). Theorem
4.1
shows it satisfies assumption (FM2). Lemmas
4.30
and
4.31
secure assumption
(FM3)
.
Regarding (FM4), Theorem
4.1
establishes the existence of a uniform definition
\varphi_{\mathbb{Q}}
for the witnesses for the self coding of
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\alpha}
. Choosing

\vec{f}^{\alpha}=\langle f^{\alpha}_{\tau}\mid\tau<\alpha^{++}\rangle\subseteq{}^{\alpha}\alpha
to be the
<_{Sing}^{\alpha}
-increasing sequence to be
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
-induced sequence of Miller generic branches, provides a definition
\varphi_{F}
, whose uniformity follows from the uniformity of
\varphi_{\mathbb{Q}}
. A uniform definition
\varphi_{\mathbb{P}}
for
\mathbb{P}
is an immediate consequene of the definition of
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha+1}
as a nonstationary support iteration of the posets
\mathbb{Q}_{\alpha}
, given by
\varphi_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

It remains to verify the “Kunen-Like” additional assumptions.
Theorem
4.1

proves that
\vec{f}^{\alpha}=\langle f^{\alpha}_{\tau}\mid\tau<\alpha^{++}\rangle\subseteq{}^{\alpha}\alpha
is
<^{\alpha}_{Sing}
-increasing, which validates assumption (KL1).
Assumption (KL2) for
\mathbb{P}
is an immediate consequence of
our choice of the stationary sets

\vec{S}^{\alpha}=\langle\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}(\tau,\eta,i):\tau<\alpha^{++},\eta<\alpha^{+},i<2\rangle\in L[\bar{E}]

from Definition
4.27
.
We conclude that
\mathbb{P}
satisfies the “Kunen-Like” blueprint.
Let
G\subseteq\mathbb{P}
be a generic filter over
V=L[\bar{E}]
. By Theorem
2.2

V[G]=L[\bar{E}][G]
is a “Kunen-Like” model where the single measurable cardinal
\kappa
satisfies
2^{\kappa}=\kappa^{++}
. Since
\bar{E},G
are sets, letting
U
denote the single normal ultrafilter on
\kappa
in
V[G]
, we can express
V[G]
as
L[A,U]
for a set of ordinals
A
.
∎
